# Literature digest for the active-inference design (auto-compiled from the sweep)

Compiled from a 13-agent WebSearch sweep (8 topics + a completeness critic + 4 gap topics). Evidence strength is the agent's judgement; every entry carries its source and URL. This digest informed docs/aif/DESIGN.md; it is not itself a claim of this repository, and we did not re-read most primary sources (academic hosts were unreachable from the authoring environment).

## lit:pc-scale

20 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Only direct PC-trained transformer language model in the literature: a BERT-style conditional LM trained with standard Gaussian PC reached test perplexity 590.08±12.60 vs BP 162.64±0.76; replacing the Gaussian output energy with a forward-KL (categorical) energy (PC_FKL) closed most of the gap at 175.90±1.74. Standard PC cannot handle softmax/attention layers; a non-Gaussian energy is required. | [Pinchetti, Salvatori et al., 'Predictive Coding beyond Gaussian Distributions', NeurIPS 20](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.03481) | 2022 | strong |
| tPC-RTRL (temporal PC + approximate real-time recurrent learning) closely matches BPTT on real sequence tasks: on En-Fr CCMatrix translation with a 15M-parameter RG-LRU recurrent model it reaches test perplexity 7.62 vs 7.49 for BPTT; byte-level WikiText-103 LM was also run. Crucially, plain tPC only tracks 'spatial BP' during training, i.e., it provides no temporal credit assignment on its own. | ['Learning Long-Range Dependencies with Temporal Predictive Coding' (Bogacz group), arXiv 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18131) | 2026 | moderate |
| μPC (Depth-μP parameterisation of PCNs) trains up to 128-layer fully-connected residual networks on MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/CIFAR-10 with accuracy comparable to BP and zero-shot transfer of both weight and activity learning rates across width/depth; all networks used as many inference steps as hidden layers. Analysis shows standard PC's inference landscape becomes ill-conditioned with depth, and that keeping conditioning constant is fundamentally at odds with forward-pass stability. | [Innocenti, Achour, Buckley, 'μPC: Scaling Predictive Coding to 100+ Layer Networks', NeurI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13124) | 2025 | strong |
| For linear residual networks, the set of width- and depth-stable feature-learning parameterisations of PC is exactly the same as for BP; under any of them the equilibrated PC energy converges to the BP loss when width >> depth, so PC computes the same gradients as BP. The regime where PC could be faster/different is unstable at large width and depth: 'to maintain stability at scale, PC must behave more like BP.' | ['On the Infinite Width and Depth Limits of Predictive Coding Networks', arXiv 2602.07697](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07697) | 2026 | strong |
| PC-ALM adds per-layer dual neurons (Lagrange multipliers), turning each layer's local recurrence into a PI feedback controller. Trains residual MLPs up to 1000 layers on MNIST within ~2 points of BP; matches BP across the full width-depth grid up to depth 128 in nonlinear nets, including deep-narrow regimes where PC fails. Example (Fashion-MNIST, width 32, depth 32, ReLU): BP 78.66%, PC 68.13%, PC-ALM 77.75%; gradient cosine to BP 0.604 (PC) vs 0.909 (PC-ALM). Inference budget T = 2L steps. In linear nets it converges to exact BP gradients via layer-local updates. Also improves over PC on Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10 and Tiny-ImageNet (ResNet-18). | [Seely (Sakana AI), 'Augmented Lagrangian Predictive Coding', arXiv 2605.31022 + github.com](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31022) | 2026 | moderate |
| In canonical state-based PC the learning signal decays exponentially with depth (multiplied by a factor <1 per layer), often hitting numerical precision limits before reaching early layers; PCNs degrade beyond ~5-7 layers. Fixes in 2025-26: (a) ePC reparameterises dynamics in error space, converging 100x faster in a 20-layer linear net and up to 10,000x in nonlinear nets, and is the first PC variant to consistently match BP test accuracy across VGG-5/7/9 and ResNet-18 on MNIST/FMNIST/CIFAR-10/100 — but does so by making the computational graph globally connected (loses locality); (b) Highway Error Propagation adds feedback matrices coupling hidden states directly to the clamped output error, giving depth-independent corrections and training 128-layer MLPs on MNIST/FMNIST; (c) DKP-PC adds learnable direct output-to-hidden feedback that converges toward the pseudoinverse of forward weights, reducing error propagation from O(L) to O(1). | [ePC arXiv 2505.20137; Error Highways arXiv 2606.22744; DKP-PC arXiv 2602.15571; 'Towards t](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20137) | 2025-2026 | moderate |
| PCX (JAX) benchmark: even with a 20x faster implementation, PC costs 5.5 s/epoch vs 3.2 s for BP on CIFAR-100 VGG-5 (A100), and 29 s vs 10 s for AlexNet (161M params). PC matched BP only up to small conv models on CIFAR-10; VGG-19 performance drops without skip connections. Identified enduring bottlenecks: inference cost, top-layer error concentration, optimisation sensitivity. PC becomes about as fast as BP on GPUs only when inference steps ≈ depth. | [Pinchetti et al., 'Benchmarking Predictive Coding Networks — Made Simple', ICLR 2025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01163) | 2024-2025 | strong |
| Variants of PC that reproduce BP exactly (Z-IL, fixed-prediction assumption) require contrived scheduling (activations initialised to the feedforward pass, each layer's weights updated exactly at its first non-zero inference step); their time complexity is lower-bounded by BP's, so they offer no computational advantage, and they sacrifice the variational-Bayes interpretation. Conclusion: 'in its current forms, PC may have more limited potential as a direct replacement of backpropagation than previously envisioned.' | [Zahid, Guo, Fountas, 'Predictive Coding as a Neuromorphic Alternative to Backpropagation: ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.02658) | 2023 | strong |
| Incremental PC (iPC) performs inference and weight updates simultaneously at every step, is fully automatic (no phase switching), converges (incremental EM guarantee), reaches better minima and is far more efficient than original PC. Caveat from the ACM Computing Surveys tutorial: it is unknown whether the desirable properties of inference learning (e.g., implicit-SGD/trust-region behaviour) transfer to incremental IL. | [Salvatori et al., 'A Stable, Fast, and Fully Automatic Learning Algorithm for PCNs' (iPC),](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.00720) | 2022-2025 | strong |
| Theory of what PC actually optimises: with batch size 1 and specific energy weightings, inference learning equals implicit SGD; more generally PC acts as an adaptive second-order trust-region method that shifts the BP gradient toward a direction found by inference dynamics. The equilibrated PC energy of deep linear nets is a rescaled MSE whose origin is a strict saddle independent of depth (unlike BP's degenerate saddle), explaining faster escape/convergence in small nets. | [Alonso, Krichmar, Neftci AAAI 2024 (arXiv 2305.13562); Innocenti et al. 'PC as adaptive tr](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11979) | 2023-2024 | strong |
| Prospective configuration (infer activity first, then change weights) outperforms BP in online learning with batch size 1, reduces catastrophic interference in continual learning, and matches neural/behavioural data; no large-scale (ImageNet/LM) advantage reported. | [Song, Millidge, Salvatori, Lukasiewicz, Xu, Bogacz, Nature Neuroscience 27:348-358](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-023-01514-1) | 2024 | strong |
| Hybrid PC: a feedforward (amortised) pass provides cheap initial beliefs and iterative PC dynamics refine them under a single objective; the network adaptively spends more iterations when uncertain. Related 2025-26 engineering: warm-starting latents from the previous frame (PCN-TA) cut inference steps by 50% and weight updates by 10% vs BP on COIL-20; better activity initialisation that preserves progress across samples notably reduces training time. | [Tschantz, Millidge, Seth, Buckley, PLOS Comp Biol 19(8) 2023; PCN-TA arXiv 2510.25993 (IRO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2204.02169) | 2023-2026 | moderate |
| Precision is the missing ingredient: current PCNs fix the precision matrix to the identity, which discards precision-weighted errors and causes exponentially imbalanced cross-layer energy; expressing PCNs as deep hierarchical Gaussian filters restores precision-weighted message passing, learns activations/weights/precisions in closed form without iterations, approaches BP wall-clock on Fashion-MNIST and beats it on online, data-efficiency and concept-drift tasks. 'Deeper PCNs' likewise used precision-weighted latent optimisation to reach 15 hidden layers on Tiny-ImageNet near BP. | ['Closed-form predictive coding via hierarchical Gaussian filters', arXiv 2605.20293; 'Towa](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20293) | 2025-2026 | weak |
| PC on arbitrary graph topologies (PC graphs) runs inference/learning on any directed graph, performs different tasks by clamping different nodes, and topology measurably affects performance; PCNs also act as associative memories that retrieve by 'predicting' missing parts; temporal PC is an asymmetric Hopfield network with implicit whitening (more stable on natural/structured sequences) and approximates a Kalman filter that does not track its own posterior variance, generalising to nonlinear systems. | [Salvatori et al. NeurIPS 2022 (arXiv 2201.13180); Salvatori et al. NeurIPS 2021 (arXiv 210](https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.13180) | 2021-2024 | moderate |
| First PCN at ImageNet scale (June 2026): a 10-layer conv PCN (VGG10) trained with centered Equilibrium Propagation plus a new equilibration scheme reached 13.23% top-5 error vs 12.2% for BP on full ImageNet. | [Kerjan et al., 'Training a Predictive Coding Network on ImageNet using Equilibrium Propaga](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03584) | 2026 | moderate |
| Layer-local learning audit (Forward-Forward): under identical backbone/recipe, BP-DeepSup beats the best FF variant by 2.40/5.93 pp on CIFAR-10/100; at 224x224 FF reaches only 49.4% vs typical BP >75%; and FF's O(1)-in-depth activation memory yields no measured advantage over BP+gradient accumulation on commodity hardware. | ['Synthetic Benchmarks Overstate Forward-Forward Scaling: Real-Data Limits of Layer-Local T](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06539) | 2026 | moderate |
| Neuroscience support and pushback: brain responses to speech are best explained by hierarchical predictions spanning multiple timescales (~8 words ahead), and adding multi-timescale predictions to LMs improves brain mapping; however, a 2024 mouse V1 study finds 'prediction errors' look like stimulus-specific amplification/adaptation rather than subtractive error, and a 2024 re-analysis argues pre-onset encoding does not reflect pre-activation. | [Caucheteux, Gramfort, King, Nature Human Behaviour 2023; Lowet & Uchida, Current Biology 2](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-022-01516-2) | 2023-2024 | moderate |
| Friston-lineage active inference at scale: AXIOM (Verses) uses object-centric mixture-model priors + structure learning + expected-free-energy planning; on Gameworld 10k it reports normalized score 77 vs 48 (DreamerV3), 3,175 vs 24,207 steps, 0.95M vs 420M params, ~10 vs ~370 GPU-minutes. Renormalising Generative Models (RGMs) demonstrate scale-free discrete active inference on images, movies, music and Atari-like games. Neither has any language-modelling result; 'active inference in neural language models' papers (CEUR 2024) and 'EFE planning as variational inference' (2025) are conceptual, with no LM experiments. | [Verses AXIOM press/whitepaper (June 2025); Friston et al. 'From pixels to planning' arXiv ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20292) | 2024-2026 | weak |
| Most PC implementations do not enforce Dale's law and rely on mixed-sign error signals; recent Dale-constrained work exists only for BP-trained RNNs (projection step, Science Advances 2025) and a V1 layer-4 spiking PC model (bioRxiv 2025). Divide-and-Conquer PC (NeurIPS 2024) shows PC can be made to respect structured correlation and do provable ML updates via Monte Carlo, at small scale. | ['Constructing biologically constrained RNNs via Dale's backpropagation', Science Advances ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05834) | 2024-2025 | moderate |
| A June 2025 tutorial preprint claims a 3.58M-parameter PCN reaches 99.92% CIFAR-10 top-1 after four epochs (above ViT-H/14's 99.5%); independent review flags probable test-label leakage and advises the benchmark not be trusted. | ['Introduction to Predictive Coding Networks for Machine Learning', arXiv 2506.06332 (and P](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06332) | 2025 | speculative |

**Usable ideas**

- Output-layer energy must be categorical/forward-KL (Pinchetti 2022), not Gaussian: standard PC on a transformer LM gives ppl 590 vs 163 BP; FKL gives 176. Any token-level PC head in the design should use this.
- Use PC primarily as a test-time/online inference-and-adaptation mechanism (hybrid PC: amortised forward pass + a few iterative refinement steps, iterations scaled by uncertainty), with bulk pretraining done by BP/BPTT. Theory (implicit SGD / trust region; prospective configuration) says PC's advantage lives in batch-size-1, online and continual settings, which maps onto in-context learning and continual adaptation for an LM.
- Carry latent states across tokens (temporal amortisation, PCN-TA) and initialise activities from the previous step: halves inference steps; sequence data are exactly where this pays.
- Adopt μP-style (Depth-μP) parameterisation and residual/skip structure for any deep PC stack; budget inference steps ≈ depth (PCX) or 2×depth (PC-ALM).
- Add anatomically-motivated 'error highways': direct feedback matrices from the output-error population to selected hidden populations (HEP, DKP-PC) — depth-independent corrections; in a connectome model these can be instantiated from real long-range feedback projections.
- Introduce dual (Lagrange-multiplier) neurons per layer/population (PC-ALM) — interpretable as an inhibitory interneuron population implementing a PI controller on prediction-error constraints; gradient cosine to BP rises from 0.60 to 0.91 and deep-narrow regimes become trainable. Natural fit for the E/I core with Lyapunov control.
- Learn precisions (HGF closed-form PC; precision-weighted latent optimisation): prevents exponential cross-layer error imbalance, yields uncertainty estimates, and doubles as the 'attention = precision weighting' mechanism of the Friston lineage.
- Use PC graphs (Salvatori 2022) to place the connectome adjacency directly into the free-energy functional; implement the mushroom-body memory as a PC associative memory / tPC asymmetric-Hopfield-with-whitening module (Salvatori 2021; Tang 2023).
- For temporal credit assignment in a recurrent connectome core, pair tPC with an approximate RTRL (tPC-RTRL); connectome sparsity and block structure make RTRL Jacobian approximations cheaper than in dense RNNs.
- Add multi-horizon (2-8 tokens ahead) auxiliary prediction targets, motivated by Caucheteux et al. 2023, as a low-risk architectural prior that does not depend on the PC learning rule.
- Use iPC (simultaneous inference+learning, no phase switching) for the always-on continual-learning pathway; retain BP-compatible checkpoints so results can be compared against a BP-trained twin.
- Benchmark protocol: always report (i) gradient cosine to BP, (ii) inference steps per update, (iii) wall-clock vs BP twin, (iv) per-layer energy distribution (top-layer error concentration), following PCX/PC-ALM diagnostics.

**Dead ends**

- Standard Gaussian PC applied to attention/softmax layers: 3.6x worse perplexity than BP on a BERT-style LM (Pinchetti 2022).
- Vanilla state-based PC beyond ~5-7 layers: exponential signal decay toward early layers; VGG-19 collapses without skips (PCX; ePC; Deeper-PCN). Anything deep needs μPC/ALM/highways/precision.
- Expecting a different or better inductive bias from PC at scale: for stable width/depth scaling, PC's parameterisations coincide with BP's and its equilibrated gradients equal BP's (arXiv 2602.07697). The 'PC differs' regime is unstable at scale.
- Exact-BP PC variants (Z-IL, fixed-prediction assumption): require contrived update scheduling, are lower-bounded in cost by BP, and lose the Bayesian interpretation (Zahid et al. 2023). Not a route to anything BP cannot do.
- Wall-clock/compute claims: even optimised PCX is 1.7-3x slower than BP per epoch on GPUs; μPC needs ~depth inference steps; FF audit (2026) shows O(1)-memory local learning yields no measured hardware advantage over BP+grad accumulation.
- ePC-style fixes that make the error graph globally connected recover BP-level accuracy but discard locality/biological plausibility — a digital-simulation trick, not a brain-like design.
- Plain tPC for sequence learning: gives only spatial credit assignment, cannot learn long-range dependencies without RTRL-style augmentation.
- 'SOTA' PC claims (99.92% CIFAR-10, 3.58M params, 4 epochs) flagged for test-label leakage; do not build on them.
- Treating subtractive error neurons as an anatomical fact: mouse V1 data (Lowet & Uchida 2024) fit stimulus-specific amplification/adaptation better than subtraction; pre-onset 'prediction' encoding results are contested.
- Active-inference LLM papers to date are conceptual reframings of existing LLMs (CEUR 2024) — no trained model, no metric; AXIOM's wins rely on hand-specified object-centric priors that have no language equivalent yet.
- Prospective configuration's advantages vanish outside batch-size-1/online/small-data conditions; no evidence at LM scale.
- Incremental PC has never been evaluated on transformers or sequence models; whether IL's implicit-SGD/trust-region properties survive in iPC is explicitly unknown (ACM CSUR tutorial).

**Open gaps**

- No PC- or iPC-trained decoder-only autoregressive LM at any scale exists; the only PC transformer is a 2022 BERT-style conditional LM and the only real-data sequence result is a 15M-param RG-LRU RNN (tPC-RTRL).
- No scaling-law study (loss vs params/tokens/compute) for any PC variant; largest PC/EP result is a 10-layer VGG on ImageNet (13.23% vs 12.2% top-5 error).
- Joint spatial + temporal credit assignment at depth: deep-PC methods (μPC, PC-ALM, HEP) are feedforward-only; recurrent PC (tPC-RTRL) is shallow. Nobody has combined them.
- Precision learning has only been shown at Fashion-MNIST/Tiny-ImageNet scale; its interaction with attention-like precision weighting in a language model is untested.
- Dale's-law/E-I-constrained PC with signed error pathways and connectome-derived sparsity: no published results; unknown whether structural priors improve or worsen the conditioning of PC inference.
- Whether PC's online/continual-learning advantage (batch size 1) translates into better in-context or test-time adaptation for language is untested.
- Expected-free-energy-based decoding/sampling for LMs (epistemic vs pragmatic value in token selection) has no empirical study.
- GPU cost accounting for PC in sequence settings (inference steps × tokens × depth) is absent; PCN-TA-style warm starts are only shown on a small robotics image stream.
- Exact WikiText-103 byte-level numbers for tPC-RTRL and per-dataset numbers for PC-ALM/HEP beyond MNIST-class tasks could not be retrieved in this sweep (academic hosts blocked); verify before citing.
- No independent replication of the 2025 Frontiers energy-based-local-learning numbers (CIFAR-100 83.96%, Tiny-ImageNet 73.35%) which would be unusually strong for a local method.

**Key numbers**

- PC transformer LM (BERT-style, 2022): test ppl BP 162.64±0.76; PC_FKL 175.90±1.74; standard PC 590.08±12.60
- tPC-RTRL (2026): 15M-param RG-LRU translation ppl 7.62 vs BPTT 7.49; byte-level WikiText-103 also run (numbers not retrieved)
- μPC (NeurIPS 2025): stable training up to 128 layers (residual MLPs, MNIST/FMNIST/CIFAR-10), inference steps = #hidden layers, zero-shot LR transfer
- PC-ALM (2026): residual MLPs to 1000 layers on MNIST within ~2 pts of BP; FMNIST w32/d32: BP 78.66%, PC 68.13%, PC-ALM 77.75%; grad-cosine to BP 0.604 → 0.909; T = 2L inference steps
- Error Highways (2026): 128-layer MLPs on MNIST/FMNIST with depth-robust accuracy; DKP-PC error propagation O(L) → O(1)
- ePC (2025): 100x faster convergence in 20-layer linear net, up to 10,000x nonlinear; matches BP on VGG-5/7/9 and ResNet-18 (MNIST/FMNIST/CIFAR-10/100)
- PCX (ICLR 2025): CIFAR-100 VGG-5 5.5 s/epoch PC vs 3.2 s BP (110 s legacy PC); AlexNet 161M params 29 s vs 10 s; PC ≈ BP speed only when inference steps ≈ depth
- Vanilla PC degrades beyond 5-7 layers; 'Deeper PCNs' reaches 15 hidden layers on Tiny-ImageNet near BP
- ImageNet PCN via EP (2026): VGG10, top-5 error 13.23% vs 12.2% BP
- Forward-Forward audit (2026): 224x224 FF 49.4% vs BP >75%; BP-DeepSup beats DTG-FF by 2.40/5.93 pp on CIFAR-10/100
- Frontiers 2025 energy-based local learning (unverified): MNIST 99.22%, CIFAR-10 93.78%, CIFAR-100 83.96%, Tiny-ImageNet 73.35%
- PCN-TA (2025): 50% fewer inference steps than PC, 10% fewer weight updates than BP (COIL-20)
- AXIOM (2025, press): Gameworld 10k score 77 vs 48 DreamerV3; 3,175 vs 24,207 steps; 0.95M vs 420M params; ~10 vs ~370 GPU-min
- Brain: hierarchical predictions up to ~8 words ahead improve LM-to-brain mapping (Caucheteux 2023)
- Suspicious claim: PCN 99.92% CIFAR-10 top-1, 3.58M params, 4 epochs (likely label leakage)

## lit:aif-lm

41 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| As of Sept 2026 there is no active-inference/FEP-native language model trained end-to-end on a real text corpus with a variational-free-energy + expected-free-energy objective at any scale competitive with transformers. Every 'active inference LLM' result found either (a) wraps a frozen LLM with a small discrete pymdp-style AIF controller, (b) uses 'active inference' as a prompting metaphor, or (c) is a theoretical reinterpretation of transformers. | [Synthesis across Kulveit et al. 2023; Wen 2025 'The Missing Reward'; Multi-LLM AIF 2024; O](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05619) | 2023-2026 | moderate |
| Predictive coding trained on real text (BERT-style masked LM, One Billion Word, 200k train/10k dev sentences, 10 seeds): incremental PC (iPC) reached perplexity 106.19±10.54 (10/10 seeds converged) vs backprop 120.02±13.19 (7/10) vs standard PC 523.08±12.3 (3/10). Conditional (causal) LM: iPC 142.54±4.23 (7/10) vs BP 113.32±0.36 (10/10) vs PC 206.34±6.46 (10/10). Standard PC is far worse than BP; iPC is competitive only on the masked objective and loses on the autoregressive one. | [Salvatori et al., 'A Stable, Fast, and Fully Automatic Learning Algorithm for Predictive C](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.00720) | 2024 | strong |
| Predictive coding networks suffer significant performance degradation beyond 5-7 layers; causes identified: exponentially imbalanced errors across layers during weight updates, ineffective guidance of deep layers by earlier predictions, and (with skip connections) residual-path energy reaching upper layers faster than the main pathway. Fixes proposed: precision-weighted optimization of latents during relaxation and a modified weight update. | ['Towards the Training of Deeper Predictive Coding Neural Networks', arXiv 2506.23800 (v3 O](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23800) | 2025 | strong |
| Depth fixes for PC exist but are only validated on toy vision data: 'Error Highways' (highway error propagation coupling hidden states directly to the clamped output error) trains MLPs up to 128 layers with depth-robust accuracy on MNIST/Fashion-MNIST; muPC claims 100+ layer PCNs via muP-style parameterization. | [Mohammadi & Ororbia, 'Error Highways', arXiv 2606.22744; 'muPC', arXiv 2505.13124](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22744) | 2025-2026 | moderate |
| PCX (ICLR 2025) is the standard JAX library/benchmark for PCNs; it trained larger PCNs than prior literature and 'clearly highlights the current limitations of PCNs'. The maintainer's README lists open questions: Xavier-style init may not give good gradients for PC, and 'waiting for the network to converge during inference is actually wrong with the current formulation' may explain the need for training tricks. | [Pinchetti et al., 'Benchmarking Predictive Coding Networks -- Made Simple' (ICLR 2025); gi](https://github.com/liukidar/pcx) | 2025 | moderate |
| Energy-Based Transformers (ICLR 2026 oral): predictions made by gradient-descent energy minimization; on text and vision EBTs scale up to 35% faster than Transformer++ w.r.t. data, batch size, parameters, FLOPs and depth; inference-time 'System 2' iteration improves language performance 29% more than Transformer++; better downstream generalization at equal or worse pretraining loss. Scale is small (sub-1B research models). | [Gladstone et al., 'Energy-Based Transformers are Scalable Learners and Thinkers', arXiv 25](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02092) | 2025-2026 | moderate |
| Theoretical mapping: standard softmax attention is a special case of minimizing a Helmholtz free energy when the energy is an elastic potential; residual connections make the optimization incremental; unnormalized linear, gated linear and softmax attention are all recipes in one energy framework. | [Ren & Sheng, 'Transformers as Intrinsic Optimizers: Forward Inference through the Energy P](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00907) | 2025 | moderate |
| In controlled 'Bayesian wind tunnels' (bijection elimination, HMM state tracking) where the true posterior is known in closed form, small transformers reproduce Bayesian posteriors to 1e-3 to 1e-4 bits, while capacity-matched MLPs fail by orders of magnitude; residual streams act as the belief substrate and attention as content-addressable routing. | [Agarwal, Dalal, Misra, 'The Bayesian Geometry of Transformer Attention', arXiv 2512.22471](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22471) | 2025 | strong |
| In-context learning is well modeled as implicit Bayesian inference (Xie et al. 2022, HMM-mixture pretraining theory); Bayesian scaling laws for ICL (Arora et al., ICLR 2025) fit GPT-2-scale models with interpretable task-prior and learning-efficiency terms and predict re-emergence of suppressed behaviours under ICL. | [Xie et al. arXiv 2111.02080; Arora et al. arXiv 2410.16531](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16531) | 2022-2025 | strong |
| Transformers violate the martingale property of Bayesian updating on exchangeable data: positional encodings induce violations of order Theta(log n / n); yet they achieve excess risk O(n^-1/2) in expectation over orderings, and the implicit posterior converges to the true posterior in sufficient-statistic space; optimal chain-of-thought length derived as k* = Theta(sqrt(n) log(1/eps)). | [Chlon & Rashidi, 'LLMs are Bayesian, in Expectation, not in Realization', arXiv 2507.11768](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11768) | 2025 | moderate |
| Game-theoretic free-energy analysis of attention heads: coalition value defined as negative variational free energy; empirically positive pairwise Harsanyi dividends and negative triple dividends (higher-order redundancy); up to 20% of heads removable with acceptable perplexity increase. | [arXiv 2605.09515](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09515) | 2026 | moderate |
| 'Predictive Minds': LLMs fit the active-inference paradigm except that they lack a tight feedback loop between acting and perceiving the consequences; the authors argue the loop is closing (RLHF, tool use, self-generated training data) with predicted consequences of self-awareness and world-changing prediction-error minimization. | [Kulveit, von Stengel, Leventov, NeurIPS 2023 workshop, arXiv 2311.10215](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.10215) | 2023 | weak |
| Pezzulo, Parr, Cisek, Clark & Friston argue generative-AI models are 'passive': their generative models are not anchored to sensorimotor control and cannot test predictions by intervention, so they lack grounded meaning; no empirical test proposed. | ['Generating meaning: active inference and the scope and limits of passive AI', Trends in C](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S1364661323002607) | 2024 | weak |
| 'The Missing Reward' (Wen 2025) proposes LLM-as-amortized-inference + AIF-as-decision-rule hybrids, arguing transformers implicitly approximate Bayesian inference over latents and AIF can replace reward engineering with free-energy minimization; explicitly warns LLM text distributions need not match consequences of actions. No implementation. | [Bo Wen, arXiv 2508.05619](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05619) | 2025 | speculative |
| Active Inference for Self-Organizing Multi-LLM Systems: a pymdp-style discrete AIF agent with 3 state factors (prompt, search, information) and 7 observation modalities selects prompts/search strategies for LLMs; evidence is qualitative (emergent structure in observation matrices), no quantitative baseline comparison reported. | [arXiv 2412.10425](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10425) | 2024 | weak |
| Orchestrator (2025) applies AIF-style monitoring/benchmarking to LLM multi-agent coordination on maze puzzles of increasing complexity; claims improved reliability and efficiency with lightweight models; toy domain, no standard benchmark. | [arXiv 2509.05651](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.05651) | 2025 | weak |
| npj Digital Medicine 2025 'active inference' prompting: Therapist agent + Supervisor agent iterating on responses restricted to a validated CBT-I dataset; blind evaluation on 100 patient queries by experienced therapists; LLM responses rated as high as or higher than therapist-written appropriate responses. The 'active inference' content is an actor-critic loop metaphor; no free energy is computed. | [npj Digital Medicine 8:119 (2025), arXiv 2407.21051](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41746-025-01516-2) | 2025 | moderate |
| Active Inference as Context Acquisition (Dutta, Ramachandran, Sra 2026): agent's decision to ask/retrieve/call tools vs proceed is cast as an inner variational Bayes update over latent task state and an outer EFE-minimizing choice among context, task and stop actions under cost; in deterministic settings the epistemic term reduces to expected information gain, optionally normalized by token cost. | [arXiv 2608.19202](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19202) | 2026 | moderate |
| 'The Information Game' (ICLR 2026 submission, withdrawn): AIF as bilevel optimization (inner VB belief update, outer EFE query selection); introduces Optimal Question Asking benchmark; GPT-4o and Haiku 3.5 show a 1-2 query planning gap vs game-theoretic optimum on 25-object tasks. | [OpenReview 1qLZsyJN2t](https://openreview.net/forum?id=1qLZsyJN2t) | 2025-2026 | weak |
| Information-gain reward for clarification (ICML 2026): training a clarifier on Bayesian belief-update-towards-goal reward improves task success by 3.7% over no-clarification while adding only 0.3 interaction steps on average, across five backbones on a clarification-enhanced tau-Bench. | ['Uncertainty-Aware Clarification in LLM Agents with Information Gain', arXiv 2606.03135](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03135) | 2026 | strong |
| FREIA (ACL 2026): unsupervised RL for LLM reasoning using a Free-Energy-Driven Reward (balancing consensus and exploration per the FEP) plus Adaptive Advantage Shaping; reported to outperform other unsupervised methods on nine datasets across three reasoning tasks. | [Huang et al., arXiv 2605.04065 / ACL 2026 long paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.04065) | 2026 | moderate |
| KL-regularized RL (the RLHF core) is exactly variational inference: the pretrained LM is the prior, the reward is the log-likelihood, and the KL-penalized objective minimizes KL to the Bayesian posterior (control-as-inference, Todorov 2006; Rawlik 2012; Levine 2018; Korbak et al. 2022). | ['RL with KL penalties is better seen as Bayesian inference' (Korbak et al.)](https://www.alignmentforum.org/posts/eoHbneGvqDu25Hasc/rl-with-kl-penalties-is-better-seen-as-bayesian-inference) | 2022 | strong |
| EFE-based planning can be derived as variational free energy minimization on a generative model augmented with preference and epistemic priors; VFE of the augmented model = VFE of predictive model + explicit entropy-correction terms + a planning correction turning marginal inference into policy optimization; a message-passing realization outperformed KL-control agents on a stochastic gridworld and partially observable MiniGrid DoorKey-8x8. | [de Vries et al. arXiv 2504.14898; Nuijten et al. 'What Type of Inference is Active Inferen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14898) | 2025-2026 | strong |
| Autoregressive active-inference agent realized purely as message passing on a factor graph with EFE distributed over a planning graph; validated on a continuous robot-navigation task where it modulates action by predictive uncertainty, arriving later than an optimal controller but with a better dynamics model. | [Kouw, Nisslbeck, Nuijten, arXiv 2509.25482 (IWAI 2025)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.25482) | 2025 | weak |
| Renormalising Generative Models (Friston et al. 2024): discrete hierarchical HMMs with paths as latents built by renormalisation-group coarse-graining over space and time; demonstrated on image classification, movie and music compression/generation, and Atari-like games; a 2026 'Foundations, Derivations, and Verification' paper provides an open verified implementation. No application to text found. | ['From pixels to planning: scale-free active inference' arXiv 2407.20292; Zaghw et al. arXi](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20292) | 2024-2026 | moderate |
| AXIOM (Verses, 2025): gradient-free object-centric active inference growing/pruning four mixture models (sMM, iMM, tMM, rMM) with Bayesian model reduction and EFE planning; on the self-designed Gameworld 10k benchmark (ten simple arcade games) it reports normalized score 77 vs 48 for BBF/DreamerV3, 7.6x sample efficiency (3,175 vs 24,207 steps) and 39x faster GPU runtime. Not evaluated on standard Atari 100k. | [arXiv 2505.24784; Verses whitepaper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24784) | 2025 | moderate |
| Verses press releases: Genius solved Mastermind 100% vs OpenAI o1-preview 71%, 140x faster and 5260x cheaper (Dec 2024); vs DeepSeek R1 245x faster and 779x cheaper (Feb 2025). Single narrow code-breaking game; no peer review. | [Verses AI news release](https://www.verses.ai/news/verses-genius-outperforms-openai-model-in-code-breaking-challenge-mastermind) | 2024-2025 | weak |
| Verses' position paper defines HMMs and switching linear dynamical systems as 'natural building blocks' for structured world models and states that the core unsolved challenge for this technology is scalable joint structure-parameter learning. | ['Natural Building Blocks for Structured World Models', arXiv 2511.02091](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.02091) | 2025 | weak |
| Friston et al. 'Active inference and artificial reasoning' (Dec 2025) adds a third information-gain term (about model structure) to EFE, with Bayesian model reduction used for fast structure learning/selection. | [arXiv 2512.21129](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.21129) | 2025 | weak |
| Deep active inference scaling limits (survey evidence): vanilla EFE needs explicit enumeration of every policy (exponential in horizon and state size); the elegant EFE machinery applies only to discrete state/action spaces; scaled implementations deviate from vanilla AIF via bootstrapped or reduced EFE; Contrastive Active Inference matched but did not exceed reward-based RL on image control; DAIMC (2020) beat DQN/A2C and matched PPO2 only on Animal-AI-scale tasks; Dreamer/TD-MPC families remain SOTA on continuous control. | [Mazzaglia et al., 'The FEP for Perception and Action: A Deep Learning Perspective' (Entrop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2207.06415) | 2020-2024 | strong |
| iP-VAE / FOND (NeurIPS 2025): deriving inference dynamics from natural gradients on variational free energy with online, iterative updates yields a recurrent spiking network with Poisson (integer spike-count) latents that beats standard VAEs and Gaussian predictive coding on sparsity/reconstruction with 25x fewer parameters and stronger OOD generalization than hybrid iterative-amortized VAEs; images only (up to CelebA). | [Vafaii, Galor, Yates, 'Brain-like Variational Inference', arXiv 2410.19315; github.com/had](https://github.com/hadivafaii/IterativeVAE) | 2025 | moderate |
| Temporal predictive coding (Millidge, Tang, Osanlouy, Harper, Bogacz 2024): a PC network with local Hebbian rules approximates Kalman filtering for linear-Gaussian dynamics and extends to nonlinear sequence prediction; small-scale simulations only. | [PLOS Computational Biology 20(4):e1011183](https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371/journal.pcbi.1011183) | 2024 | moderate |
| Friston's language models under active inference ('Deep temporal models', 2017; 'Active listening', 2021) are hand-crafted discrete hierarchical generative models (letters/words/sentences, nested timescales, speech segmentation as covert action) with tiny lexicons; they reproduce reading eye-movement and ERP-like phenomena but are not learned from corpora and have no perplexity-type evaluation. | [Friston et al. Neurosci Biobehav Rev 2018; Hearing Research 2021](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC7812378/) | 2017-2021 | moderate |
| LLM-JEPA (LeCun group, 2025): an embedding-space predictive objective added to standard LLM training improves finetuning and pretraining results across models and reduces overfitting; exact margins vary by dataset. | [Huang, LeCun, Balestriero, arXiv 2509.14252](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14252) | 2025 | moderate |
| Latent-variable LMs remain small-scale: a latent-autoregressive GP-VAE LM (Dec 2025) beats only a 'minimal' transformer baseline on WikiText-2 in a short-context regime; historically VAE text models collapse (decoder ignores z), a problem documented from 2016 with only partial fixes (KL annealing, free bits, DVAE, TWR-VAE). | [arXiv 2512.09535; 2512.24102; posterior-collapse literature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09535) | 2016-2025 | strong |
| TWISTER (ICLR 2025): a transformer world model trained with a contrastive predictive coding objective reaches 162% human-normalized mean / 77% median on Atari 100k vs DreamerV3 112%/49% and STORM 127%/58%, without look-ahead search. | [Burchi & Timofte, arXiv 2503.04416](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04416) | 2025 | strong |
| Bayesian Model Reduction for structured pruning (BMRS, NeurIPS 2024): multiplicative-noise priors + BMR give a threshold-free pruning rule with O(1) scoring per element and Pareto-optimal accuracy-compression on classical and modern networks; Markovic, Friston & Kiebel (2023/24) applied BMR sparsification to deep nets. | [Beckers et al., arXiv 2406.01345](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01345) | 2024 | moderate |
| WorldLLM (2025): LLM world model whose natural-language hypotheses are refined by Bayesian inference with a second LLM as proposal distribution, and evidence gathered by a curiosity-driven RL policy seeking low-log-likelihood transitions; improved predictive accuracy in a textual object-manipulation game. | [arXiv 2506.06725; github.com/flowersteam/WorldLLM](https://github.com/flowersteam/WorldLLM) | 2025 | moderate |
| 'Thermodynamic Signatures of Reasoning' (June 2026) computes free-energy functionals (Z, F, S, C) and spectral form factors from symmetrized attention-map Laplacians as inference-time hallucination detectors; quantitative results not retrievable. | [arXiv 2606.19404](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19404) | 2026 | weak |
| 'Active Inference as the Test-Time Scaling Law for Physical AI Agents' (Friston, Razi et al. 2026) and 'Active Inference for Physical AI Agents -- An Engineering Perspective' (2026) argue AIF-based prediction-error resolution grounds observed LLM test-time scaling; theoretical, no LM experiments. | [arXiv 2606.22813; arXiv 2603.20927](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22813) | 2026 | speculative |
| No work was found that combines the free energy principle / active inference with the Drosophila connectome (FlyWire or MaleCNS); connectome-constrained whole-brain fitting exists (e.g., online fitting of FlyWire-constrained models) but without FEP objectives. | [Search across FlyWire, OpenReview connectome fitting, AIF literature](https://openreview.net/pdf?id=wCBNxp1qWe) | 2024-2026 | moderate |

**Usable ideas**

- Treat next-token cross-entropy as the accuracy term of a variational free energy with an amortized posterior, then add a bounded number of iterative refinement steps (EBT/PC-style) only where it pays: EBT shows +29% System-2 gains on language and up to 35% better scaling, while PC degrades beyond 5-7 layers, so keep PC relaxation shallow (top of hierarchy) or add error highways / per-layer precision weighting.
- Use the incremental-PC schedule (update weights at every inference step, never wait for inference convergence): iPC matched or beat BP on masked LM (106 vs 120 PPL) where vanilla PC collapsed (523 PPL, 3/10 seeds).
- Implement attention as precision: learn per-layer, per-channel log-precisions (inverse variances) that gate prediction errors; this is simultaneously Friston's 'attention = precision optimisation', the fix for PC depth imbalance (2506.23800), and consistent with 'attention = Helmholtz free-energy descent' (2511.00907).
- Make EFE decoding an inference problem, not a search: augment the generative model with preference and epistemic priors so EFE minimization becomes VFE minimization with entropy-correction and planning-correction terms (de Vries 2025; Nuijten 2026); run it as message passing over a short planning graph of candidate continuations.
- Define the epistemic term concretely as expected information gain about a specific latent (e.g., the MB memory contents or the ring-attractor state), normalized by token cost (Dutta et al. 2026); in deterministic settings this reduces to EIG, which already yields measurable (if modest, +3.7% success at +0.3 steps) gains in language agents.
- Set the preference prior C via the RLHF equivalence: KL-regularized RL is variational inference with the pretrained model as prior and reward as log-likelihood, so FlyAI's pragmatic value can be trained with standard KL-regularized policy optimization; FREIA (ACL 2026) shows free-energy-shaped unsupervised rewards also work.
- Use Bayesian model reduction (BMRS multiplicative-noise formulation; Friston's structure-information-gain term) to prune/grow the connectome-derived masks under one Bayesian criterion instead of hand thresholds; AXIOM demonstrates BMR-driven growing/pruning at small scale.
- Adopt the deep-temporal / RGM 'paths as latents' structure: a discrete high-level HMM over coarse-grained events with a continuous low-level token filter, giving nested timescales (Friston 2017 reading model; RGM 2024/2026 verified implementation).
- Derive the low-level filter from temporal predictive coding (approximates Kalman filtering with local rules) and consider iP-VAE/FOND-style natural-gradient online updates with Poisson/spike-count latents, which gave 25x parameter savings and better OOD generalization in vision and match Dale/E-I fly modules.
- Prevent posterior collapse by making latents structural and discrete (MB memory slots, ring attractor phase) and by adding latent-space predictive/contrastive terms (LLM-JEPA, TWISTER's CPC objective) rather than relying on a continuous Gaussian z the decoder can ignore.
- Evaluate Bayesian-ness with closed-form 'wind tunnels' (HMM state tracking, bijection elimination; target 1e-3 bit posterior accuracy) and with martingale/order-permutation tests (Chlon & Rashidi) to detect positional-encoding-induced deviations.
- Close the action-perception loop for text via WorldLLM-style surprise-seeking exploration and clarification/tool-call actions selected by EFE; this is what distinguishes an active-inference LM from a passive one (Kulveit 2023; Pezzulo et al. 2024).
- Use VFE-based coalition analysis of attention heads (up to 20% removable) as a cheap structure-learning probe for redundancy in connectome-constrained heads.

**Dead ends**

- Vanilla predictive coding run to inference convergence at depth: performance collapses beyond 5-7 layers; on BERT-style LM standard PC gave 523 PPL vs 120 for BP with only 3/10 seeds converging; the PCX maintainer suspects inference-to-convergence is itself wrong under the current formulation.
- Autoregressive (conditional) language modelling with PC/iPC: iPC lost to BP (142.5 vs 113.3 PPL) even at BERT-small scale; no PC decoder LM has been reported since.
- Continuous-latent VAE language models under an ELBO/VFE objective: posterior collapse (decoder ignores z) since Bowman 2016; 2025 GP-VAE LMs only beat 'minimal' transformer baselines on WikiText-2 in short-context regimes.
- Discrete active inference with explicit policy enumeration and hand-specified A/B/C/D matrices (pymdp wrappers around LLMs): exponential in horizon and state size, yields only qualitative results (Multi-LLM AIF 2024, Orchestrator 2025 mazes), and does not touch the LM's own objective.
- Deep active inference for control 2019-2022: matched but did not surpass reward-based RL, required reduced/bootstrapped EFE approximations, and has been overtaken by Dreamer/TD-MPC lines; AXIOM's 2025 wins are on a bespoke Gameworld 10k, not standard Atari 100k.
- 'Active inference' as a prompting metaphor (therapist/supervisor actor-critic loops in npj Digital Medicine 2025): no free energy is computed, so results cannot be attributed to FEP and should not be cited as evidence for AIF LMs.
- Hand-crafted deep temporal language models (Friston 2017 reading, 2021 active listening): tiny lexicons, no learning from corpora, no perplexity evaluation; do not scale without learned generative models.
- Vendor benchmark claims (Verses Genius vs o1/R1 on Mastermind: 100% vs 71%, 140x faster, 5260x cheaper): single narrow game, press-release only; not evidence about language modelling.
- Pure amortized encoders for free-energy inference: iP-VAE shows worse OOD generalization than iterative inference, and 'Transformers are Bayesian networks'-style exactness claims hold only for sigmoid (not softmax) transformers in a single-author preprint.
- Renormalising generative models applied to text: no attempt found; RGMs require discretized/quantized observations and have only been shown on images, movies, music and Atari-like games.

**Open gaps**

- No end-to-end AIF-native LM trained on a real corpus with VFE + EFE objectives at >=100M parameters; the only PC-vs-BP language numbers are BERT-small on 200k One Billion Word sentences (2024).
- No demonstration that EFE-based decoding (epistemic + pragmatic) improves generation quality or calibration over beam/nucleus/entropy-minimization decoding at any scale; the epistemic target latent for language is undefined in the literature.
- No principled method for setting the preference prior C for open-ended text beyond reusing RLHF rewards; no study of how EFE interacts with KL-regularized fine-tuning in practice.
- Attention-as-precision has not been implemented with explicit precision hyperpriors and prediction-error gating in a language model; precision learning across layers is only studied for PC depth on MNIST.
- PC depth fixes (error highways, muPC, precision-weighted relaxation) are validated on MNIST-level tasks only; no deep PC transformer on text exists.
- Scalable joint structure-parameter learning (BMR-driven growth/pruning) is unsolved beyond AXIOM-scale mixture models, per the AIF community's own position paper.
- No prior work combines the FEP/active inference with a connectome prior (Drosophila or otherwise); connectome-constrained models exist only with standard fitting objectives.
- The closed action-perception loop for LMs (Kulveit 2023) has no controlled empirical study; clarification/context-acquisition results (+3.7% success) are the only quantified epistemic-action gains.
- Transformers are Bayesian only in expectation over orderings (positional-encoding martingale violations); how an explicit VFE objective interacts with positional encodings and whether it restores exchangeability is unstudied.
- Inference-time compute accounting for iterative free-energy descent (EBT, PC) versus a single forward pass at matched FLOPs has only been reported for small EBTs; no scaling study for PC or AIF decoders on text.
- No standard benchmark for epistemic/active behaviour in LMs beyond toy Optimal Question Asking and tau-Bench clarifications; Bayesian 'wind tunnel' tasks exist but have not been adopted for AIF LMs.

**Key numbers**

- iPC vs BP vs PC, BERT-style masked LM, One Billion Word (200k train / 10k dev, 10 seeds): PPL 106.19±10.54 (10/10 converged) vs 120.02±13.19 (7/10) vs 523.08±12.3 (3/10) [Salvatori et al. ICLR 2024]
- iPC vs BP vs PC, conditional (causal) LM: 142.54±4.23 (7/10) vs 113.32±0.36 (10/10) vs 206.34±6.46 (10/10) [same]
- PCNs degrade significantly beyond 5-7 layers [2506.23800]; Error Highways trains 128-layer MLPs on MNIST/F-MNIST with depth-robust accuracy [2606.22744]; muPC claims 100+ layers [2505.13124]
- Energy-Based Transformers: up to 35% higher scaling rate vs Transformer++ (data, batch, params, FLOPs, depth); +29% more improvement from System-2 iteration on language tasks [2507.02092, ICLR 2026 oral]
- Small transformers reproduce closed-form Bayesian posteriors to 1e-3 to 1e-4 bits; capacity-matched MLPs fail by orders of magnitude [2512.22471]
- Martingale violations from positional encodings Theta(log n / n); excess risk O(n^-1/2) in expectation over orderings; optimal CoT length k* = Theta(sqrt(n) log(1/eps)) [2507.11768]
- Up to 20% of attention heads removable with acceptable perplexity increase under VFE-based coalition analysis [2605.09515]
- Information-gain clarification: +3.7% success rate over no-clarification, +0.3 interaction steps, 5 backbones, tau-Bench [2606.03135, ICML 2026]
- Optimal Question Asking: GPT-4o and Haiku 3.5 show a 1-2 query planning gap vs optimum on 25-object tasks [OpenReview 1qLZsyJN2t]
- AXIOM vs BBF/DreamerV3 on Gameworld 10k: normalized score 77 vs 48; 7.6x sample efficiency (3,175 vs 24,207 steps); 39x faster GPU runtime [2505.24784]
- Verses Genius Mastermind: 100% solved vs o1-preview 71%; 140x faster; 5260x cheaper; vs DeepSeek R1 245x faster, 779x cheaper (press releases, Dec 2024 / Feb 2025)
- TWISTER (CPC transformer world model): 162% mean / 77% median human-normalized Atari 100k vs DreamerV3 112%/49%, STORM 127%/58% [2503.04416]
- iP-VAE: best reconstruction-sparsity trade-off with 25x fewer parameters and integer spike-count latents; scales to CelebA [2410.19315, NeurIPS 2025]
- Multi-LLM AIF wrapper: 3 state factors, 7 observation modalities, no quantitative baseline [2412.10425]
- npj Digital Medicine 'active inference' prompting: 100 patient queries, blind therapist ratings, LLM responses rated >= therapist-written responses [s41746-025-01516-2]
- EFE-as-VI message passing beat KL-control agents on stochastic gridworld and MiniGrid DoorKey-8x8 (toy) [2508.02197]
- FlyWire connectome: 139,255 proofread neurons, >50M synapses (context for connectome priors; no AIF work on it)

## lit:iterative-inference

40 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Energy-Based Transformers (EBTs) assign a scalar energy to (context, candidate) pairs and predict by gradient descent on the energy. Reported: up to 35% higher scaling rate than Transformer++ w.r.t. data, batch size, parameters, FLOPs and depth (measured as slope of log-log fits); 'thinking longer' (more GD steps) and 'self-verification' (best-of-N by min energy) improve LM performance by up to 29% more than Transformer++, which shows no gain from extra inference compute. Largest model 800M; text runs include a 44M model on RedPajama-v2 (32B tokens); scaling runs up to ~120B tokens. | [Gladstone et al., 'Energy-Based Transformers are Scalable Learners and Thinkers' (arXiv 25](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02092) | 2025 | moderate |
| EBT caveats stated by the authors and summarizers: training costs 3.3x-6.6x the FLOPs of a standard Transformer (extra gradient computations through the energy); new hyperparameters (step size, number of optimization steps) are critical and poorly chosen values give unstable training; pretraining perplexity can be higher than Transformer++ yet downstream perplexity (GSM8K, SQuAD, BigBench MathQA) is lower; no comparison to SOTA reasoning models; not explored beyond 800M. | [EBT paper limitations; the-decoder / emergentmind summaries](https://the-decoder.com/new-energy-based-transformer-architecture-aims-to-bring-better-system-2-thinking-to-ai-models/) | 2025 | moderate |
| System-2 benefits of EBTs grow with out-of-distribution shift: a roughly linear relationship between OOD shift magnitude and the gain from thinking; energy behaves as an epistemic-uncertainty signal (high energy = OOD/hard input); self-verification gains increase with model/data scale. | [EBT paper (Section on System 2 thinking / OOD)](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.02092v1) | 2025 | moderate |
| The EBT energy scalar (NRGPT) is a robust predictor of human reading times across Natural Stories, UCL eye-tracking and UCL self-paced reading, giving significant fit beyond surprisal and attention entropy in all three corpora, and appears to subsume both. | [Dotlacil & Takmaz, 'Energy-Based Transformers as Predictors of Reading Difficulty' (arXiv ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23382) | 2026 | moderate |
| Huginn-3.5B (recurrent-depth transformer, 3.5B params, 800B tokens) improves with test-time recurrence up to a compute load equivalent to a 50B-parameter model: ARC-Challenge 27.99 (r=4) -> 38.23 (r=32); HellaSwag 43.46 -> 65.21; MMLU 23.39 -> 31.38. GSM8K improves only marginally with recurrence and stays far below explicit-CoT models. Latency scales linearly with loop count. | [Geiping et al., 'Scaling up Test-Time Compute with Latent Reasoning: A Recurrent Depth App](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05171) | 2025 | strong |
| Probing Huginn's latent trajectories finds limited evidence of an interpretable latent chain of thought; interpretability degrades with recurrence depth. | ['Latent Chain-of-Thought? Decoding the Depth-Recurrent Transformer' (arXiv 2507.02199)](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.02199) | 2025 | moderate |
| In recurrent-depth transformers, loop steps become smaller and increasingly orthogonal over training (within-block refinement, across-block drift). An early-exit rule based on the second-order difference in step size outperforms the KL-divergence exit in accuracy, stability and time. | ['Two-Scale Latent Dynamics for Recurrent-Depth Transformers' (arXiv 2509.23314)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23314) | 2025 | moderate |
| Ouro (LoopLM) 1.4B/2.6B trained on 7.7T tokens with 4 recurrent steps and an entropy-regularized exit objective match up to 12B SOTA LLMs; vs Qwen3-4B: BBH 71.02 vs 70.95, GSM8K 78.92 vs 72.86, MATH500 82.40 vs 59.60. Controlled experiments attribute the gain to knowledge manipulation, not capacity. But per-token inference FLOPs are 6.75x Qwen3-1.7B and 2.63x Qwen3-4B, so the comparison is parameter-matched, not FLOP-matched. | [Zhu et al., 'Scaling Latent Reasoning via Looped Language Models' (arXiv 2510.25741); FLOP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.25741) | 2025 | strong |
| Iso-depth scaling law for looped LMs: sweeping recurrence r in {1,2,4,8} over ~50x training compute gives a recurrence-equivalence exponent phi = 0.46 (phi=1 would mean a recurrence is worth a unique block; 0 means no capacity gain). Example: a 410M looped model at r=4 matches a 580M non-looped model but costs as much to train as a 1B non-looped one. Truncated backprop through loops lowers phi to 0.38; hyperconnections raise it to 0.65. | ['How Much Is One Recurrence Worth? Iso-Depth Scaling Laws for Looped Language Models' (arX](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.21106v3) | 2026 | strong |
| Mixture-of-Recursions (135M-1.7B, recursion count 3, ~1/3 unique params): for models >360M it matches or exceeds vanilla Transformer at equal training FLOPs (isoFLOP budgets 2e18, 5e18, 16.5e18) with lower validation perplexity and higher few-shot accuracy; MoR-4 with continuous depth-wise batching and early exit gives up to 2.06x throughput. Expert-choice routing plus recursion-wise KV caching were required. | [Bae et al., 'Mixture-of-Recursions' (NeurIPS 2025, arXiv 2507.10524)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.10524) | 2025 | moderate |
| Before MoEUT (NeurIPS 2024) no shared-layer (Universal) Transformer design had been competitive on parameter-dominated tasks like language modeling (the 'parameter-compute ratio problem'); MoEUT with sigma-MoE feedforward and SwitchHead attention only slightly outperforms standard Transformers on BLiMP/PIQA while using less compute and memory. | [Csordas et al., 'MoEUT: Mixture-of-Experts Universal Transformers' (arXiv 2405.16039)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16039) | 2024 | moderate |
| Learned halting in looped Transformers entangles exit selection with trajectory formation because one exit distribution both stops inference and weights per-depth losses. On modular arithmetic/parity and on Ouro-1.4B/2.6B checkpoints, fixed-prior depth supervision (no input-dependent halting policy) yields difficulty-aware trajectories that outperform jointly learned gates; Ouro's pretrained ponder gates are competitive but not Pareto-optimal. | ['Adaptive Depth in Looped Transformers: Diagnosing Learned Halting Gates and Trajectory Re](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20519) | 2026 | moderate |
| Per-loop cross-entropy through scale-invariant readouts (RMSNorm/LayerNorm) leaves recurrent hidden-state scale uncontrolled: in 44M and 129M looped transformers without inter-loop normalization, final hidden-state norms grow into the thousands or tens of thousands while early exits remain usable. Scale-visible readouts or explicit norm penalties keep norms in the tens and lower perplexity on variable-depth benchmarks. | [Sharma & Vu, 'Dense Supervision Is Not Enough: The Readout Blind Spot in Looped Language M](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24898) | 2026 | moderate |
| Whether extra test-time iterations improve, preserve or degrade an answer is predicted by the trained operator's finite-time dynamical regime (settling / marginal / drifting). A sufficient depth-safety condition: once per-step displacement is small relative to the decoder margin, further iteration cannot change the decoded answer. On algorithmic tasks trained from 800 unaugmented examples per tier, settling operators never degrade with depth and sometimes convert depth into accuracy on harder unseen instances (e.g., Sudoku). | ['Think Shallow, Solve Deep: Controlling Recurrent Dynamics for Reliable Test-Time Depth' (](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18222) | 2026 | moderate |
| ANIRA (ICML 2026): with confounds removed, per-token compute allocation in recurrent Transformers does align with algorithmic token complexity without difficulty supervision, and online halting tracks execution state better than early (shallow-representation) allocation; but models still fail to extrapolate to unseen input sizes despite allocating more compute. | ['Understanding Dynamic Compute Allocation in Recurrent Transformers' (arXiv 2602.08864); G](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.08864) | 2026 | moderate |
| Continuous Depth Batching realizes up to 99% of the theoretical adaptive-depth speedup on Ouro-1.4B and Huginn-3.5B: 1.5-1.9x higher offline throughput and 45-90% lower normalized latency under dynamic load. | ['Depth-adaptive Inference of Looped Language Models via Continuous Depth Batching' (arXiv ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09444) | 2026 | moderate |
| LoopFormer (ICLR 2026) trains looped models with variable-length trajectories and a shortcut-consistency regularizer; at a 12x compute budget its perplexity stays close to a non-looped 12x Base, reasoning accuracy scales with budget, and training overhead is ~1.5x FLOPs of fixed-loop/vanilla training. | [Jeddi et al., 'LoopFormer: Elastic-Depth Looped Transformers for Latent Reasoning via Shor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11451) | 2026 | moderate |
| AdaPonderLM: self-supervised token-wise early exit during pretraining (Pythia 70M-410M; continued pretraining to 2.8B) with iteration-specific MLP gates and monotonic halting; learned gates allocate more compute to high-NLL (hard) tokens; ~10% inference compute reduction at comparable perplexity; under iso-FLOPs the learned policy beats fixed pruning. | ['AdaPonderLM: Gated Pondering Language Models with Token-Wise Adaptive Depth' (arXiv 2603.](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01914) | 2026 | moderate |
| Recurrent-depth transformers acquire implicit (single-pass) compositional reasoning through a three-stage grokking process (memorization -> in-distribution -> systematic generalization); depth extrapolation can be unlocked by more inference-time recurrence, but 'overthinking' (excessive recurrence) degrades predictions and limits generalization to very deep compositions. | ['Loop, Think, & Generalize: Implicit Reasoning in Recurrent-Depth Transformers' (arXiv 260](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07822) | 2026 | moderate |
| Mechanistically, in looped reasoning LMs each layer in the cycle converges to a distinct fixed point so the block follows a consistent cyclic trajectory; attention-head behaviour stabilizes as fixed points are reached and mirrors the 'mixing' inference stages of feedforward models; stable fixed-point models preserve these stages while unstable ones deviate. | [Blayney et al., 'A Mechanistic Analysis of Looped Reasoning Language Models' (arXiv 2604.1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11791) | 2026 | moderate |
| Latent Thinking Optimization (ICLR 2026): a latent classifier reliably predicts answer correctness from Huginn's latent thoughts; used as a Latent Reward Model it improves test-time thinking directly in latent space. | [Du et al., 'Latent Thinking Optimization' (arXiv 2509.26314)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26314) | 2026 | moderate |
| ARC Prize independent analysis of the Hierarchical Reasoning Model: the H/L hierarchy has minimal impact versus a similar-size transformer; the outer-loop refinement with deep supervision is the true driver, especially during training. Tiny Recursive Model (single 2-layer network) generalizes better than HRM. At inference most useful computation happens in the first recursion step, with later steps giving modest refinement. | [ARC Prize HRM analysis (summarized); Jolicoeur-Martineau, 'Less is More: Recursive Reasoni](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04871) | 2025 | moderate |
| Press reports (The Information, 1 Sept 2026) say OpenAI's 'Astra' model uses a constrained form of recurrent depth (looped transformers), obscuring some chain-of-thought; OpenAI's chief scientist said the looped architecture's use was deliberately limited so reasoning remains legible. Benchmark figures circulating (e.g., 99.9% ARC-AGI-3) are unverified. | [Fortune (3 Sept 2026); Sebastian Raschka blog on Astra and looped transformers](https://fortune.com/2026/09/03/reports-openais-astra-model-uses-a-new-more-efficient-ai-architecture-alarms-ai-safety-experts-who-worry-the-method-makes-models-harder-to-control/) | 2026 | weak |
| TTT layers (TTT-Linear, TTT-MLP; 125M-1.3B) keep reducing perplexity as context grows (like Transformers) whereas Mamba stops improving after 16k; TTT-Linear is faster than Transformer at 8k context and matches Mamba wall-clock; TTT-MLP is bottlenecked by memory I/O. | [Sun et al., 'Learning to (Learn at Test Time): RNNs with Expressive Hidden States' (ICML 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04620) | 2024 | strong |
| LaCT (Test-Time Training Done Right) updates fast weights with very large chunks (2K-1M tokens, tokens within a chunk treated as an unordered set) plus window attention; reaches ~70% GPU utilization on A100 and gives lower per-token loss and up to +20pp retrieval accuracy vs GLA/DeltaNet at long positions. | ['Test-Time Training Done Right' (ICLR 2026, arXiv 2505.23884); GitHub a1600012888/LaCT](https://github.com/a1600012888/LaCT) | 2025 | moderate |
| TTT-E2E: a sliding-window Transformer that keeps training on the context at test time via next-token prediction, with a meta-learned initialization. For 3B models trained on 164B tokens, loss scales with context length like full attention (Mamba 2 and Gated DeltaNet do not), with lower loss than full attention throughout; inference latency is constant in context length (2.7x faster than full attention at 128K). Training is 3.4x slower than full attention at 8K because of gradient-of-gradients. Checkpoints: 125M, 1B (1x Chinchilla), 3B (3x Chinchilla), 8K context, one 3B variant extended to 128K on Books. | ['End-to-End Test-Time Training for Long Context' (arXiv 2512.23675); GitHub test-time-trai](https://github.com/test-time-training/e2e) | 2025 | strong |
| Titans (NeurIPS 2025): neural long-term memory updated at test time with a surprise signal = gradient of the memory loss, plus momentum ('past surprise') and weight-decay forgetting; variants MAC/MAG/MAL at 170M/340M/400M/760M trained on 15B-30B FineWeb-Edu tokens; beats Transformer++, RetNet, GLA, Mamba/Mamba2, DeltaNet, TTT, Gated DeltaNet on LM/commonsense; fine-tuned MAC beats GPT-4 and RAG-Llama-3 on BABILong and scales past 2M tokens. No official code released since Jan 2025. | [Behrouz et al., 'Titans: Learning to Memorize at Test Time' (arXiv 2501.00663)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00663) | 2025 | moderate |
| Independent lightweight reimplementation of Titans (MLM, time series, recommendation): Titans does not always beat established baselines, largely because of chunking; the neural memory component does consistently help over attention-only; memory updates alone are insufficient for meaningful test-time learning because the frozen backbone's key/value projections are mismatched with the evolving memory unless jointly adapted. | ['Titans Revisited: A Lightweight Reimplementation and Critical Analysis of a Test-Time Mem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09551) | 2025 | moderate |
| Small-scale probe (embedding dim 128, corpora of N=10/25/50 facts): Titans memory reaches 100% training-loss convergence yet free-form completion retrieval is 0-40% and forced-choice retrieval 45-85% depending on corpus size, i.e., a memorization-retrieval gap. | ['Facts as First Class Objects: Knowledge Objects for Persistent LLM Memory' (arXiv 2603.17](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.17781) | 2026 | weak |
| ATLAS (340M/400M/790M/1.3B on 15B/15B/30B/100B FineWeb tokens) replaces token-wise surprise with the Omega rule (context-window memorization) optimized with Muon and polynomial feature maps; with these choices deep test-time memorization strictly generalizes softmax attention; on BABILong ATLAS keeps >80% accuracy at 10M context where Titans degrades. MIRAS frames such models by four choices: memory architecture, attentional bias, retention gate, learning algorithm (instances Moneta, Yaad, Memora). | [Behrouz et al., 'ATLAS' (arXiv 2505.23735) and 'It's All Connected' / MIRAS (arXiv 2504.13](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23735) | 2025 | moderate |
| Test-time training on augmented in-context demonstrations gives up to 6x accuracy over fine-tuned baselines on ARC: 53.0% (8B LM) and 61.9% ensembled with program synthesis, matching average human performance. | [Akyurek et al., 'The Surprising Effectiveness of Test-Time Training for Few-Shot Learning'](https://huggingface.co/papers/2411.07279) | 2024 | strong |
| Coconut (continuous thoughts fed back as embeddings) excels at exploration-style tasks (ProsQA 97.0%) but fails on computation-heavy ones (GSM8K 34.1%); scaling to 5 latent thoughts collapses accuracy to 12.5% in follow-up analyses; theory attributes this to continuous representations amplifying noise on precise computation. | [Hao et al., 'Training LLMs to Reason in a Continuous Latent Space' (arXiv 2412.06769); 'Ca](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06769) | 2024 | moderate |
| Pause tokens give inference-time gains only when models are both pretrained and finetuned with pauses (1B: +18% EM on SQuAD, +8% CommonsenseQA, +1% GSM8K); a later analysis finds thinking tokens underperform in practice. | [Goyal et al., 'Think before you speak' (ICLR 2024, arXiv 2310.02226); 'Rethinking Thinking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.02226) | 2023 | moderate |
| RL post-training for adaptive latent-reasoning length (Llama-3.2-1B, GSM8K-Aug) cuts total reasoning length by 52% with no accuracy penalty; a separate GRPO router learns NoThink/Short/Long modes on a 1.5B model that sort problems by difficulty without mode collapse. | ['Learning When to Stop: Adaptive Latent Reasoning via RL' (arXiv 2511.21581); 'Learning Wh](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21581) | 2025 | moderate |
| Predictive coding as a training algorithm: muPC trains up to 128-layer residual MLPs on classification competitively, but it is unknown whether standard Transformers (shallow or deep) can be trained with PC at all; a 2025 theory/practice review concludes PC currently offers no practical benefit over backprop at modern scale and would need non-GPU hardware because PC inference is inherently sequential. | ['muPC: Scaling Predictive Coding to 100+ Layer Networks' (arXiv 2505.13124); 'Towards Scal](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.23323) | 2025 | moderate |
| Supervised Memory Training (SMT) trains nonlinear RNNs without BPTT by supervising one-step memory transitions with labels from a Transformer trained on a predictive-state objective; time-parallel, O(1)-length gradient path; outperforms BPTT when pretraining several RNN architectures on language modeling and pixel sequences. | [Kumar & Isola, 'Pretraining Recurrent Networks without Recurrence' (arXiv 2606.06479); Git](https://github.com/akarshkumar0101/smt) | 2026 | moderate |
| Masked-diffusion LMs (an alternative iterative-refinement route) beat autoregressive models only in data-constrained, compute-abundant regimes (critical compute threshold derived in closed form; the '16x more compute' framing); LLaDA-8B on 2.3T tokens roughly matches LLaMA-3-8B trained on 15T tokens on several benchmarks. | [Prabhudesai et al., 'Diffusion Beats Autoregressive in Data-Constrained Settings' (arXiv 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15857) | 2025 | moderate |
| Early-exit methods for LMs: CALM performed worse than a simple static-exit baseline on all summarization datasets (state-copying and many exit points); exited tokens lack deeper-layer KV pairs needed by later tokens and approximating them hurts; Mixture-of-Depths 'can hardly guarantee both efficiency and effectiveness without extremely costly training'. | ['Accelerating LLM Inference via Early-Exiting Algorithms' (arXiv 2509.05915); 'MoDificatio](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.05915) | 2025 | moderate |
| Compression is fragile for recursive reasoners: naive INT4 quantization and pruning collapse puzzle accuracy despite cell-level preservation; and DEQ-style 'physics-structured' equilibrium reasoners show a lazy identity-collapse failure mode around initialization. | [Awesome-Loop-Models curated list entries: 'What Survives When You Compress' (June 2026) an](https://github.com/huskydoge/Awesome-Loop-Models) | 2026 | weak |
| Searches for 2025-2026 work applying expected free energy (pragmatic + epistemic value) to LLM decoding or to an implemented active-inference language model returned only conceptual pieces (e.g., IWAI 2025 'Free Energy Principle and Active Inference in Neural Language Models') and robotics/POMDP planning papers; no LM-scale implementation with numbers was found. | [CEUR-WS Vol-3923 Paper 3; EFE literature (Millidge et al.; Friston et al.)](https://ceur-ws.org/Vol-3923/Paper_3.pdf) | 2025 | weak |

**Usable ideas**

- Use a scalar energy (EBT-style verifier) as the single 'free energy' quantity that (a) drives per-token gradient/relaxation iterations, (b) gates iteration count (high energy = OOD/hard token gets more steps), and (c) does self-verification by min-energy selection among candidates; the EBT reading-time result suggests this scalar also tracks human processing difficulty, which can be a validation target.
- Decouple halting from trajectory shaping: train the recurrent core with fixed-prior per-depth supervision (uniform or difficulty-agnostic weighting over depths), and halt at inference with a dynamics-based criterion (second-order difference of step size, or per-step displacement below decoder margin) rather than a jointly learned gate.
- Enforce a settling (contractive) regime in the recurrent operator (Lyapunov/contraction constraint, scale-visible readouts or explicit norm penalties, inter-loop normalization) so extra iterations are 'depth-safe'; monitor fixed-point stability and hidden-state norm as health metrics.
- Restore capacity lost by weight tying: hyperconnections raised the recurrence-equivalence exponent from 0.46 to 0.65; MoE-style sublayers (MoEUT) or connectome-structured sparse but wide recurrent blocks are alternatives to test under iso-depth scaling.
- Surprise-driven weight memory: implement the Titans/MIRAS rule (gradient of memory loss + momentum + decay forgetting) but (i) jointly adapt read/write projections with the memory (Titans Revisited), (ii) memorize context windows with the Omega rule and a Muon-like optimizer (ATLAS), (iii) apply updates in large chunks for hardware efficiency (LaCT), and (iv) evaluate on-demand retrieval, not reconstruction loss.
- Adopt TTT-E2E's recipe for long context: sliding-window attention plus continual next-token learning on the context with a meta-learned initialization; budget the ~3.4x training slowdown and reap constant-latency inference.
- Combine activation-space iteration (looping) with weight-space iteration (TTT/Titans) under one energy, which no published system does; a precision-weighted (inverse-variance) prediction error is absent from EBT/looped models and is the natural place to inject an active-inference prior.
- Deep supervision on every iteration plus an outer refinement loop is what actually drove HRM/TRM gains; ablate any hierarchical (e.g., MB/CX) structure against a flat iterated block with the same supervision.
- Keep a discrete/symbolic output channel (CoT or explicit tokens) for exact serial computation; continuous latent iteration wins on exploration/search tasks but fails on GSM8K-style arithmetic.
- Report matched-compute accounting from day one: iso-depth phi, inference FLOPs per token relative to a dense baseline (Ouro: 2.63x-6.75x), training FLOPs multiple (EBT: 3.3x-6.6x; TTT-E2E: 3.4x), and use continuous depth batching for serving so adaptive depth gives real latency savings (1.5-1.9x).
- Consider SMT (supervised memory training) to pretrain the recurrent core without BPTT, avoiding the truncated-backprop penalty; and consider a masked-diffusion objective if the training corpus is data-constrained.
- Train a latent verifier (Latent Thinking Optimization) or let the energy act as the latent reward to select among iteration trajectories; evaluate legibility by decoding intermediate states, since press reports and probing studies both flag opacity of latent iteration.

**Dead ends**

- Naive weight-tied Universal Transformers / ACT / PonderNet for language modeling: never competitive with standard Transformers on parameter-dominated LM tasks (parameter-compute ratio problem) until MoEUT added MoE capacity, and even then only slight gains.
- Jointly learned halting gates that also weight per-depth losses: entangle exit selection with trajectory formation and underperform fixed-prior depth supervision; Ouro's learned ponder gates are not Pareto-optimal.
- Expecting adaptive per-token depth to deliver length/complexity extrapolation: ANIRA shows allocation aligns with difficulty but models still fail on unseen input sizes; realized savings from learned halting are modest (~10% in AdaPonderLM).
- Unbounded 'think longer': overthinking degrades predictions (Loop, Think & Generalize; drifting operators in Think Shallow, Solve Deep); iteration must be convergence-gated.
- Looping as a free lunch: at matched training compute, shared recurrence increases validation loss (phi = 0.46; a 410M r=4 looped model equals a 580M dense model but costs like 1B to train); truncated BPTT makes it worse (phi = 0.38); Ouro's 'matches 12B' is at 2.6x-6.75x more inference FLOPs.
- Recurrent depth as a replacement for chain-of-thought on serial computation: Huginn's GSM8K barely improves with recurrence; Coconut collapses on GSM8K (34.1%) and with >~5 latent thoughts (12.5%).
- Pause/thinking tokens added without pretraining them in: little or no gain; thinking tokens underperform in practice.
- CALM-style confidence early exit: worse than static exit on summarization; per-token exits break the KV cache for later tokens; Mixture-of-Depths needs very costly training to be both fast and accurate.
- Titans as published: no code, chunked training removes its edge on several tasks, memory-only updates without co-adapting projections give limited test-time learning, and small probes show a memorization-retrieval gap (100% fit vs 0-40% free-form recall).
- Predictive coding as the learning algorithm at LM scale: no practical benefit over backprop, transformers not shown trainable with PC, inherently sequential inference cost on GPUs.
- HRM-style two-timescale hierarchy: independent ablation found the hierarchy contributed little; a 2-layer tiny recursive net with deep supervision did better; most inference-time gain comes from the first refinement step.
- EBTs as currently demonstrated: 3.3x-6.6x training FLOPs, fragile step-size/step-count hyperparameters, higher pretraining perplexity, <=800M scale, no independent FLOP-matched replication; scaling-rate advantages are extrapolated slopes.
- Scale-invariant readouts (RMSNorm/LayerNorm) with per-loop losses in looped models: hidden-state norms silently explode into the thousands while early exits look fine.
- DEQ/equilibrium reasoners near initialization: lazy identity-collapse; recursive reasoners under naive INT4/pruning: puzzle accuracy collapses (both from 2026 curated-list summaries, weakly sourced).

**Open gaps**

- No independent, FLOP-matched replication of Energy-Based Transformers at >=1B parameters, and no head-to-head between EBT (energy descent), looped transformers (fixed iteration) and TTT (weight updates) at matched training AND inference FLOPs.
- No implemented active-inference language model with expected-free-energy (epistemic + pragmatic) decoding at any scale; the only material found is conceptual.
- No iterative-inference LM uses precision-weighted (inverse-variance) prediction errors; energy/loss is an unweighted scalar in EBT, looped models and TTT, so the 'precision' half of predictive coding is untested in LMs.
- No system unifies activation-space iteration (looping/energy descent) with weight-space iteration (surprise-driven memory/TTT) under a single objective; whether the two forms of test-time compute are complementary or redundant is unknown.
- Dynamics-based halting (settling regime, depth-safety margin) is validated only on algorithmic tasks with ~800 examples per tier, not on natural-language pretraining at scale.
- Whether structured biological priors (a connectome-constrained sparse recurrent operator, Dale's law, E/I balance) change the recurrence-equivalence exponent phi, the fixed-point stability, or the settling regime has not been measured.
- The memorization-vs-retrieval gap of test-time weight memory lacks a standard benchmark beyond BABILong/NIAH; no evidence on retrieval reliability at multi-billion scale after long streams of updates.
