- Energy-as-reading-difficulty is shown only for a small NRGPT model; unknown whether energy remains a cognitive-effort predictor under iteration count changes or at scale.
- Legibility/safety of latent iteration is unresolved: probing finds little interpretable latent CoT, latent reward models exist only for Huginn, and industry reportedly limits recurrent depth to keep reasoning legible.
- Joint reporting of training-cost multiples and inference-cost multiples is rare; the trade-off frontier (e.g., TTT-E2E 3.4x slower training vs 2.7x faster inference at 128K) is unmapped for energy-based and looped models.

**Key numbers**

- EBT: up to 35% higher scaling rate vs Transformer++ (data, batch, params, FLOPs, depth); System-2 thinking gain 29% more than Transformer++; models <=800M; 44M text model on 32B RedPajama-v2 tokens; training FLOPs 3.3x-6.6x of standard Transformer (arXiv 2507.02092)
- EBT energy predicts reading times beyond surprisal + attention entropy on 3 corpora (arXiv 2606.23382)
- Huginn-3.5B: 3.5B params, 800B tokens; ARC-C 27.99 -> 38.23, HellaSwag 43.46 -> 65.21, MMLU 23.39 -> 31.38 from r=4 to r=32; effective compute of a 50B model (arXiv 2502.05171)
- Ouro: 1.4B/2.6B, 7.7T tokens, 4 loops; vs Qwen3-4B: BBH 71.02 vs 70.95, GSM8K 78.92 vs 72.86, MATH500 82.40 vs 59.60; inference FLOPs/token 6.75x Qwen3-1.7B, 2.63x Qwen3-4B (arXiv 2510.25741, 2602.10520)
- Iso-depth scaling: phi = 0.46 (r in {1,2,4,8}, ~50x compute); 410M looped r=4 == 580M dense at cost of 1B dense; truncated BPTT phi = 0.38; hyperconnections phi = 0.65 (arXiv 2604.21106)
- MoR: 135M-1.7B, recursion 3 (~1/3 unique params), isoFLOP 2e18/5e18/16.5e18, matches/exceeds vanilla above 360M; MoR-4 throughput up to 2.06x (arXiv 2507.10524)
- Continuous Depth Batching: 99% of theoretical adaptive-depth speedup; 1.5-1.9x throughput; 45-90% lower normalized latency on Ouro-1.4B/Huginn-3.5B (arXiv 2608.09444)
- LoopFormer: ~1.5x training FLOPs overhead; perplexity at 12x budget close to non-looped 12x Base (arXiv 2602.11451)
- AdaPonderLM: Pythia 70M-410M (pretrain), 2.8B (continued); ~10% inference compute reduction at comparable perplexity (arXiv 2603.01914)
- Readout blind spot: 44M/129M looped models, hidden-state norms in the thousands to tens of thousands without scale-visible readouts; fixes keep norms in the tens (arXiv 2606.24898)
- Think Shallow, Solve Deep: 800 unaugmented examples per difficulty tier; settling operators never degrade with depth (arXiv 2608.18222)
- TTT-E2E: 3B on 164B tokens; constant inference latency; 2.7x faster than full attention at 128K; training 3.4x slower at 8K; checkpoints 125M/1B/3B, 8K context, 3B extended to 128K (arXiv 2512.23675)
- TTT layers: 125M-1.3B; Mamba stops improving after 16k context, TTT-Linear/MLP keep improving; TTT-Linear faster than Transformer at 8k (arXiv 2407.04620)
- LaCT: chunks 2K-1M tokens; ~70% A100 utilization; up to +20pp long-position retrieval vs GLA/DeltaNet (arXiv 2505.23884)
- Titans: 170M/340M/400M/760M on 15B-30B FineWeb-Edu tokens; fine-tuned MAC beats GPT-4 and RAG-Llama-3 on BABILong; >2M-token context (arXiv 2501.00663)
- Titans retrieval probe: 100% training-loss convergence but 0-40% free-form and 45-85% forced-choice retrieval at dim 128, N=10/25/50 facts (arXiv 2603.17781)
- ATLAS: 340M/400M/790M/1.3B on 15B/15B/30B/100B tokens; >80% BABILong accuracy at 10M context where Titans drops (arXiv 2505.23735)
- Akyurek TTT on ARC: 53.0% (8B) and 61.9% ensembled; up to 6x over fine-tuned baselines (arXiv 2411.07279)
- Coconut: ProsQA 97.0% vs GSM8K 34.1%; collapse to 12.5% with 5 latent thoughts (arXiv 2412.06769 and follow-ups)
- Pause tokens (1B): +18% EM SQuAD, +8% CommonsenseQA, +1% GSM8K, only when pretrained and finetuned with pauses (arXiv 2310.02226)
- Adaptive latent reasoning via RL: 52% shorter reasoning at no accuracy cost on Llama-3.2-1B/GSM8K-Aug (arXiv 2511.21581)
- muPC: PC trains up to 128-layer residual MLPs; transformers not shown trainable with PC (arXiv 2505.13124, 2510.23323)
- LLaDA-8B: 2.3T tokens (~85% less data than LLaMA-3-8B's 15T) for comparable benchmarks; diffusion beats AR only past a critical compute threshold in data-constrained regimes (arXiv 2502.09992, 2507.15857)

## lit:precision-neuromod

30 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Attention = optimisation of precision (gain on prediction-error units). Foundational statement that attentional gain is the expected precision of sensory prediction errors, implemented as post-synaptic gain of superficial pyramidal cells; precision is itself inferred (hyperparameters of the generative model). No empirical ML numbers; simulation only. | [Feldman & Friston, 'Attention, uncertainty, and free-energy', Front. Hum. Neurosci. (found](https://www.frontiersin.org/articles/10.3389/fnhum.2010.00215/full) | 2010 | moderate |
| Dopamine encodes the precision (inverse temperature gamma) of beliefs about policies in active inference, not reward prediction error per se; simulated phasic dopamine responses reproduce RPE-like bursts as changes in expected precision. 'Modulators of synaptic gain like dopamine do not report perceptual content but the context in which percepts are formed.' Discrete-state simulations only, tiny state spaces. | [Friston et al., 'Dopamine, Affordance and Active Inference', PLOS Comput Biol](https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371/journal.pcbi.1002327) | 2012 | moderate |
| Dopamine encodes precision of beliefs about alternative actions and thereby controls outcome-sensitivity of behaviour; updates of gamma look like RPEs (the two hypotheses are hard to distinguish behaviourally). Companion fMRI study reported midbrain BOLD correlating with expected certainty about desired outcomes (precision), n small, single task. | [FitzGerald, Dolan & Friston, 'Dopamine, reward learning, and active inference', Front Comp](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4631836/) | 2015 | moderate |
| Active inference generative models carry distinct precision parameters: zeta (sensory/likelihood precision, A-matrix), omega (transition precision, B-matrix, inverse volatility), gamma (policy precision). Simulated saccades were deployed less often to low-sensory-precision regions; higher volatility shortened inhibition of return. Purely epistemic behaviour emerges from precision-weighted EFE. | [Parr & Friston, 'Uncertainty, epistemics and active inference', J R Soc Interface](https://royalsocietypublishing.org/doi/10.1098/rsif.2017.0376) | 2017 | moderate |
| Locus coeruleus / noradrenaline modelled as a state prediction error under active inference; NA release raises the rate of learning about environmental contingencies (transition precision). Human pupil dilation tracked Bayes-optimal belief updates in a contingency-reversal task; proposes an LC-cortex loop promoting flexibility under uncertainty. Unifies tonic (arousal/flexibility) and phasic (salience) LC modes. | [Sales, Friston, Jones, Pickering & Moran, 'Locus Coeruleus tracking of prediction errors o](https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371/journal.pcbi.1006267) | 2019 | moderate |
| LC as a 'global model failure system' (Jordan, Trends Neurosci 2024); TICS 2025 review: LC-NA drives learning across timescales; PNAS 2026: rats' probabilistic reversal behaviour best fit by adaptive-learning-rate models and orbitofrontal NA release tracked trial-by-trial volatility estimates. | [Jordan 'The locus coeruleus as a global model failure system' (Trends Neurosci); 'Noradren](https://www.pnas.org/doi/abs/10.1073/pnas.2536535123) | 2024-2026 | moderate |
| Doya's mapping of neuromodulators to RL meta-parameters: dopamine = TD/reward prediction error, serotonin = discount gamma, noradrenaline = inverse temperature beta (action randomness), acetylcholine = learning rate alpha. Recent ML instantiations: 'Lifelong RL via Neuromodulation' (2024) built ACh/NA adaptive algorithm validated only on a non-stationary multi-armed bandit; 2025/26 review notes neuromodulatory signals have been approximated by LSTM forget gates and by training weights/biases separately. | [Doya 'Metalearning and neuromodulation', Neural Networks 2002; arXiv:2408.08446; arXiv:250](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08446) | 2002 / 2024 / 2025 | moderate |
| Hierarchical Gaussian Filter: learning rate at each level = ratio of precisions (precision-weighted prediction error); a higher level's value sets the log-variance (step size) of the level below (volatility coupling); generalized HGF (2023-2025, eLife reviewed preprint; PyHGF) extends to arbitrary networks with volatility and noise coupling. 2026 arXiv work gives closed-form predictive coding via HGF and robust volatility updates. | [Mathys et al. 2014; Weber et al. 'The generalized Hierarchical Gaussian Filter'; arXiv:260](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.10937) | 2014-2026 | strong |
| Parr, Pezzulo & Friston argue the problem transformer attention solves is the non-Markovian structure of sequences; attention in transformers and precision in deep temporal active-inference models both increase precision (decrease conditional entropy) of predictions. Position paper; no experiments. | [Parr, Pezzulo & Friston, 'Beyond Markov: Transformers, memory, and attention', Cognitive N](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/17588928.2025.2484485) | 2025 | weak |
| Theorem: for any transformer whose attention logits are bounded, softmax attention coefficients must disperse as the number of items n grows (max coefficient -> 0, entropy -> maximal), so no training fixes sharp size generalisation even for max-retrieval. Proposed fix: inference-time adaptive temperature computed from observed attention entropy (sharpen only); improves OOD max-key retrieval and modestly improves Gemma-2B on CLRS-Text. Presented NeurIPS 2024 workshop, ICML 2025 (PMLR v267). Exact numbers not retrievable here. | [Veličković, Perivolaropoulos, Barbero & Pascanu, 'Softmax is not Enough (for Sharp Size Ge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01104) | 2024/2025 | strong |
| Scalable-Softmax (SSMax): multiply attention logits by s*log(n) with a learnable per-head s (optionally a bias). Prevents 'attention fading' (softmax denominator grows with n). Reported faster pretraining loss reduction and substantially better long-context key retrieval than standard softmax; concurrent theory says log n scaling is necessary to keep sharpness. Model sizes/exact accuracies not retrievable here. | [Nakanishi, 'Scalable-Softmax Is Superior for Attention', arXiv:2501.19399](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19399) | 2025 | moderate |
| Bayesian Attention Mechanism (BAM): positional encoding formulated as a prior in a probabilistic model; unifies NoPE and ALiBi; proposes a Generalized Gaussian positional prior. Reports accurate retrieval at 500x the training context length, >25x higher retrieval accuracy than the previous SOTA in length extrapolation, comparable perplexity, minimal extra parameters; composable with SSMax. Accepted ICLR 2026. | [Bianchessi et al., 'Bayesian Attention Mechanism: A Probabilistic Framework for Positional](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22842) | 2025/2026 | moderate |
| Sparse alpha-entmax attention keeps attention entropy bounded by O(log s) (s = support size) instead of approaching O(log n) as with softmax, giving consistent focus regardless of sequence length (ICLR 2026 'Long-Context Generalization with Sparse Attention'). | ['Long-Context Generalization with Sparse Attention', ICLR 2026, arXiv:2506.16640](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.16640) | 2025/2026 | moderate |
| Attention entropy collapse: pathologically low attention entropy co-occurs with training instability; proved a tight lower bound on attention entropy that decreases exponentially with the spectral norm of the logits. sigma-Reparam (spectral normalisation + learned scalar on all linear layers) prevents collapse and stabilises training across vision/speech/LM. | [Zhai et al., 'Stabilizing Transformer Training by Preventing Attention Entropy Collapse', ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.06296) | 2023 | strong |
| Gated Attention: a head-specific sigmoid gate applied to the SDPA output (headwise or elementwise), query-dependent. Compared 30 variants of 15B MoE and 1.7B dense models trained on 3.5T tokens: consistent perplexity/benchmark gains, higher tolerance to large learning rates, better scaling, mitigates massive activations and attention sink, improves long-context extrapolation. NeurIPS 2025 Oral (77/5290 ~1.5%) and Best Paper; deployed in Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B. (Recalled, not retrieved: first-token attention share fell from ~46.7% to ~4.8%; gates placed on values/queries or before SDPA worked worse.) | [Qiu et al., 'Gated Attention for Large Language Models: Non-linearity, Sparsity, and Atten](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06708) | 2025 | strong |
| Selective Attention (parameter-free mechanism letting tokens reduce attention to unneeded tokens) improved LM perplexity; recalled figures: models behave like they have ~2x heads/parameters and context memory can be cut 16x, 25x, 47x at 512/1024/2048 context for ~100M models. Numbers recalled, not retrieved. | [Leviathan et al., 'Selective Attention Improves Transformer', arXiv:2410.02703](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.02703) | 2024 | moderate |
| Entropy-based Dynamic Temperature (EDT): token-level decoding temperature is a power-law function of the Shannon entropy of the next-token distribution (low entropy -> lower temperature, high entropy -> higher). Reported to outperform fixed-temperature sampling across several generation tasks. Follow-ups: entropy-aware speculative decoding (2025), entropy-informed branching decoding (2026), multi-sample temperature optimisation (2025). | [Zhang, Bao & Huang, 'EDT: Improving LLMs' Generation by Entropy-based Dynamic Temperature ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.14541) | 2024 | moderate |
| Backpropamine (differentiable neuromodulated Hebbian plasticity, dopamine-like modulator M(t) gating the Hebbian trace, plus retroactive eligibility traces): on Penn Treebank a large neuromodulated plastic LSTM reached test perplexity 61.44 vs 62.48 for a standard LSTM with fewer total parameters; retroactive neuromodulation beat non-modulated plastic LSTMs (p=1e-6) and gave ~1.7 perplexity over the LSTM baseline (p=1e-7). Multi-million-parameter model; no follow-up at LLM scale. | [Miconi et al., 'Backpropamine', ICLR 2019, arXiv:2002.10585](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2002.10585) | 2019 | strong |
| ANML: a neuromodulatory network gates the forward pass (and thereby the plasticity) of a prediction network; sequentially learned up to 600 Omniglot classes (>9,000 SGD updates) with state-of-the-art continual-learning accuracy at the time. Small vision setting only. | [Beaulieu et al., 'Learning to Continually Learn' (ANML), ECAI 2020; GitHub uvm-neurobotics](https://github.com/uvm-neurobotics-lab/ANML) | 2020 | moderate |
| NM-RNN: a neuromodulatory subnetwork outputs a low-dimensional signal that dynamically scales the low-rank recurrent weights of an output RNN. Theory shows neuromodulatory gain scaling endows networks with LSTM/GRU-style gating; empirically NM-RNNs train and generalise with higher accuracy than low-rank RNNs on canonical tasks. NeurIPS 2024. Exact numbers not retrievable here. | [Costacurta, Bhandarkar, Zoltowski & Linderman, 'Structured flexibility in recurrent neural](https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2024/hash/03bec5d9f651c1fb89be07a4120238a0-Abstract-Conference.html) | 2024 | moderate |
| Uncertainty-modulated gain dynamics inspired by LC-NE bursts (Aston-Jones & Cohen adaptive gain theory): neuronal gain transiently increased under uncertainty, approximating a two-timescale optimiser; attenuates the continual-learning 'stability gap' on domain- and class-incremental MNIST and CIFAR under joint training. Magnitudes not retrievable here. | [Rodriguez-Garcia, Ghosh & Ramaswamy, 'Noradrenergic-inspired gain modulation attenuates th](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14056) | 2025 | moderate |
| Neuromodulation Gated Transformer (NGT): a gating block computes sigmoid values in [0,1] from a layer's own activations (context) and multiplicatively dampens/enhances them; reported best average SuperGLUE validation score vs baselines at BERT scale. ICLR 2023 Tiny Paper; no numbers on the public README; no follow-up at scale. | [Knowles et al., 'Neuromodulation Gated Transformer', arXiv:2305.03232; GitHub KobeKnowles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.03232) | 2023 | weak |
| Neuromodulated bistable recurrent cell (nBRC) with recurrent neuromodulation was the top performer on tasks with long-term memory requirements; Vecoven et al. 2020 (PLOS ONE) used a neuromodulatory network to adapt an agent across navigation tasks in meta-RL. | [Vecoven et al., 'A bio-inspired bistable recurrent cell allows for long-lasting memory' (P](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0227922) | 2020/2021 | moderate |
| Titans: a neural long-term memory updated at test time by gradient steps on an associative loss, gated by a 'surprise' term (gradient magnitude) with momentum and weight-decay forgetting; reported better perplexity/accuracy than Transformer++ and modern linear RNNs on LM, commonsense, genomics and time-series benchmarks (models up to ~760M; recalled) and retrieval beyond 2M context (recalled). A 2026 arXiv paper ('Surprise as a Signal for Plasticity and Metacognition') continues the surprise-gating theme. | [Behrouz, Zhong & Mirrokni, 'Titans: Learning to Memorize at Test Time', arXiv:2501.00663; ](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00663) | 2025/2026 | moderate |
| Precision-weighted predictive coding approximates natural-gradient descent (local Fisher approximation); hierarchical PCNs with learnable precision matched backprop-with-natural-gradient on supervised/unsupervised tasks and outperformed plain gradient descent when high noise was injected in data or labels. | [Ofner, Millidge & Stober, 'Predictive Coding, Precision and Natural Gradients', arXiv:2111](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.06942) | 2021 | moderate |
| Predictive coding networks match backprop only up to ~5-7 layers; deeper PCNs degrade because errors become exponentially imbalanced across layers and upstream predictions fail to guide deep updates. A precision-weighted optimisation of latent variables that balances error/energy across layers during relaxation, plus a modified weight update, largely restores train/test accuracy beyond 7 layers; residual connections propagate energy faster than the main path. Companion 2025 work addresses scaling PCNs in theory and practice. | ['Towards the Training of Deeper Predictive Coding Neural Networks', arXiv:2506.23800; 'Tow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.23800) | 2025 | moderate |
| Bayesian multisensory integration with explicit divisive normalisation is Bayes-optimal but suffers the curse of dimensionality; a scalable feedforward network reproduces the multisensory empirical principles and approximates Bayesian inference without explicit divisive operations and at lower complexity (J Neurosci 2025). Divisive normalisation is linked to contrast gain control, attentional modulation and near-optimal marginalisation (Beck/Latham/Pouget 2011). | ['Beyond Divisive Normalization: Scalable Feedforward Networks for Multisensory Integration](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12509496/) | 2011 / 2025 | moderate |
| Stochastic/Bayesian attention: Uncertainty-Aware Attention (Heo 2018) models attention weights as Gaussians with input-dependent variance; Bayesian Attention Modules (Fan 2020) treat attention weights as normalised Weibull/lognormal random variables with variational training, giving small gains on GLUE/VQA/NMT at BERT-era scale. Variational attention in seq2seq (Bahuleyan 2018) was bypassed when combined with deterministic attention. | [Fan et al., 'Bayesian Attention Modules', NeurIPS 2020, arXiv:2010.10604; Heo et al. NeurI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.10604) | 2018-2020 | moderate |
| Fly mushroom body models: dopaminergic neurons can implement reward-prediction errors using feedback from MBONs (Bennett, Philippides & Nowotny 2021); models of heterogeneous DAN signalling infer diverse DAN tuning sufficient for tasks under MB architectural constraints (Jiang & Litwin-Kumar 2021); DAN activity in MB compartments depends on physiological state (hunger/thirst; 2025 PMC); DA modulation of MBONs mediates nociception-induced escape (eLife 2025). | [Bennett et al. Nat Commun 2021; Jiang & Litwin-Kumar PLOS CB 2021; 'Systematic analysis of](https://journals.plos.org/ploscompbiol/article?id=10.1371/journal.pcbi.1009205) | 2021-2025 | moderate |
| Recent 2026 perspective work continues to cast precision weighting as the pivot parameter of predictive processing in health and disease (Frontiers Psychology 2026) and neuromodulation as encoding expected reliability of inferences rather than content; no ML scale results. | ['The predictive processing embodied in brain conditions: the role of precision', Frontiers](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2026.1887747/full) | 2026 | weak |

**Usable ideas**

- Implement precision as three separate inferred quantities per Parr & Friston: (a) likelihood/sensory precision as per-head, per-channel log-precision on attention logits with SSMax-style s*log(n) length scaling (learnable s per head); (b) transition/volatility precision as an HGF-style volatility level that sets learning-rate/plasticity gain on state-transition beliefs; (c) policy precision gamma as the decoding temperature, updated by expected free energy and benchmarked against EDT entropy heuristics.
- Add a Gated-Attention-style sigmoid multiplicative gate on each attention head's output (query-dependent, elementwise or headwise); this is the only neuromodulatory gain proven at 1.7B-15B/3.5T-token scale (NeurIPS 2025 best paper, in Qwen3-Next). Extend it so the gate is also a function of inferred precision (attention entropy / surprise), not just the query.
- Use surprise-gated plasticity (Titans) as the mechanism for DAN-gated KC->MBON fast weights: the gate = inferred precision-weighted prediction error (or its HGF learning rate), with momentum and weight-decay forgetting; compare against Backpropamine-style Hebbian modulation (which gave only ~1.7 ppl at PTB scale).
- Realise neuromodulators as a tiny modulatory subnetwork emitting a handful of scalars that scale low-rank recurrent weight components (NM-RNN); theory says this yields LSTM/GRU gating for free and it is parameter-cheap, so connectome-derived low-rank structure can be kept fixed while gains are learned.
- Global 'LC/arousal' gain: one broadcast scalar computed from the magnitude of state prediction error (Sales 2019) or estimated volatility (PNAS 2026) that transiently raises effective step size/plasticity (fast-slow two-timescale optimiser, Rodriguez-Garcia 2025); test explicitly for stability-gap reduction in continual/streaming training.
- Bound precision above during training: apply sigma-Reparam-style spectral constraints or a hyperprior on log-precision so attention entropy cannot collapse (Zhai 2023 entropy lower bound decays exponentially with logit spectral norm); and prevent precision decay with length via log n scaling or alpha-entmax (entropy O(log s) vs O(log n)).
- Learn per-layer diagonal precisions in the predictive-coding filter and balance energy across layers during relaxation (2025 deeper-PCN result); this doubles as a natural-gradient preconditioner (Ofner 2021) and is required for depth >7.
- Represent memory addressing with a probabilistic positional prior (BAM: Generalized Gaussian prior over positions) composed with SSMax; BAM reports retrieval at 500x training context, so a generative prior over 'where to look' is both principled and empirically strong.
- Use the Doya mapping as the minimal neuromodulatory state vector (beta inverse temperature, alpha learning rate, gamma discount/horizon, RPE) and expose each as an ablatable channel wired through connectome-identified modulatory neuron classes (DANs, octopaminergic, serotonergic).
- At inference, add an entropy-monitored sharpen-only adaptive temperature on internal attention (Veličković 2024) as a cheap fallback controller; expect modest gains only, and prefer trained precision over inference-time patches.

**Dead ends**

- Fixed softmax temperature/precision: provably disperses as context length grows regardless of training (Veličković et al. 2024 theorem); inference-time adaptive temperature only partially patches it, so any 'precision' that is a constant per head is a dead end for length generalisation.
- Stochastic/variational attention (Heo 2018, Bahuleyan 2018, Fan 2020 Bayesian Attention Modules): small gains at BERT scale, optimisation difficulties, and the variational path is bypassed when a deterministic attention path coexists; never adopted at LLM scale.
- Differentiable neuromodulated Hebbian plasticity (Backpropamine): +1.7 perplexity on PTB in 2019 with multi-million-param LSTMs, computationally expensive (backprop through lifetimes), no scaling results since; the lineage was effectively superseded by attention and by test-time-training memories (Titans).
- Neuromodulation Gated Transformer (2023 tiny paper): gating from layer context gave only 'best average' on SuperGLUE at BERT scale with no numbers on the public repo and no follow-up; generic activation gating without a principled signal did not scale on its own.
- Predictive coding networks beyond 5-7 layers degrade due to exponentially imbalanced layer errors; even with 2025 precision-balancing fixes there is no language-model-scale PCN result, so a pure PCN backbone remains high-risk.
- Explicit divisive-normalisation Bayesian circuits suffer the curse of dimensionality (J Neurosci 2025); hard-coded normalisation modules do not scale to real-world input sizes and are outperformed by learned feedforward approximations.
- Neuromodulator-as-RL-hyperparameter systems (Doya mapping) have only been validated on bandits and small navigation/meta-RL tasks (2020-2024); no sequence-model or LM demonstration.
- ANML/OML-style neuromodulatory gating for continual learning: strong at 600 Omniglot classes but never shown on language; and even LC-inspired gain (2025) only attenuates, does not remove, the stability gap on MNIST/CIFAR.
- Unbounded precision during training leads to attention entropy collapse and divergence (Zhai 2023); learning free log-precision scalars without a norm constraint or hyperprior is known to be unstable.
- Dopamine-as-precision (active inference) rests on discrete-state simulations and one fMRI study; it has not been realised in any trained ML model with baselines, and behaviourally it is nearly indistinguishable from RPE accounts (FitzGerald 2015), so it should not be assumed as a design axiom without an explicit ablation.

**Open gaps**

- No published trained language model (>100M parameters) implements active-inference precision weighting (zeta/omega/gamma) with ablations and standard baselines; the theory side (Beyond Markov 2025) offers only a conceptual mapping.
- No work makes the Gated-Attention sigmoid gate a function of inferred uncertainty (attention entropy, surprise, volatility) rather than the query alone; whether an uncertainty-driven gate improves on the query-driven gate at scale is untested.
- No study of a two-level precision hierarchy in transformers: a global broadcast gain (LC-like) interacting with local per-head precisions, or of learned volatility (HGF-style) setting attention temperature online during training rather than at inference.
- No comparison of the three length-aware precision mechanisms (SSMax log n scaling, alpha-entmax bounded entropy, BAM positional prior) under a shared precision interpretation, nor calibration analyses testing whether attention entropy actually tracks predictive uncertainty.
- Dopamine-as-precision vs dopamine-as-RPE has never been tested in a connectome-constrained mushroom-body model with identical wiring; MaleCNS DAN->KC/MBON structure makes this a feasible, novel experiment.
- Precision learning in predictive coding networks has been validated only on image classification up to ~10-20 layers; no sequence/autoregressive language results, and no analysis of interaction between learned precision and length generalisation.
- Surprise-gated test-time memory (Titans) uses raw gradient norm as surprise; whether a properly precision-weighted (variance-normalised) surprise improves memorisation/forgetting trade-offs is unexplored.
- No quantitative head-to-head between EFE-based policy precision (gamma) and entropy-heuristic decoding temperature (EDT) on LLM generation quality, diversity and calibration.
- The mapping of distinct fly neuromodulator classes (dopamine, octopamine, serotonin, tyramine) to distinct precision types has no ML instantiation; existing 'multi-neuromodulator' ML papers (2024-2026) remain reviews or bandit-scale.
- Stability-gap attenuation via gain modulation is shown only on MNIST/CIFAR under joint training; its behaviour in streaming language pretraining with distribution shift is unknown.

**Key numbers**

- Backpropamine (ICLR 2019): Penn Treebank test perplexity 61.44 (neuromodulated plastic LSTM) vs 62.48 (LSTM, more parameters); retroactive neuromodulation ~1.7 ppl better than baseline (p=1e-7) and better than non-modulated plastic LSTM (p=1e-6).
- Gated Attention (NeurIPS 2025 best paper): 30 variants of 15B-parameter MoE and 1.7B dense models trained on 3.5T tokens; oral = top 1.5% (77 of 5,290 accepted); deployed in Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B. Recalled (unverified this session): first-token attention-sink share ~46.7% -> ~4.8%.
- BAM (ICLR 2026): accurate retrieval at 500x training context length; >25x higher retrieval accuracy than prior SOTA at comparable perplexity, minimal extra parameters.
- SSMax: attention logits scaled by s*log(n), s learnable per head; theory: log n scaling is necessary to preserve sharpness as n grows.
- alpha-entmax attention: entropy bounded by O(log s) versus softmax entropy approaching O(log n).
- Softmax dispersion theorem (Veličković 2024): for bounded logits, the maximum attention coefficient -> 0 as item count n -> infinity, for any trained circuit.
- Attention entropy lower bound (Zhai 2023) decreases exponentially with the spectral norm of attention logits.
- ANML: 600 Omniglot classes learned sequentially over >9,000 SGD updates without catastrophic forgetting.
- Predictive coding networks: performance matches backprop only to ~5-7 layers; precision-weighted latent optimisation restores accuracy for >7 layers (2025).
- Doya 2002: 4 neuromodulators <-> 4 RL meta-parameters (DA=TD error, 5-HT=discount gamma, NA=inverse temperature beta, ACh=learning rate alpha).
- Parr & Friston 2017: three precision parameters zeta (sensory), omega (transition), gamma (policy).
- Selective Attention (recalled): behaves like ~2x heads/params; context memory reduced 16x/25x/47x at 512/1024/2048 context for ~100M models.
- Uncertainty-modulated gain (2025): tested on domain- and class-incremental MNIST and CIFAR only; attenuates (does not eliminate) the stability gap.
- Web retrieval note: 26 searches run; only github.com was fetchable, all academic mirrors blocked, so several exact magnitudes (SSMax, NM-RNN, EDT, Gated Attention PPL deltas) could not be verified.

## lit:criticality

43 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Real-time computation at the edge of chaos: in random threshold-unit recurrent networks with Gaussian weights (mean mu, variance sigma^2) there is an order/chaos phase transition line, and performance of a linear readout on temporal tasks (e.g. delayed parity) peaks on that critical line; ordered phase forgets input too fast, chaotic phase amplifies noise. | [Bertschinger & Natschlaeger, Neural Computation 16(7):1413-1436](https://doi.org/10.1162/089976604323057443) | 2004 | strong |
| Edge of chaos is NOT a universal optimum: for spiking liquid-state machines the critical line predicts performance for some tasks but not others; a separation/generalization-based measure predicts better. Task-dependence of the optimal distance to criticality is a recurring theme. | [Legenstein & Maass, Neural Networks 20(3):323-334](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neunet.2007.04.017) | 2007 | strong |
| In input-driven echo state networks, memory capacity, active information storage and transfer entropy are all maximized close to the edge of chaos, operationalized as largest Lyapunov exponent lambda_max ~ 0 (the effective ESN spectral radius near 1 under input), with the exact optimum slightly task/measure dependent. | [Boedecker, Obst, Lizier, Mayer, Asada, Theory in Biosciences 131:205-213](https://doi.org/10.1007/s12064-011-0146-8) | 2012 | strong |
| Linear memory capacity of an N-unit reservoir is bounded by N (MC <= N); it is approached only for near-linear, near-orthogonal/delay-line (non-normal) reservoirs as spectral radius -> 1. Nonlinear (tanh) random reservoirs realize a small fraction of N. Non-normal networks (delay-line-like) achieve extensive memory duration ~N while normal networks are limited; Fisher memory curve formalism. | [Jaeger 2002 (GMD Report 152); White, Lee & Sompolinsky PRL 92:148102 (2004); Ganguli, Huh ](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.0804451105) | 2002-2011 | strong |
| Random rate networks x' = -x + g J tanh(x) with J_ij ~ N(0,1/N) transition from a fixed point to chaos at g = 1; largest Lyapunov exponent grows continuously from 0 at g=1; attractor dimension and entropy rate are extensive (scale with N) and increase with g. FORCE learning works best with a pre-training gain g ~ 1.5 (mildly chaotic), which training then suppresses. | [Sompolinsky, Crisanti & Sommers PRL 61:259 (1988); Sussillo & Abbott Neuron 63:544 (2009);](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevResearch.5.043044) | 1988/2009/2023 | strong |
| Deep information propagation: in mean-field theory of deep tanh MLPs the correlation depth scale xi_c diverges on the critical line (chi_1 = 1 in the (sigma_w, sigma_b) plane); networks are trainable only when depth is less than roughly 6*xi_c; dropout eliminates the critical point (no order-to-chaos line), capping trainable depth. | [Schoenholz, Gilmer, Ganguli & Sohl-Dickstein, ICLR 2017 (arXiv:1611.01232); Poole et al. N](https://arxiv.org/abs/1611.01232) | 2017 | strong |
| Dynamical isometry (all Jacobian singular values ~1) is achievable with orthogonal weights + tanh at criticality but not with ReLU/Gaussian; enables training 10,000-layer tanh CNNs with delta-orthogonal init. ResNets are 'always at the edge of chaos' (polynomial rather than exponential signal growth), which is why they train at almost any init; smooth activations (ELU/tanh) at the edge of chaos train deeper than ReLU. | [Pennington, Schoenholz & Ganguli NeurIPS 2017; Xiao et al. ICML 2018; Yang & Schoenholz Ne](https://arxiv.org/abs/1806.05393) | 2017-2019 | strong |
| Mean-field theory of RNNs: vanilla RNNs cannot reach dynamical isometry over long time horizons; gating (minimalRNN/GRU/LSTM) enables signal propagation and trainability over ~1000 steps. Gated RNNs possess a continuum of marginally stable slow modes (a 'line of criticality' rather than a point), created by the update gate. | [Chen, Pennington & Schoenholz ICML 2018 (arXiv:1806.05394); Gilboa et al. 2019 (arXiv:1901](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevX.12.011011) | 2018-2022 | strong |
| Gradient flossing: pushing (a subset of) the forward-pass Lyapunov exponents of an RNN toward zero, before or during training (differentiating through a QR-based Lyapunov computation), improves trainability on long-horizon tasks (repo example: delayed XOR with 80 units, delays 10-55 steps, 3,001 epochs); complements orthogonal init and gradient clipping. | [Engelken, NeurIPS 2023 'Gradient Flossing: Improving Gradient Descent through Dynamic Cont](https://github.com/RainerEngelken/GradientFlossing) | 2023 | strong |
| Training on chaotic targets necessarily produces exploding gradients (BPTT Jacobian products grow ~exp(lambda_max T)); Generalized Teacher Forcing (GTF) with an alpha chosen from the Jacobian norm yields 'provably strictly all-time bounded gradients', with an adaptive-alpha (aGTF) variant; bifurcations during training cause discrete loss jumps. | [Mikhaeil, Monfared & Durstewitz NeurIPS 2022; Hess, Monfared, Brenner & Durstewitz ICML 20](https://github.com/DurstewitzLab/GTF-shPLRNN) | 2022-2023 | strong |
| Early 'criticality regularizer' for RNNs: add a penalty keeping the norm of the backpropagated gradient through each step near 1 (//dC/dh_{t+1} * dh_{t+1}/dh_t// / //dC/dh_{t+1}// ~ 1), plus norm clipping; solved long-dependency tasks that plain SGD failed. | [Pascanu, Mikolov & Bengio, ICML 2013 (arXiv:1211.5063)](https://arxiv.org/abs/1211.5063) | 2013 | strong |
| Lyapunov spectra of trained LSTM/GRU networks can be computed by QR iteration on the recurrent Jacobian; spectral features (largest exponent near zero, mean/variance of spectrum) correlate with task performance and generalization, offering a training-free diagnostic. | [Vogt, Puelma Touzel, Shlizerman & Lajoie, Frontiers in Applied Mathematics and Statistics ](https://doi.org/10.3389/fams.2022.818799) | 2022 | moderate |
| Balanced excitation-inhibition networks: with synaptic strengths scaling 1/sqrt(K) (K inputs per neuron) and strong E/I, the network self-organizes to a balanced state with chaotic, irregular activity, linear population response and very fast tracking, without fine-tuning of parameters. | [van Vreeswijk & Sompolinsky, Science 274:1724 (1996); Neural Computation 10:1321 (1998)](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.274.5293.1724) | 1996 | strong |
| Random-matrix theory of E/I (Dale) connectivity: the bulk eigenvalue radius is sqrt(N(f sigma_E^2 + (1-f) sigma_I^2)) for mean-subtracted columns; non-zero E/I column means produce an outlier eigenvalue at ~N(f mu_E - (1-f) mu_I) that dominates stability. Sparse E/I networks show a transition to chaos analogous to g=1; local reciprocal/chain motifs shift these eigenvalues. | [Rajan & Abbott PRL 97:188104 (2006); Kadmon & Sompolinsky Phys. Rev. X 5:041030 (2015); Ma](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRevLett.97.188104) | 2006-2023 | strong |
| Dale-constrained RNNs can be trained with BPTT by parameterizing W = /W/ * D (D = +-1 diagonal), e.g. 80% E / 20% I, on cognitive tasks with performance comparable to unconstrained networks; E/I structure enables analyses of inhibitory roles. | [Song, Yang & Wang, PLoS Computational Biology 12:e1004792](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pcbi.1004792) | 2016 | strong |
| Naively sign-constrained deep networks (Dale's principle) underperform unconstrained ones; 'ColumnEI' (inhibitory units acting through a column/subtractive-divisive structure) closes the gap on MNIST/CIFAR-scale tasks. Follow-up: exponentiated-gradient (EG) updates natively preserve weight signs, yield log-normal weight distributions, and 'in biologically relevant settings EG outperforms GD, including learning from sparsely relevant signals and dealing with synaptic pruning'. | [Cornford et al. ICLR 2021 'Learning to live with Dale's principle'; Cornford et al. bioRxi](https://github.com/linclab/EG_optimiser) | 2021/2024 | strong |
| Stability-optimized circuits: strongly coupled excitatory networks (unstable alone) are stabilized by tuning inhibitory connections so the spectral abscissa becomes negative while retaining strong non-normal transient amplification ('balanced amplification'); training dynamically balanced E/I networks (FORCE) preserves the balanced state. | [Hennequin, Vogels & Gerstner Neuron 82:1394 (2014); Murphy & Miller Neuron 61:635 (2009); ](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2014.04.045) | 2009-2019 | strong |
| Tight E/I balance is mathematically equivalent to predictive/efficient coding: in spike-coding networks the membrane potential equals a prediction error and balance is the condition that error is minimized; learning rules that drive networks to balance exist. | [Boerlin, Machens & Deneve PLoS CB 9:e1003258 (2013); Deneve & Machens Nature Neuroscience ](https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.4243) | 2013-2020 | strong |
| Self-organizing recurrent networks (SORN): STDP + synaptic normalization + intrinsic (homeostatic) plasticity in a binary E/I reservoir (N_E=200, N_I=40 in the original) outperform static reservoirs on sequence tasks; later work shows SORN produces power-law neuronal avalanches, and 'fading memory is improved at the noise level that also results in power-law distributed neuronal avalanches' (README of delpapa/SORN_V2, verified). | [Lazar, Pipa & Triesch Frontiers Comp. Neurosci. 3:23 (2009); Del Papa, Priesemann & Triesc](https://github.com/delpapa/SORN_V2) | 2009/2017 | moderate |
| Homeostatic plasticity does not tune networks exactly to criticality: with external input the homeostatic fixed point is subcritical, with distance to criticality set by input rate (branching parameter m ranging ~0.9-0.999 as input decreases). In vivo cortex is measured in a 'reverberating' regime m ~ 0.98 (about 2% below critical) with intrinsic timescales of order 100-300 ms. | [Zierenberg, Wilting & Priesemann Phys. Rev. X 8:031018 (2018); Wilting & Priesemann Nature](https://github.com/Priesemann-Group/mrestimator) | 2018 | strong |
| On a neuromorphic spiking network with homeostatic plasticity, distance from criticality was controlled by input strength; performance on tasks requiring long memory (high-order n-bit parity/sum) peaked at criticality, while simpler tasks were solved better in the subcritical regime - criticality is optimal only for memory-heavy computation. | [Cramer et al., Nature Communications 11:2853 'Control of criticality and computation in sp](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-020-16548-3) | 2020 | moderate |
| Cortical circuits in vivo are dynamically tuned to criticality by homeostasis: after monocular deprivation, criticality signatures are restored within ~2 days, before firing rates recover (~4-5 days), suggesting criticality (not rate) is the homeostatic set-point. Signal-propagation analysis (Zucchet & Orvieto 2024) shows the problem is the derivative w.r.t. the eigenvalue, not the recurrent Jacobian per se, and that diagonal + normalization + reparameterization mitigate it. | [Li, Han, E & Li 2022 (arXiv:2205.04794); Wang & Li ICLR 2024 'StableSSM' (arXiv:2311.14495](https://github.com/radarFudan/Curse-of-memory) | 2022-2024 | strong |

**Usable ideas**

- Balance before scaling: for the MaleCNS signed matrix, subtract/scale so E and I column means cancel (Rajan-Abbott), then rescale to a target bulk spectral radius of ~0.95-0.98 (biological reverberating regime m ~ 0.98), not 1.0; verify no outlier eigenvalue remains.
- Use branching ratio m (mrestimator-style multistep regression), largest Lyapunov exponent under input drive (QR method), and linear memory capacity as the three pre-registered criticality diagnostics; expect trained cores to sit at lambda_max ~ 0 or slightly negative.
- Lyapunov control of the E/I core: (a) gradient-flossing pre-training that pushes the top-k Lyapunov exponents to zero, (b) a cheap per-step Pascanu-style Jacobian-norm regularizer as the in-training proxy, (c) GTF-style adaptive teacher forcing if the core is allowed to be mildly chaotic.
- Never learn a spectral radius with a raw parameter: use the LRU/StableSSM maps (|lambda| = exp(-exp(nu)), input normalization sqrt(1-|lambda|^2), ring init |lambda| in [0.9, 0.999]) for any diagonal slow-memory pathway; allow negative and complex eigenvalues (needed for parity/state tracking).
- Separate two pathways with different stability targets: a near-critical memory pathway (radius ~0.98, non-normal transients from connectome motifs) and a contractive inference/iteration pathway (recurrent-depth belief updates, radius clearly < 1, input re-injected each step, 1/sqrt(L) or 1/L residual scaling).
- Implement the predictive-coding/precision layer as a tightly balanced E/I circuit (Deneve-Machens): inhibition predicts excitation, the residual imbalance is the precision-weighted prediction error, and stability comes from tuning inhibitory weights only (stability-optimized circuits) rather than from shrinking the E connectome.
- Make distance-to-criticality a controlled variable tied to precision: strong/high-precision drive pushes the core subcritical (fast, low-memory), weak drive lets it approach criticality (long memory), mirroring Zierenberg 2018 / Cramer 2020; expose this as the homeostatic set-point.
- Homeostatic normalization stack for the ML side: RMSNorm or DyT with width-scheduled gain (alpha0 0.8 -> 0.2 as width grows), QK-norm plus attention-logit cap (soft-cap 15-30 or hard QK-clip at 100), z-loss 1e-4, spectral-norm reparameterization (sigma-Reparam) or Muon-style orthogonalized updates on all projections including the recurrent matrix.
- Use multiplicative gating (neuromodulatory gain / shunting) rather than fine-tuning g to obtain marginal stability over a region (gated-RNN theory), which is robust to connectome weight noise.
- Add SORN-style per-neuron intrinsic plasticity (target-rate gain adaptation) and synaptic normalization to the biological core; these self-tune toward the near-critical regime and improved fading memory in prior small-scale work.
- Data curriculum: pretraining on structured-but-not-chaotic sequences (Wolfram class IV / intermediate complexity) improved downstream reasoning transfer for GPT-2-scale models; consider a complexity-graded warm-up corpus.
- Evaluate temperature/precision of the EFE decoder by measuring the correlation length of generated text vs temperature (phase-transition diagnostic) and keep the operating point near but below the critical temperature.
- Report effect sizes against degree-preserving nulls at matched spectral radius (conn2res protocol); prior connectome-reservoir gains over nulls were modest and concentrated near alpha ~ 1.

**Dead ends**

- Exactly-critical initialization as a universal recipe: performance at the edge of chaos is task-dependent (Legenstein & Maass 2007; Cramer 2020), and vanilla RNNs cannot achieve dynamical isometry over long horizons even at criticality without gating (Chen et al. 2018).
- Pushing eigenvalues of a raw-parameterized memory pathway toward 1 to get long memory: gradients w.r.t. eigenvalues explode (curse of memory); vanilla RNN, diagonal RNN, S4-style SSM and LRU all fail polynomial-decay memory without a stable reparameterization (Wang & Li 2024).
- Positive-real-only eigenvalue ranges [0,1] (default Mamba/S4D/DeltaNet): provably cannot solve parity or track state; must include negative/complex eigenvalues.
- Naively sign-constrained (Dale) deep networks with plain SGD/Adam: consistently underperform unconstrained ones (Cornford et al. 2021); needs ColumnEI-like inhibitory architecture or exponentiated-gradient updates.
- Unbalanced signed connectome matrices: non-zero E/I column means create a large outlier eigenvalue that dominates dynamics regardless of the bulk spectral radius (Rajan & Abbott 2006) - scaling the whole matrix to radius 1 does not fix this.
- Dot-product attention without gain control: non-Lipschitz (Kim et al. 2021); attention-logit growth and entropy collapse cause loss spikes at scale (Wortsman 2023; Zhai 2023) - fixed only by QK-norm, spectral reparameterization or QK-clip.
- Pure attention stacks without skip/MLP: rank collapse doubly exponential in depth (Dong et al. 2021); Post-LN transformers beyond ~ a few dozen layers without DeepNorm-type scaling; FC nets deeper than ~6 correlation lengths (Schoenholz 2017).
- Dropout in the recurrent/deep core: removes the critical point and caps trainable depth (Schoenholz 2017).
- Plain BPTT on chaotic targets: exploding gradients are unavoidable (Mikhaeil et al. 2022); only sparse/generalized teacher forcing bounds them.
- Expecting homeostatic plasticity to land exactly at criticality: with input it settles subcritical at an input-dependent distance (Zierenberg 2018); biology measures m ~ 0.98, not 1.
- Binary/spiking reservoirs at the edge of chaos: abrupt memory loss and high sensitivity to connectivity (Buesing 2010) - use rate units for the LM core.
- Gradient flossing / Lyapunov-spectrum regularization has only been demonstrated on ~10-100-unit RNNs with CPU-scale compute; there is no evidence it scales to 10^4-10^5-unit connectome cores or to transformers/SSMs.
- Hobby-scale 'edge of chaos' LLM/LSTM repos (e.g., FTLE-vs-generalization for LSTMs, 2026) report no results; do not cite as evidence.

**Open gaps**

- No published measurement of criticality (spectral radius after E/I balancing, branching ratio, Lyapunov spectrum, memory capacity) for adult Drosophila connectome-derived dynamics (MaleCNS or FlyWire); the only whole-connectome reservoirs are unpublished larval-connectome repos without null models.
- No Dale-constrained (E/I) recurrent core has been trained inside a language model at >100M parameters; Dale's law in attention/SSM layers is untested, and ColumnEI/EG fixes were validated only on MNIST/CIFAR-scale tasks.
- No systematic sweep of spectral radius / distance-to-criticality vs language-modeling perplexity for modern recurrent LMs; the optimal distance for language (memory-heavy but structured) is unknown.
- Lyapunov exponents of the residual stream / recurrent-depth iteration in trained LLMs during training are not characterized; the relation between optimizer-level edge of stability (sharpness 2/eta, 38/eta) and dynamical edge of chaos is unstudied.
- The E/I-balance = predictive-coding equivalence (Deneve-Machens) has no implementation at LM scale, and no one has tied precision (temperature) in active inference to the effective gain/criticality of a balanced E/I core.
- Cost/scaling of Lyapunov-spectrum regularization (QR per step) at connectome scale is unquantified; no low-rank or stochastic estimators have been benchmarked for this purpose.
- Contribution of non-normal (feedforward-chain) memory from connectome motifs vs normal-network memory near criticality has not been quantified for any insect connectome.
- Homeostatic normalization in transformers (LN/RMSNorm/DyT/QK-norm/logit caps) lacks a unifying theory linking it to criticality; the DyT width-dependent gain schedule (0.8 -> 0.2) has no recurrent/E-I analogue.
- Data-criticality result (edge-of-chaos ECA pretraining helps transfer) has not been replicated beyond GPT-2 scale or connected to network-level criticality measures.
- Whether the Kimi-K2 style hard logit cap (tau = 100) or sigma-Reparam is the better analogue of synaptic scaling for a recurrent E/I matrix is untested; interactions between spectral-norm-constrained updates (Muon) and Dale sign constraints are unexplored.

**Key numbers**

- Random tanh network chaos threshold: gain g = 1 (Sompolinsky 1988); FORCE works best from g ~ 1.5 pre-training (Sussillo & Abbott 2009).
- Linear memory capacity bound MC <= N; approached only for near-linear, orthogonal/delay-line reservoirs with spectral radius -> 1.
- In-vivo cortical branching parameter m ~ 0.98 (~2% subcritical), intrinsic timescale ~100-300 ms (Wilting & Priesemann 2018); homeostatic fixed points span m ~ 0.9-0.999 depending on input (Zierenberg 2018).
- Criticality restored ~2 days after monocular deprivation vs ~4-5 days for firing rates (Ma et al. 2019).
- Mean-field trainable depth ~ 6 * correlation length xi_c; xi_c diverges on the critical line; 10,000-layer tanh CNNs trainable with delta-orthogonal init (Xiao 2018).
- Edge of stability: sharpness -> 2/eta (GD); preconditioned sharpness -> 38/eta (Adam).
- E/I random matrix: bulk radius sqrt(N(f sigma_E^2 + (1-f) sigma_I^2)); outlier at ~N(f mu_E - (1-f) mu_I) if unbalanced; typical Dale RNN split 80% E / 20% I (Song 2016); SORN N_E = 200, N_I = 40.
- Gradient flossing repo example: 80 recurrent units, delayed XOR delay 10-55 steps, 3,001 epochs, minutes-to-hours on one CPU.
- LRU: lambda = exp(-exp(nu) + i theta), gamma = sqrt(1-|lambda|^2); minimal-LRU defaults r_min = 0.0, r_max = 1.0, max_phase = 6.28; paper long-range settings |lambda| in [0.9, 0.999] (recalled).
- Negative-eigenvalue fix: eigenvalue range [0,1] -> [-1,1] enables parity in Mamba/DeltaNet at zero extra cost.
- DyT alpha0 for LLaMA: 7B attn 0.8 / FFN 0.2; 13B 0.6 / 0.15; 34B 0.2 / 0.05; 70B 0.2 / 0.05; ViT-B 82.3 -> 82.5, ViT-L 83.1 -> 83.6 top-1.
- Muon: Newton-Schulz 5 steps, singular values mapped into ~0.68-1.13, <2% wall-clock overhead, ~1.5x sample efficiency vs Adam; CIFAR-10 94% in 2.6 vs 3.3 A100-s; GPT-2 small 1.35x; speedrun 124M to val loss 3.28: 45 min -> 1.23 min on 8xH100 with ~400M tokens; logit soft-cap 30 -> 15.
- Moonlight: 3B/16B MoE, 5.7T tokens, ~2x compute efficiency (~52% of AdamW FLOPs); Kimi K2: 1T total / 32B active with MuonClip (QK-clip tau = 100; 15.5T tokens, zero loss spikes - recalled).
- Wortsman 2023: QK-LN + z-loss 1e-4 flatten LR sensitivity over ~3 orders of magnitude, models 10M-1.2B.
- LipsFormer-Swin-Tiny: 82.70% ImageNet top-1 with no warmup; Lipschitz-constrained NanoGPT: 145M params with fully constrained weights competitive; unconstrained baseline max activations ~1.5e5 vs ~100 constrained (recalled).
- nGPT: ~4x fewer steps at 1k context (~200k steps), ~10x at 4k context (reimplementation, with caveats).
- DeepNorm: Post-LN transformers to 1,000 layers; CompleteP (alpha = 1 depth scaling): 12-34% compute savings (recalled).
- Huginn recurrent-depth LM: 3.5B params, mean recurrence 32, trained on 4096 AMD GPUs (Frontier).
- Edge-of-chaos ECA pretraining: GPT-2 tiny (1L/1H/64d), small (12L/12H/768d), large (36L/20H/1280d); downstream ARC, chess, NIM.
- Larval-connectome reservoir (unpublished): 2,952 neurons, ~110k synapses, ~94% MNIST with ridge readout, no null models.

| Antennal-lobe divisive normalisation (Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson 2010): PN_i = Rmax * ORN_i^n / (sigma + m*sum_j ORN_j)^n with n ~ 1.5; the divisor pools total ORN activity across glomeruli; total ORN drive spans 57x over four concentration decades while total PN drive is compressed to 6.6x; mechanism is GABA-B presynaptic inhibition of ORN terminals scaled to total ORN activity (Olsen & Wilson 2008). Same computation recurs at the KC layer via APL/GGN. | [Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson 2010 Neuron 66:287 (summarised in stootoon/circuits-to-algorithm](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2010.04.009) | 2010 | strong |

Subtractive normalisation gives flat means only in narrow parameter windows and no response diversity; intraglomerular gain control gives neither. DN produces diverse non-monotonic second-order curves only when first-order responses show rank cross-overs. Primacy sets stay stable (Jaccard > 0.96) but 70 % of top-10 neuron ranks change between low and high concentration. | [Shen et al. 2025, eLife reviewed preprint 108236, 'An evolutionarily conserved scheme for ](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/108236) | 2025 | moderate |
