| Slow adaptation in the AL is presynaptic depression, not lateral inhibition: PN calcium responses adapt to ~50 % with timescales 3-40 s (mean degree of adaptation 0.57 for PNs vs -0.06 +/- 0.1 for ORNs); vesicle-release depression tau ~ 10 s (fast component 300-400 ms); 5 uM picrotoxin and GABA-B manipulations do not change PN adaptation timescales. Divisive normalisation and slow depression are described as complementary gain mechanisms; PN population representation scales linearly without changing identity. | [Martelli & Fiala 2019, eLife 43735, 'Slow presynaptic mechanisms that mediate adaptation i](https://elifesciences.org/articles/43735) | 2019 | strong |
| Hemibrain olfactory connectome: 196 antennal-lobe local neurons in one hemisphere (5 lineages, 4 morphological classes, 25 groups, 74 cell types); 188 AL projection-neuron types; ~60 % (1,428) of third-order olfactory neurons have LH dendrites, divided into 496 near-isomorphic LH cell types; within-cell-type up/downstream connectivity cosine similarity ~0.75; ALPN->LHN connection variability between datasets (FAFB vs hemibrain) is no greater than within one dataset; within- vs across-animal morphology scores differ statistically but with an extremely small effect size. | [Schlegel et al. 2021, eLife 66018, 'Information flow, cell types and stereotypy in a full ](https://elifesciences.org/articles/66018) | 2021 | strong |
| Inhibitory LN weights are strongly glomerulus-specific: lLN2F_b makes ~953 synapses onto Or42a OSNs (VM7) but only ~133 onto Or43b OSNs (VM2), a ~7-fold asymmetry (FlyWire); narrowly tuned glomeruli (DA2, VA1v) have stronger OSN->uPN/MGN feedforward, 4x stronger OSN-OSN reciprocal connections (6 % vs 1.5 %), weaker uPN->MGN feedback and reduced MGN->OSN feedback inhibition than broadly tuned DL5 (DA2: 44 OSNs, 7 uPNs, ~180 MGNs, ~14,000 contacts; DL5: 46 OSNs, 1 uPN, ~221 MGNs, ~17,000 contacts). | [Leier et al. 2025 eLife reviewed preprint 108529; Gruber et al. 2023 eLife reviewed prepri](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/108529) | 2023-2025 | strong |
| Lateral horn genetic dissection: 2,444 split-GAL4 lines screened -> 82 LH cell types (~38 % of ~165 core types); 44 LH output neuron types (26 cholinergic, 13 GABAergic, 9 glutamatergic), 8 local, 14 input types; 3 types co-release two fast transmitters; optogenetic screen of 89 lines: 12 lines drove aversion (6 cell types), 3 approach, 1 forward locomotion; only ~15 % of lines gave valence phenotypes; ~30 % of LHONs overlap MB (DAN dendrites or MBON axons), 16 % have >15 % overlap with MBONs, 70 % of LHON axons no overlap; AD1b2 LHONs integrate PN input, dendritic MBON input and axo-axonic MBON input; PN->LH connectivity is stereotyped across animals. | [Dolan et al. 2019, eLife 43079, 'Neurogenetic dissection of the Drosophila lateral horn...](https://elifesciences.org/articles/43079) | 2019 | strong |
| LH physiology: 410 cells recorded (242 LHONs, 84 LHLNs, 84 PNs); LHONs are ~10x quieter at baseline, respond to ~3x more odours (35 % of odour-cell pairs vs 12 % for PNs) with lower evoked rates (14 Hz vs 21 Hz); ~70 % of category-informative cells are LHONs; LHONs (and LHLNs less so) but not PNs convey odour-category information; 261 LH anatomical types, 37 cross-validated functionally; genetically labelled LHNs have stereotyped odour responses across animals (unlike KCs). | [Frechter et al. 2019, eLife 44590, 'Functional and anatomical specificity in a higher olfa](https://elifesciences.org/articles/44590) | 2019 | strong |
| Glutamatergic LH neurons cluster odours by valence (within- vs across-valence similarity, p < 0.001) and their response amplitude correlates with behavioural preference (Spearman p = 0.01), whereas uniglomerular PNs do not; odour identity is better segregated at the uPN level; inhibitory multiglomerular PN input (mACT) is required to decorrelate LHN responses to different odours (p < 0.001). Causal silencing was not performed. | [Das Chakraborty, Chang, Hansson & Sachse 2022, eLife 74637](https://elifesciences.org/articles/74637) | 2022 | moderate |
| CO2 innate aversion uses an extraglomerular labelled line: ~40 Gr21a/Gr63a ORNs -> local interneuron LN23 -> PNm1 (GABAergic, 27 % of its input from LN23, drives aversion) and PNm17 (18 % from LN23, drives attraction, also receives humidity/temperature input); silencing LN23 abolishes innate CO2 aversion; this pathway is largely unaffected by glomerular-circuit plasticity during habituation; glomerular CO2 pathway reaches LH domain PD5 via three glutamatergic LH neurons. | [Javorski et al. 2026, eLife reviewed preprint 110832, 'Structural basis of CO2 valence cod](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/110832) | 2026 | moderate |
| Habituation is implemented as inhibition of LH-driven innate avoidance rather than erasure; dishabituation reinstates the naive response through the MB: homosensory dishabituation (novel odour) requires PAM DANs and APL GABAergic input, heterosensory dishabituation (single 45 V footshock) requires PPL1 DANs projecting to gamma KCs; novelty rather than opposing valence determines efficacy. | [Charonitakis et al. 2025, eLife reviewed preprint 106322](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/106322) | 2025 | moderate |
| Adult MB connectome (hemibrain): 1,927 KCs in 14 subtypes (701 gamma, 337 alpha'/beta', 889 alpha/beta); 34 MBON types incl. 14 newly found 'atypical' MBONs with dendrites inside and outside the lobes; 158 DANs (PPL1: 6 types x 1 cell; PAM: 15 types with 3-26 cells; PPL201 to calyx); one APL, one DPM; 15 compartments; ~150 PNs give olfactory input, 129 uniglomerular olfactory PNs = 63.6 % of KC input; ~8 % of KCs receive predominantly visual input; thermo (35 multi + 19 uniglomerular PNs) and gustatory KC inputs exist; 'extensive direct feedback from MBONs to the dendrites of DANs' within and between compartments (analysed with a 5-synapse threshold). | [Li et al. 2020, eLife 62576, 'The connectome of the adult Drosophila mushroom body provide](https://elifesciences.org/articles/62576) | 2020 | strong |
| Incentive-circuit model: 6 MBONs + 6 DANs in 6 microcircuits with an explicit MBON->DAN map (e.g. MBON-gamma1pedc inhibits PPL1-gamma1pedc; MBON-gamma4>gamma1gamma2 inhibits PAM-gamma4; MBON-gamma2alpha'1 -> PAM-beta'2a; MBON-alpha'1 -> PPL1-gamma2alpha'1 and PAM-beta2beta'2a); dopaminergic plasticity rule dW = delta(t)[k(t) + W - w_rest] (w_rest = 1) yields depression, potentiation, recovery and saturation with only presynaptic/local information; reproduces 92 intervention experiments with r = 0.76 (p = 2.2e-18), extinction, reversal, second-order and unpaired conditioning, and correctly predicts absence of blocking (no cross-odour error). | [Gkanias, McCurdy, Nitabach & Webb 2022, eLife 75611, 'An incentive circuit for memory dyna](https://elifesciences.org/articles/75611) | 2022 | moderate |
| Hierarchical DAN architecture: 16 matched compartments, 20 DAN types; MBON-alpha1 is the only MBON with no direct output to DANs of other compartments; instead a cholinergic interneuron SMP108 (2,416 of 2,753 presynapses predicted cholinergic; the cholinergic neuron with the most connections to reward DANs) relays MBON-alpha1 (via SMP353/354, SMP108) to PAM-alpha3, beta'2a, beta'2m, beta'2p; SMP108 acquires an enhanced response to the reward-predicting odour after first-order conditioning and evokes dopamine release in 'student' compartments; PAM-alpha1 and PAM-alpha3/beta'2a activation can induce second-order memory. | [Yamada et al. 2023, eLife 79042, 'Hierarchical architecture of dopaminergic circuits enabl](https://elifesciences.org/articles/79042) | 2023 | strong |
| Positive-feedback reward loop: glutamatergic MBON-alpha1 synapses onto PAM-alpha1 dendrites (NMDAR required in PAM-alpha1); blocking either for 1 h immediately after conditioning impairs appetitive LTM, blocking at 22 h does not; MBON-alpha1 but not PAM-alpha1 is required at retrieval. | [Ichinose et al. 2015, eLife 10719, 'Reward signal in a recurrent circuit drives appetitive](https://elifesciences.org/articles/10719) | 2015 | strong |
| Dopamine release onto MB neurons is gated by coincident postsynaptic activity: stimulation of antennal lobe or the ascending fibres alone does not evoke DA release; release requires activation of nAChR, NMDAR and D1 pathways with rutabaga adenylyl cyclase in KCs; resulting long-term enhancement lasts >= 2 h. | [Ueno et al. 2017, eLife 21076, 'Coincident postsynaptic activity gates presynaptic dopamin](https://elifesciences.org/articles/21076) | 2017 | strong |
| Postsynaptic plasticity at KC->MBON synapses: nAChR alpha5 knockdown abolishes the immediate appetitive memory and the novelty response; alpha1/alpha2/alpha5 needed for 3 h memory; paired ACh + optogenetic dopamine ex vivo potentiates (n = 18) while ACh-only (n = 13) or DA-only (n = 10) do not; familiarity learning triggers alpha2-GFP receptor turnover in the alpha'3 compartment; aversive memories largely unaffected by these subunits (except alpha7 at 3 h). | [Pribbenow et al. 2022, eLife 80445, 'Postsynaptic plasticity of cholinergic synapses under](https://elifesciences.org/articles/80445) | 2022 | moderate |
| Nitric oxide is a co-transmitter in a subset of DANs; its effects develop more slowly than dopamine, depend on soluble guanylate cyclase in KCs, act antagonistically to dopamine, shorten memory retention and facilitate rapid updating, specialising those compartments for 'predicting the value of odors based only on recent events'. | [Aso et al. 2019, eLife 49257, 'Nitric oxide acts as a cotransmitter in a subset of dopamin](https://elifesciences.org/articles/49257) | 2019 | strong |
| DAN cell types (20 types) write and update memories with cell-type-specific rules for training requirement, decay dynamics, storage capacity and flexibility; a single DAN type can write aversive memory, reduce aversive memory, or write appetitive memory depending on the timing of its activation relative to odour. | [Aso & Rubin 2016, eLife 16135, 'Dopaminergic neurons write and update memories with cell-t](https://elifesciences.org/articles/16135) | 2016 | strong |
| Recall-gated consolidation theory mapped onto MB: LTM synapses update only when STM recall exceeds a threshold; in flies the gamma1 MBON (STM) depression disinhibits DANs of LTM compartments; with N = 2000 synapses (1000 STM / 1000 LTM), learning rates 0.25 (STM) / 0.05 (LTM), reliable-memory probability 0.25, the learnable timescale grows linearly with repetitions vs logarithmically for simple synapses; predicts inverted-U spacing effects. | [Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024, eLife 90793, 'Selective consolidation of learning and memory ](https://elifesciences.org/articles/90793) | 2024 | moderate |
| Trans-synaptic mapping of MBON outputs (25 of 28 split-GAL4 lines): MBONs converge on other MBONs (beta'2mp receives glutamatergic, GABAergic and cholinergic convergent input; gamma3beta'1 glutamatergic), on fan-shaped-body layers 4-5 and on the lateral accessory lobe; TH+ DANs overlap most with glutamatergic MBON gamma5beta'2a (25 +/- 0.7 cells) and beta'2mp (10.25 +/- 1.3; 4.75 +/- 1.1). Driver-line connectome annotation: 110 cell types directly connected to DANs/MBONs; 30 interneuron types receive >100 MBON synapses; CRE011 receives >2000 synapses from nine MBON types; SMP108 makes the most cholinergic connections to appetitive DANs; ~35 % of flies carry at least one MBON08 (65 % have four MBON09 only) - a stereotyped source of individuality. | [Scaplen et al. 2020, eLife 63379; Shuai et al. 2024, eLife 94168 (driver lines for associa](https://elifesciences.org/articles/63379) | 2020-2024 | moderate |
| Motor-related suppression of feature detectors: small-object optic glomeruli (LC11, LC21, LC18, LC12, LC15) but not loom detectors are suppressed when walking amplitude exceeds ~10 deg/s and within ~500 ms of saccadic turns; behaviour-modulation correlation rho = -0.23; removing trial-to-trial correlations degrades decoding by ~35 %; visual (saccade) suppression and motor suppression are balanced in magnitude, independent and combine linearly; gain recovers during inter-saccade intervals (~1 s). | [Turner, Krieger, Pang & Clandinin 2022, eLife 82587, 'Visual and motor signatures of locom](https://elifesciences.org/articles/82587) | 2022 | strong |
| Behavioural evidence for efference-copy integration in flight steering: bar + moving background responses add linearly (no dependence on onset latency -200/0/+200 ms), but loom and background responses mutually suppress (background response weaker when loom precedes by 100 ms; loom response reduced when simultaneous), consistent with an all-or-none efference copy; whether EC is graded vs all-or-none and which neurons ensure visual stability remain unidentified. | [Canelo et al. 2024, eLife reviewed preprint 93487, 'Integrative models of visually guided ](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/93487) | 2024 | moderate |
| Foundational cellular efference-copy results (NOT re-verified this session; from prior knowledge): Kim, Fitzgerald & Maimon 2015 Nat Neurosci 18:1247 showed HS/VS lobula-plate tangential cells receive saccade-locked motor-related inputs during flight opposite in sign to the expected visual reafference; Kim, Fenk, Lyu & Maimon 2017 Cell 168:280 showed the signal is quantitatively scaled to turn size/direction ('quantitative predictions'); Fenk et al. 2021 Curr Biol 31:4608 showed suppression during course-changing but not course-stabilising turns; Fujiwara et al. 2017 Nat Neurosci showed walking-speed/direction signals in HS cells independent of vision. Review confirmation: HS cells receive walking-state, speed and direction signals that modulate membrane potential independent of visual input. | [Kim et al. 2015; Kim et al. 2017; Fenk et al. 2021; Fujiwara et al. 2017; summarised in De](https://doi.org/10.1038/nn.4083) | 2015-2021 | strong |
| Hunger acts at the memory read-out, not the encoder: five MBON types (gamma1pedc>alphabeta, gamma2alpha'1, alpha'2, beta2beta'2a, alpha3) are required for hungry food-seeking; starvation potentiates MBON-gamma1pedc and alpha3 and depresses gamma2alpha'1, beta2beta'2a, alpha'2 (at KC->MBON synapses; KC population responses unchanged); six DAN types are modulated (PPL1-gamma1pedc suppressed; PPL1-gamma2alpha'1, PPL1-alpha'2alpha2, PAM-beta'2a, PAM-beta2beta'2a, PPL1-alpha3 activated) by serotonin (5HT1B/5HT2A), NPF, sNPF (hunger) and insulin, AstA (satiety) via DAMB; active-zone density rises in three DAN types under starvation. | [Tsao et al. 2018, eLife 35264, 'Drosophila mushroom bodies integrate hunger and satiety si](https://elifesciences.org/articles/35264) | 2018 | strong |
| Starvation re-tunes the input stage with opposite signs for attraction and aversion: sNPF sensitises an attraction-wired glomerulus, tachykinin suppresses an aversion-wired glomerulus, so starved flies gain sensitivity to attractive and lose sensitivity to aversive odours (Ko et al. 2015; earlier Root et al. 2011 Cell showed insulin-regulated sNPFR on Or42b ORNs mediating presynaptic facilitation - from prior knowledge, not re-verified). | [Ko et al. 2015, eLife 08298, 'Starvation promotes concerted modulation of appetitive olfac](https://elifesciences.org/articles/08298) | 2015 | moderate |
| Dopamine receptor balance as state-dependent gain: Dop1R1 is expressed in 58,049 and Dop2R in 68,528 of 118,331 brain cells; 10-48 h starvation elevates Dop1R1 in PAM-gamma5 terminals while 24 h starvation raises Dop2R in PPL1-alpha3 terminals (opposite directions); Dop2R knockdown in PPL1 impairs appetitive memory. Larval DAN-c1: presynaptic D2 autoreceptors plus postsynaptic D2R form a 'dual brake' keeping cAMP in an optimal range - excess DA (D2R knockdown or optogenetic over-activation) impairs learning (interaction p < 0.0001). Dop1R2 is required only in alpha/beta and alpha'/beta' lobes for 2 h and 24 h memory, not for STM and not in gamma. | [Hiramatsu et al. 2024, eLife 98358; Qi et al. 2024, eLife 104262 (larva); Kaldun et al. 20](https://elifesciences.org/articles/98358) | 2024 | moderate |
| Octopamine encodes internal energy status into memory formation: after 16 h starvation controls form only 2-min STM and after 40 h form 6-h ITM, whereas octopamine-null Tbh mutants form LTM at 16 h and anaesthesia-resistant LTM at 40 h; octopamine antagonists let controls form memory when they normally would not; octopamine given after training blocks LTM and before training inhibits STM; blocking insulin receptor in octopaminergic reward neurons restores normal STM; mutants eat ~34 % more sucrose after 40 h starvation. | [Berger et al. 2023, eLife reviewed preprint 88247, 'Octopamine integrates the status of in](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/88247) | 2023 | moderate |
| Whole-brain imaging during walking: a global brain-state change is composed of activity in identifiable neuron classes; DANs innervating MB compartments gamma3, gamma1-gamma2, alpha1, alpha2, alpha3, alpha'3 and CX dopaminergic populations (PB, EB), the wedge and a posterior 'M-omega' pattern are activated during spontaneous and forced walking. Hedonic feeding requires PAM DANs to alpha'/beta' lobes (not homeostatic feeding); valence (wall-following) and arousal (total activity) vary largely independently (r^2 = 0.14 to r = 0.44), PAM activation reduces both, NPF neurons affect valence only. | [Aimon, Cheng, Gjorgjieva & Grunwald Kadow 2023, eLife 85202; Weaver et al. 2023, eLife 845](https://elifesciences.org/articles/85202) | 2023-2025 | moderate |
| Serotonergic CSD neuron: the sole serotonergic pair innervating the antennal lobe is inhibited by nearly all odours, co-releases acetylcholine, and exogenous vs endogenous serotonin have opposite effects on olfactory responses. In the MB, the PAM-alpha1 -> DPM (serotonergic/GABAergic) microcircuit carries 4,709 synapses (14 PAM-alpha1 neurons, 5-21 synapses each) and is inhibitory; after appetitive training PAM-alpha1 activity rises and DPM activity falls in the second hour; brief 1-h PAM-alpha1 disruption fragments sleep (only under 30-46 h starvation) and abolishes 24-h LTM while sparing 2-min memory; Dop1R1 in DPM couples sleep and LTM, Dop1R2 affects LTM only; THIP 0.1 mg/ml rescues. | [Zhang & Gaudry 2016, eLife 16836; Yan et al. 2025, eLife reviewed preprint 104862](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/104862) | 2016-2025 | moderate |
| Sleep-dependent consolidation of courtship LTM: ventral fan-shaped-body neurons (necessary and sufficient) drive re-activation of DAN-aSP13 7-10 h after training; sleep deprivation at 7-9 h or 8-10 h abolishes LTM (suppression index 30-40 % -> ~0) while deprivation outside the window has little effect; activating FB neurons 5-7 h after 1-h training converts STM to LTM; dorsal FB (classic sleep switch) is neither necessary nor sufficient; vFB activation with DAN-aSP13 silenced yields no LTM. | [Dag et al. 2019, eLife 42786, 'Neuronal reactivation during post-learning sleep consolidat](https://elifesciences.org/articles/42786) | 2019 | strong |
| Two induced sleep states have non-overlapping transcriptional programs: dFB optogenetic ('active') sleep keeps wake-like brain activity and upregulates axon-guidance genes, Dop1R1 and nAChR alpha1/3/4/5 (278 DEGs: 171 up, 107 down), whereas THIP ('quiet') sleep lowers neural activity (only ~30 % of active neurons shared with wake) and downregulates metabolic/ribosomal genes (129 DEGs: 110 down, 19 up); nAChR alpha1/alpha2 knockout reduces sleep, alpha3/4/6/7 knockout increases it. DPM neurons promote sleep via GABA onto alpha'/beta' KCs (GABA-A, GABA-B-R3). | [Anthoney et al. 2023, eLife reviewed preprint 88198; Haynes, Christmann & Griffith 2015, e](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/88198) | 2015-2023 | moderate |
| Spacing effect mechanism: MP1 (PPL1-gamma1pedc) DANs show sustained calcium activity for ~2 h after spaced training; via the Gq-coupled DAMB receptor this activates PKC-delta in alpha/beta KC mitochondria (cytosolic 30-120 min, t17 = 3.18, p = 0.0055; mitochondrial 3-4.5 h) and sustains pyruvate flux 8-9 h; PKC-delta knockdown impairs 24-h memory after 5x spaced (F2,58 = 12.59, p < 0.0001) but not massed or single-cycle training; ~2,000 alpha/beta KCs per hemisphere. | [Comyn, Preat, Pavlowsky & Placais 2024, eLife reviewed preprint 92860](https://elifesciences.org/reviewed-preprints/92860) | 2024 | strong |
| Hemibrain antennal-lobe circuit as modelled in FlyBrainLab: 1,357 OSNs across 51 receptor types, 51 uniglomerular PN channels (141 PNs), 97 presynaptic LNs, 154 postsynaptic LNs; the presynaptic-LN divisive-normalisation subcircuit is explicitly labelled a 'Predictive Coding circuit' for concentration-invariant identity encoding, and the hemibrain-based model additionally detects odorant-waveform transients (ON-OFF LN subcircuit). Neurosecretory connectome: 80 NSCs in 10 subtypes receive indirect input from 321 ORNs dominated by aversive and food-related channels. | [Lazar, Liu, Turkcan & Zhou 2021, eLife 62362 (FlyBrainLab); McKim et al. 2024, eLife revie](https://elifesciences.org/articles/62362) | 2021-2024 | moderate |
| Reward-prediction-error style evidence in flies (NOT re-verified this session; from prior knowledge): Felsenberg et al. 2018 Cell 175:709 - extinction of aversive memory is driven by reward DANs responding to omission of expected shock (new opposing memory, not erasure); Hattori et al. 2017 Cell 169:956 - PPL1-alpha'3 DAN/MBON-alpha'3 encode odour novelty that habituates with familiarity via KC->MBON depression; Rajagopalan et al. 2023 PNAS 120:e2221415120 - fly choice follows operant matching and DAN reward signals are reduced by reward expectation, fit by an RPE-like model with MBON feedback; Handler et al. 2019 Cell 178:60 - forward vs backward pairing flips plasticity sign via Dop1R1 (cAMP) vs Dop1R2 (ER Ca2+); Villar et al. 2022 Curr Biol - PPL1 DANs encode relative not absolute aversive value; Zolin et al. 2021 Nat Neurosci 24:1555 - PAM DANs carry context-dependent movement signals; models: Bennett et al. 2021 Nat Commun 12:2569 (RPE via MBON->DAN feedback), Jiang & Litwin-Kumar 2021 PLOS Comput Biol (meta-learned heterogeneous DAN rules), Springer & Nawrot 2021 eNeuro (MBON->DAN feedback for reward prediction and extinction), Zhao et al. 2021 Sci Rep 'Predictive olfactory learning in Drosophila'. | [Felsenberg 2018; Hattori 2017; Rajagopalan 2023; Handler 2019; Villar 2022; Zolin 2021; Be](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2221415120) | 2017-2023 | moderate |
| MaleCNS v1.0 as described by third-party simulation repositories: 165,122 neurons, ~90 million synapses, ~6.2 million connections with >= 5 synapses; whole-CNS LIF simulation following Shiu et al. 2024 (Nature) parameters required retuning the per-synapse weight from 0.275 mV to 0.125 mV to avoid saturation; caveats listed: point neurons, chemical synapses only (no gap junctions), no neuromodulation or learning, single male individual. | [sukoji/flyboard and davidvvliet/MaleCNS-Connectome GitHub READMEs (third-party; the primar](https://github.com/sukoji/flyboard) | 2026 | weak |

**Usable ideas**

- Two-stage input normaliser copied from AL: per-channel Weber-Fechner (log/integral-feedback) adaptation with tau ~ 250 ms followed by population divisive normalisation with exponent n ~ 1.5, sigma ~ 12 (Olsen units), pool weight k ~ 0.1; Kadakia & Emonet show the two are additive (95/85 % vs 70/40 % identity classification). Apply again at the sparse-expansion layer (APL analogue).
- Make the normalisation pool connectome-weighted, not uniform: derive per-channel inhibitory weights from LN->OSN/PN synapse counts (7-fold asymmetries exist; narrowly tuned channels get little feedback inhibition), and route labelled-line channels (CO2/LN23-type) around normalisation and plasticity as non-updatable priors.
- Add a slow presynaptic-depression term (tau ~ 10 s, adapts to ~50 %) that rescales gain without changing the population identity vector, separately from fast divisive normalisation - this is what preserves identity under sustained input.
- Implement the innate-valence head as a fixed, stereotyped, low-rate, broadly tuned categorical pooling layer (44 output types, ~3x broader tuning than inputs, ~15 % valence-coding units) read directly from PN->LH connectivity; output both a valence scalar and an identity vector decorrelated by inhibitory (mPN-like) inputs; keep it non-plastic but gate its precision (habituation = inhibition, dishabituation = DAN-driven reset).
- Combine innate and learned heads in 'convergence' units (larval and adult evidence: MBON->LHON axo-axonic input, ~30 % of LHONs modulated by MB, downstream hubs CRE011/SMP108 receiving >2000 / most MBON inputs) where the learned head modulates the prior head's output gain rather than its input.
- Prediction-error module = MBON->DAN loops with three motif classes: (i) within-compartment inhibitory feedback on 'discharging' DANs (error cancellation), (ii) excitatory positive-feedback loops for LTM (MBON-alpha1 -> PAM-alpha1, ~1 h window), (iii) hierarchical instructor->student relays through one cholinergic interneuron (SMP108) enabling second-order prediction. Use the local rule dW = delta [k + W - w_rest] as a cheap default; expect (and test for) absence of blocking.
- Precision on the teaching signal: gate dopamine (learning-rate) delivery by coincident postsynaptic activity (Ueno) and by an inverted-U autoreceptor term (D2 'dual brake'); scale learning rates per head by state vectors (hunger, energy reserves via octopamine-like suppression, arousal) with head-specific signs; add a slow opposite-sign co-transmitter channel (NO analogue) to give each compartment its own forgetting rate.
- Mixture of memory timescales: 15-16 compartments with distinct decay constants and receptor-phase specialisation (Dop1R2 only for >2 h phases in alpha/beta, alpha'/beta'); consolidation into slow weights gated by fast-memory recall strength (recall-gated plasticity), which yields spacing effects and linear scaling of learnable timescale with repetitions.
- Efference copy as action-conditioned, feature-specific precision reduction: for self-initiated 'course-changing' actions inject a sign-inverted, amplitude-scaled copy of the predicted reafference into the relevant feature detectors; suppress small-object/feature heads but not loom/threat heads; keep motor and sensory (saccade) suppression as independent additive terms with ~0.5-1 s recovery.
- State-dependent gain belongs at the read-out and teaching stages, not the sparse code: hunger changes KC->MBON synaptic gains bidirectionally and DAN excitability while KC responses are unchanged; at the input stage use opposite-sign peptidergic gains on attraction vs aversion channels.
- Offline consolidation phase with two modes: (a) 'active' replay that re-activates the same teaching neurons in a delayed window (7-10 h analogue) driven by a sleep-need circuit (vFB, not dFB), coupled to the PAM-alpha1 <-> DPM loop; (b) 'quiet' phase for metabolic/weight downscaling. Make long-term commitment depend on an hour-scale integrator of teaching-signal magnitude (MP1/DAMB/PKC-delta spacing effect).
- Broadcast arousal as a global scalar that co-activates dopaminergic teaching populations across compartments and CX during action (walking), kept orthogonal to valence; use it to scale precision globally.

**Dead ends**

- Uniform (global, identity-blind) normalisation pool: the original Olsen 2010 fit assumed one pool; connectome data show ~7-fold glomerulus-specific LN weights and narrowly tuned glomeruli with reduced feedback inhibition, so uniform-pool models mispredict per-channel gain.
- Lateral inhibition as the mechanism of slow (seconds) adaptation: picrotoxin/GABA-B manipulations do not alter PN adaptation timescales; the mechanism is presynaptic vesicle depression (Martelli & Fiala 2019).
- Subtractive normalisation and intraglomerular gain control: fail to give concentration-invariant mean plus diverse second-order tuning (Shen et al. 2025).
- Full Rescorla-Wagner / scalar reward-prediction-error as the fly learning rule: flies show no blocking; a local three-term dopaminergic rule with MBON->DAN feedback fits 92 intervention experiments (r = 0.76) and correctly predicts no blocking; cross-odour error terms are unsupported.
- Treating LH as purely innate and MB as purely learned, or wiring them in series: ~30 % of LHONs receive MB modulation, MBONs synapse axo-axonically onto LHONs, and dedicated convergence neurons integrate both; also only ~15 % of LH lines carry valence, so 'LH = valence map' is too simple.
- Using the classic dorsal fan-shaped-body sleep switch as the consolidation gate: dFB is neither necessary nor sufficient for courtship LTM consolidation (vFB is), and optogenetic dFB 'sleep' is wake-like in activity with a transcriptional program disjoint from pharmacological quiet sleep.
- Modelling hunger as sensory gain only: KC odour responses are unchanged by starvation; the modulation sits at KC->MBON synapses and DANs (plus opposite-sign peptidergic tuning of specific input channels).
- Naive whole-connectome LIF dynamics with uniform synaptic weights: third-party MaleCNS simulations saturate at 0.275 mV/synapse and had to be re-tuned to 0.125 mV; they omit neuromodulation, gap junctions and learning (weak, unverified source).
- Light-microscopy overlap (Dolan 2019) and trans-Tango (Scaplen 2020) for connectivity: give neither directionality nor synaptic strength; use EM connectome counts instead.
- Assuming a single graded efference-copy signal: behavioural modelling shows reflex-specific regimes (linear addition for bar+background, all-or-none suppression for loom+background), and the graded-vs-all-or-none question is unresolved at the cellular level.

**Open gaps**

- No fly study demonstrates hierarchical predictive coding in the Rao-Ballard sense (top-down prediction units subtracted from sensory input to yield explicit error units, or 'mismatch' neurons); fly evidence is limited to motor-related cancellation (efference copy) and reinforcement prediction in the MB. The AL 'predictive coding circuit' label (FlyBrainLab) denotes divisive normalisation only.
- Per-DAN-type fraction of dendritic input contributed by MBONs versus KCs, SMP/CRE interneurons and sensory pathways (present in Li et al. 2020 / neuPrint) could not be retrieved here and has not, to my knowledge, been re-derived for MaleCNS; this is the key number for weighting error-cancellation versus other drives on the teaching signal.
- Dopamine as precision (uncertainty- or confidence-scaled learning rate) has not been tested in flies; the closest evidence is postsynaptic gating of DA release (Ueno 2017), autoreceptor 'brakes', and state-dependent receptor balance. No experiment varies outcome variance and measures DAN gain.
- Lateral horn function remains mostly unknown: 496 connectome LH types vs 82 tool-accessible; ~15 % of tested lines show valence; the quantitative rule by which convergence neurons weight innate (LH) versus learned (MB) valence in the adult is unmeasured.
- Which memories are selected for sleep replay, and how replay content is specified, is unknown; the link between R5/dFB slow-wave activity and compartment-specific consolidation is unquantified; Yan et al. 2025 note the microcircuit-to-systems 'leap' remains open.
- Serotonergic (CSD, DPM) modulation has sign that depends on source and state (exogenous vs endogenous 5-HT opposite), so a single-sign serotonin gain cannot be specified from current data.
- Whether fly efference-copy signals are graded or all-or-none, and which neurons implement visual stability during steering, are unidentified (Canelo 2024); efference copy outside vision (auditory/mechanosensory during song or grooming) is largely undocumented in adult flies.
- The MaleCNS synaptic graph does not capture peptidergic/aminergic volume transmission (e.g., sNPF, tachykinin, NPF, insulin, AstA, octopamine effects on DANs), so connectome-derived priors omit exactly the precision/state channels; male-specific differences in these circuits are unassessed.
- No 2024-2026 fly paper explicitly claiming 'predictive coding' in sensory circuits could be located through the accessible sources (eLife mirrors only); arXiv/bioRxiv/Nature/Cell were unreachable, so recent work (e.g., Turner-lab RPE follow-ups, MaleCNS analyses) may exist but is unverified.

**Key numbers**

- AL divisive normalisation: n ~ 1.5; 57x ORN drive range compressed to 6.6x in PNs (Olsen 2010, via secondary source); Shen 2025 fit: sigma = 12, n = 1.5, k = 0.1; PN concentration slope 0.05 -> 0.000023
- Kadakia & Emonet 2019: ORN adaptation tau ~ 250 ms; identity classification (N_ID = 100 / 1000): both mechanisms 95 / 85 %, ORN adaptation only 92 / 82 %, DN only 88 / 65 %, neither 70 / 40 %; MI 2.5-3 bits vs 0.5-1.5 bits
- Martelli & Fiala 2019: PN adaptation to ~50 %, timescales 3-40 s, presynaptic depression tau ~ 10 s (fast 300-400 ms)
- Hemibrain AL/LH: 196 AL LNs (74 types), 188 ALPN types, 1,357 OSNs / 51 receptor types, 97 presynaptic + 154 postsynaptic LNs (model), 1,428 LH-dendrite third-order neurons -> 496 LH cell types; within-type connectivity cosine similarity ~0.75
- LN weight asymmetry: 953 vs 133 synapses (lLN2F_b onto Or42a vs Or43b OSNs, ~7x); DA2 vs DL5 OSN-OSN reciprocal 6 % vs 1.5 %
- Dolan 2019: 2,444 lines -> 82 LH types (~38 % of ~165); 44 LHON types (26 ACh / 13 GABA / 9 Glu); 12 aversion + 3 approach lines of 89; ~15 % valence phenotypes; ~30 % LHONs overlap MB, 16 % >15 % overlap with MBONs, 70 % none
- Frechter 2019: 410 cells; LHONs 10x quieter, 3x broader (35 % vs 12 % responsive pairs), 14 Hz vs 21 Hz; ~70 % of category-informative cells are LHONs
- CO2 pathway: ~40 ORNs; PNm1 27 % and PNm17 18 % of input from LN23
- MB connectome: 1,927 KCs (701 gamma / 337 alpha'beta' / 889 alphabeta), 34 MBON types (14 atypical), 158 DANs (PPL1 6 x 1; PAM 15 types, 3-26 cells), 15-16 compartments, 20 DAN types; uniglomerular olfactory PNs = 63.6 % of KC input; ~8 % KCs visual; 5-synapse analysis threshold
- Gkanias 2022: 6 MBONs + 6 DANs; rule dW = delta [k + W - w_rest], w_rest = 1; r = 0.76 over 92 experiments (p = 2.2e-18); blocking absent
- Yamada 2023: SMP108 2,416 / 2,753 cholinergic presynapses; MBON-alpha1 is the only MBON without direct output to other-compartment DANs
- Ichinose 2015: MBON-alpha1 -> PAM-alpha1 loop, 1 h post-training window; Ueno 2017: LTE >= 2 h, DA release needs coincident nAChR + NMDAR + D1
- Lindsey & Litwin-Kumar 2024: N = 2000 synapses, learning rates 0.25 (STM) / 0.05 (LTM), lambda = 0.25; learnable timescale linear vs logarithmic in repetitions
- Turner 2022: suppression of LC11/21/18/12/15 for walking > ~10 deg/s and within ~500 ms of saccades; rho = -0.23; ~35 % decoding loss without correlations; inter-saccade interval ~1 s
- Tsao 2018: 5 MBON types, 6 DAN types modulated by hunger; Hiramatsu 2024: Dop1R1 in 58,049 and Dop2R in 68,528 of 118,331 brain cells; starvation raises Dop1R1 in PAM-gamma5 (10 h) and Dop2R in PPL1-alpha3 (24 h)
- Berger 2023: 16 h starvation -> STM only, 40 h -> 6 h ITM in controls; Tbh mutants form LTM at 16 h; ~34 % more sucrose intake
- Dag 2019: DAN-aSP13 re-activation 7-10 h post-training; sleep deprivation 7-9 / 8-10 h abolishes LTM (SI 30-40 % -> 0); FB activation 5-7 h converts STM -> LTM
- Yan 2025: PAM-alpha1 -> DPM 4,709 synapses (14 neurons, 5-21 each); effects only under 30-46 h starvation; THIP 0.1 mg/ml rescue
- Anthoney 2023: active sleep 278 DEGs (171 up) vs quiet sleep 129 DEGs (110 down); ~30 % of quiet-sleep-active neurons shared with wake
- Comyn 2024: MP1 sustained ~2 h; PKC-delta mitochondrial 3-4.5 h; pyruvate flux elevated 8-9 h; ~2,000 alpha/beta KCs per hemisphere
- Shuai 2024: 828 lines / ~300 cell types; 110 types directly connected to DANs/MBONs; 30 interneurons with >100 MBON inputs; CRE011 >2000 MBON synapses; MBON08 present in ~35 % of flies
- MaleCNS v1.0 (third-party README, unverified): 165,122 neurons, ~90 M synapses, 6.2 M connections >= 5 synapses; LIF weight retuned 0.275 -> 0.125 mV/synapse
- Valence vs arousal independence: r^2 = 0.14 to r = 0.44 (Lueningschroer-Wang 2025)

## lit:multiscale

26 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| H-Net (Dynamic Chunking for End-to-End Hierarchical Sequence Modeling) replaces tokenization with a learned, differentiable chunker. Verified from the repo: a RoutingModule computes boundary probability p_t = clamp((1 - cos(q_proj(h_{t-1}), k_proj(h_t)))/2, 0, 1) with identity-initialised projections (first token p=1), ChunkLayer keeps only boundary positions, and DeChunkLayer upsamples with an EMA implemented on the Mamba2 kernel (A=-1, b=p, c=1; output = p*current + (1-p)*previous). The paper (arXiv 2507.07955, not directly fetchable) adds a ratio loss with a target compression ratio N and a confidence-scaled straight-through term; abstract claims a 1-stage byte-level H-Net beats a compute/data-matched BPE Transformer, a 2-stage H-Net matches a token Transformer of ~2x its size with better data scaling, and gains grow for Chinese, code and DNA (~4x data efficiency on DNA). | [goombalab/hnet repository (README + hnet/modules/dc.py); Hwang, Wang, Gu 2025](https://github.com/goombalab/hnet) | 2025 | strong |
| Exact H-Net configurations (verified JSON): 1-stage XL = arch ['m4', ['T24'], 'm4'] with d_model [1024, 2048], d_intermediate [0, 5504], vocab 256, Mamba2 ssm_cfg {chunk 256, d_conv 4, d_state 128, expand 2}, sliding window 1023 in the outer stage and full attention (-1) in the main stage. 2-stage XL = ['m4', ['T1m4', ['T27'], 'm4T1'], 'm4'] with d_model [1024, 1536, 2048], d_intermediate [0, 4096, 5504], 16 heads per stage, rotary dims [32, 48, 64], windows [1023, 1023, -1]. Models trained on 100B-token FineWeb-Edu subsets, L/XL compute-matched to GPT-3 Large/XL; Chinese and code 2-stage XL variants released. | [goombalab/hnet configs/hnet_1stage_XL.json and hnet_2stage_XL.json](https://github.com/goombalab/hnet/blob/main/configs/hnet_2stage_XL.json) | 2025 | strong |
| Byte Latent Transformer (BLT): bytes are grouped into variable patches by a small byte-LM's next-byte entropy; a light local encoder and local decoder wrap a large latent transformer. Repo README states a scaling study 'up to 8B parameters and 8T training bytes' (the arXiv v1 abstract said 4T) and that BLT 'matches tokenization-based LLM performance at scale, with significant improvements in inference efficiency and robustness'; released blt-1b and blt-7b. From the paper (recollection, not fetchable here): Llama 3 tokens average ~4.4 bytes, BLT patches ~6-8 bytes, patch size 8 yields up to ~50% fewer inference FLOPs, and BLT 8B is roughly at parity with Llama 3 8B on standard benchmarks while much stronger on character-noise robustness. | [facebookresearch/blt README; Pagnoni et al. 2024 (arXiv 2412.09871)](https://github.com/facebookresearch/blt) | 2024 | strong |
| Bolmo (Ai2, Dec 2025) byteifies existing subword LMs: learn local byte encoder/decoder + boundary predictor with the global model frozen, then fine-tune end-to-end, for <1% of original pretraining cost. Stage 1 (subword-to-byte distillation) 9.8B tokens (~43B bytes); Stage 2 (end-to-end) 39.3B tokens (~173B bytes). Bolmo-7B (7.6B, from OLMo 3 7B) and Bolmo-1B (1.5B, from OLMo 2 1B). CUTE character understanding: Bolmo 7B 78.6 vs OLMo 3 7B 56.9 vs BLT 7B 52.3; EXECUTE multilingual character: 71.6 vs 55.1 vs 46.3; math 48.9 vs 55.3 (worse); code 41.0 vs 40.1. | [allenai/bolmo-core README](https://github.com/allenai/bolmo-core) | 2025 | strong |
| EvaByte: 6.5B byte-level LM trained on 1.5T bytes with multibyte prediction and EVA linear-ish attention; claims to rival tokenizer-based open LMs with 5x less training data and up to 2x faster decoding (Medusa-style multibyte generation). | [OpenEvaByte/evabyte README](https://github.com/OpenEvaByte/evabyte) | 2025 | moderate |
| SpaceByte (2024) inserts global transformer blocks only at 'space-like' bytes (word boundaries) and local blocks everywhere. Verified config: SpaceByte-793M+184M has 28 global layers (d=1536) and 26 local layers (d=768), byte context 8192, global context 1344 (~6.1 bytes per global position), attention window 768; compared against a 1B subword Transformer (d=1536, 2048-token context) on PG19, Stories, arXiv, GitHub. Paper claims parity with subword Transformers and superiority over MegaByte at matched compute. | [kjslag/spacebyte reproduce/ README; Slagle 2024 (arXiv 2404.14408)](https://github.com/kjslag/spacebyte) | 2024 | moderate |
| bGPT (2024): hierarchical byte model with a 12-layer patch-level decoder and a 3-layer byte-level decoder (hidden 768, 110M params), default patch 16 bytes x 512 patches (8 KB context). Reported 99.99% accuracy simulating CPU state transitions and 0.0011 bits-per-byte error converting ABC notation to MIDI; separate checkpoints for Wikipedia, ImageNet, LibriSpeech, IrishMAN, CPU. | [sanderwood/bgpt README](https://github.com/sanderwood/bgpt) | 2024 | strong |
| MambaByte (CoLM 2024): byte-level selective SSM at 353M and 972M params trained on PG19 (11.68B training bytes); reports better bits-per-byte than MegaByte at matched compute and introduces resumable speculative decoding via a modified parallel scan. Repo provides conversion between byte cross-entropy, bits-per-byte and word/subword perplexity (example test byte CE 0.59). | [jxiw/MambaByte README](https://github.com/jxiw/MambaByte) | 2024 | moderate |
| Multiscale Byte Language Model (MBLM, IBM 2025): model-agnostic hierarchical stack (Mamba or Transformer per stage) that trains on contexts of 5M+ bytes; example config: 2 stages, seq lengths [1024, 8], dims [1024, 1024], 5 layers each, vocab 257. | [ai4sd/multiscale-byte-lm README](https://github.com/ai4sd/multiscale-byte-lm) | 2025 | moderate |
| Block Transformer (2024): global-to-local hierarchy (self-attention between coarse blocks in lower layers, local per-block decoding above, block length 4) yields '10-20x gains in throughput' at equivalent perplexity; 1.2B checkpoint released. | [itsnamgyu/block-transformer README](https://github.com/itsnamgyu/block-transformer) | 2024 | moderate |
| MrT5 (2024/2025): a learnable delete gate after layer 3 of a ByT5 encoder merges/deletes byte tokens; deletion rate is steered to a target (e.g. 40%) by a PI controller instead of hyperparameter sweeps. | [jkallini/mrt5 README](https://github.com/jkallini/mrt5) | 2025 | moderate |
| ByteChunk (EMNLP 2026): an H-Net-style router (1-layer local Mamba encoder + cosine-similarity boundary router, byte embeddings initialised from the backbone's subword embeddings, chunk-alignment loss, frozen backbone) trained on 500K Hindi sentences transfers zero-shot to related low-resource languages: POS accuracy avg 36.9 (subword) -> 47.3 (H-Net), +10.4; Bhojpuri 43.8->54.4, Marathi 39.1->46.3, Magahi 41.0->53.1, Sanskrit 21.6->30.6, Urdu 38.8->52.1. Motivation: 97.7% of Bhojpuri words are split into multiple subword tokens. | [snjev310/ByteChunk (EMNLP 2026)](https://github.com/snjev310/ByteChunk) | 2026 | moderate |
| OpenAI Parameter Golf (2026, 16 MB artifact limit, tokenizer-agnostic bits-per-byte on FineWeb): a byte-level H-Net (byte260 vocab) reached 1.2070 BPB after 4 h vs 1.2107 for an sp1024 (SentencePiece-1024) H-Net and 1.2074 for the official baseline; at 10 min the byte model lagged (1.4116 vs 1.3734 BPB). The byte model learned whitespace-aligned, word-like chunk boundaries without supervision. | [DariusFeher/parameter-golf-byte-hnet README](https://github.com/DariusFeher/parameter-golf-byte-hnet) | 2026 | moderate |
| GeneZip (arXiv 2602.17739, 2026): H-Net-style dynamic routing with a Region-Aware Ratio objective and bounded routing for DNA; 70M-636M checkpoints with 12.8K and 128K contexts; region-specific compression targets (promoter/CDS/UTR/intergenic) learned from annotations and applied to unannotated DNA; best average rank on DNALongBench contact-map prediction and eQTL, and eQTL fine-tuning 50.4x faster than JanusDNA (50 vs 2520 min). Without supervision the router assigns more compression to repetitive elements. | [AndyJZhao/GeneZip README](https://github.com/AndyJZhao/GeneZip) | 2026 | moderate |
| Hierarchical Reasoning Model (HRM, 2025): two coupled recurrent modules (slow high-level, fast low-level), 27M params, trained on ~1000 examples without CoT; claims strong ARC-AGI, near-perfect Sudoku-Extreme and Maze-Hard; full ARC runs ~24 h on 8 GPUs. Tiny Recursive Model (TRM, 2025, 7M params) reports ARC-AGI-1 45%, ARC-AGI-2 8%, Sudoku-Extreme ~87% (MLP) / ~75% (attention) with a single network, explicitly 'without any hierarchy' and without the fixed-point/implicit-gradient theory, and characterises HRM as 'too complicated' and over-reliant on brain analogies. | [sapientinc/HRM and SamsungSAILMontreal/TinyRecursiveModels READMEs](https://github.com/SamsungSAILMontreal/TinyRecursiveModels) | 2025 | strong |
| ARC Prize's independent HRM analysis (Aug 2025; blog blocked here, recollection): HRM verified at ~32% on ARC-AGI-1 semi-private (vs 40.3% public claim) and ~2% on ARC-AGI-2; swapping the H/L hierarchy for a same-size plain transformer cost only a few points, while the outer refinement loop (1 -> 8-16 iterations) and heavy train-time augmentation (~300 per task) accounted for most of the performance; cross-task transfer was limited. | [ARC Prize HRM analysis (arcprize.org/blog/hrm-analysis; code at arcprize/hierarchical-reas](https://github.com/arcprize/hierarchical-reasoning-model-analysis) | 2025 | moderate |
| Nested Learning / HOPE (Behrouz et al., NeurIPS 2025; arXiv 2512.24695): a Continuum Memory System of MLP levels updated at different frequencies (lowest every chunk, higher every 2 or 4 chunks) plus Self-Modifying Titans (associative memory with learned key/value/learning-rate/forget projections, surprise-gated writes) and deep optimizers; paper experiments reportedly reach 1.3B params with gains over Titans/Samba/Mamba/Transformer baselines. An independent small-scale reproduction reports 1.721 bits/byte at 30.6M params (byte-level), ppl ~81 at 161.5M (BPE), and no degradation extrapolating to 4x training length (8K bytes). | [smallhours19/nested-learning-hope (reproduction) and the Nested Learning paper](https://github.com/smallhours19/nested-learning-hope) | 2025 | moderate |
| Large Concept Models (Meta, Dec 2024): autoregression over sentence embeddings in the SONAR space (200 languages text / 57 speech), with MSE, diffusion (two-tower) and quantized variants; development at 1.6B params on ~1.3T tokens (7B in the paper). Repo warns results are sensitive to the exact GPU recipe. Paper (recollection) reports competitive-but-not-superior summarisation vs Llama-3.1-8B and strong zero-shot cross-lingual transfer. | [facebookresearch/large_concept_model README](https://github.com/facebookresearch/large_concept_model) | 2024 | moderate |
| Deep temporal active inference (Friston, Rosch, Parr, Price, Bowman 2017/2018, Neurosci Biobehav Rev): discrete-state hierarchy in which each higher-level state generates an entire sequence (policy/initial state) at the level below; lower-level posteriors over initial states are passed up as likelihoods; higher levels evolve on slower clocks; expected free energy drives epistemic sampling at each level (reading/saccade demo). Follow-ups: Pezzulo, Rigoli, Friston 2018 'Hierarchical active inference: a theory of motivated control'; Friston et al. 2020 'Generative models, linguistic communication and active inference'; Friston et al. 2021 'Active listening' (word segmentation chosen to minimise free energy over candidate boundaries). | [Curated list BerenMillidge/FEP_Active_Inference_Papers; Friston et al. 2017](https://github.com/BerenMillidge/FEP_Active_Inference_Papers) | 2017-2021 | strong |
| The 2026 textbook 'Fundamentals of Active Inference' (Namjoshi) codifies hierarchical depth as nested POMDP layers with nested time scales ('a slow top regime contextualizes a fast bottom layer', Ch. 10 §10.4, Example 10.8), continuous two-layer hierarchies with top-down priors (Ch. 8), and temporal depth via generalised coordinates (Ch. 6); implementations exist in the companion repo. | [ActiveInferenceInstitute/fundamentals](https://github.com/ActiveInferenceInstitute/fundamentals) | 2026 | moderate |
| Renormalising generative models (Friston et al. 2024, 'From pixels to planning: scale-free active inference', arXiv 2407.20292; and 'Supervised structure learning' 2024) build discrete hierarchies by grouping lower-level paths into higher-level states without gradient descent, demonstrated on MNIST, short videos and simple games; VERSES' AXIOM (Heins et al. 2025, arXiv 2505.24784) uses expanding object-centric mixture models with expected-free-energy planning on the Gameworld 10k benchmark and reports outperforming DreamerV3/BBF within 10k steps with far fewer parameters (exact figures not verifiable here). None of these are language models. | [VersesTech/axiom README; Friston et al. 2024](https://github.com/VersesTech/axiom) | 2024-2025 | weak |
| Hierarchical active inference in spatial tasks (Van de Maele, Dhoedt, Verbelen, Pezzulo, Nature Communications 2024): a two-level model (clone-structured cognitive graph / HMM under a PyMDP active-inference layer) reproduces hippocampal-prefrontal behaviour in spatial alternation; evaluated by success rates from every start pose. | [toonvdm/bridging-cognitive-maps](https://github.com/toonvdm/bridging-cognitive-maps) | 2024 | moderate |
| Foundational multi-timescale recurrence: Clockwork RNN (Koutník et al. 2014) partitions hidden units into modules with periods 1,2,4,8... updated only when t mod T_i = 0, with connections from slow to fast modules; Dilated RNN (Chang et al. 2017) uses exponentially dilated skip connections; HM-RNN (Chung, Ahn, Bengio, ICLR 2017) learns hard boundaries with a straight-through estimator (text8 ~1.29 bpc). HM-RNN-style boundary learning was hard to train and prone to degenerate segmentations; H-Net's EMA smoothing plus ratio loss is the fix that made it work at LM scale. | [Koutník et al. 2014; Chang et al. 2017; Chung et al. 2017 (recollection; not fetchable)](https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.01704) | 2014-2017 | moderate |
| Evidence for multiscale predictive coding in the human brain (Caucheteux, Gramfort, King, Nature Human Behaviour 2023): augmenting GPT-2 activations with forecasts of future words improves fMRI mapping, best at a horizon of ~8 words, and higher/longer-range predictions map to frontal areas while short-range map to temporal areas — a predictive hierarchy over timescales. | [Caucheteux, Gramfort, King 2023 (recollection; host blocked)](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-022-01516-w) | 2023 | moderate |
| Predictive-coding training of deep nets has been scaled to 100+ layers (µPC, Innocenti et al. 2025) but only on MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/CIFAR-10, and benchmark suites (PCX, Pinchetti et al. 2024) show PC networks still trail backprop on standard vision tasks; no backprop-free PC-trained language model competitive at >100M params was found (GitHub search for 'predictive coding transformer backpropagation-free' returned 0 repos). | [Innocenti et al. 2025 (arXiv 2505.13124); Pinchetti et al. 2024 (recollection); GitHub sea](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13124) | 2024-2025 | moderate |
| Open-source 'active inference language model' work is essentially absent: GitHub searches for active-inference + LLM and expected-free-energy + LLM agent return only hobby-scale repos (e.g. micro-actinf 20 KB POMDP filter for LLM agents, 4 stars) and no trained LM with EFE-based decoding or a reported perplexity. | [GitHub repository search (Sept 2026)](https://github.com/naderloocodelab/micro-actinf) | 2026 | moderate |

**Usable ideas**

- Instantiate Friston's deep temporal model as an H-Net: level-k+1 hidden state = one chunk of level-k sequence; router boundary probability p_t = (1-cos(q_{t-1}, k_t))/2 acts as the posterior over a binary 'segment' action; H-Net's ratio loss (target N bytes/chunk) is exactly a prior over chunk rate; EMA dechunk (output = p*current + (1-p)*previous) is the top-down prediction being smoothed across the chunk.
- Use SSM/recurrent (fly E/I core, Mamba2) layers at the fast byte stages and attention at the slow stage, mirroring verified H-Net layouts: 1-stage ['m4', ['T24'], 'm4'] d=1024->2048; 2-stage ['m4', ['T1m4', ['T27'], 'm4T1'], 'm4'] d=1024->1536->2048, sliding-window 1023 at fast stages, full attention at the slow stage.
- Make the boundary rule surprise-driven (BLT): trigger a new chunk when next-byte entropy / precision-weighted prediction error crosses a threshold; this allocates compute where free energy is high and is the same signal that gates dopaminergic plasticity in the mushroom body.
- Hold the chunk rate with a controller rather than a fixed loss weight (MrT5's PI controller on target deletion rate), or make the rate context-dependent (GeneZip region-aware ratio) so precision can modulate temporal resolution.
- Adopt HOPE/Nested-Learning multi-frequency memory: fast memory written every chunk, slower memories every 2 and 4 chunks, with surprise-gated writes (Titans) — map to KC->MBON fast plasticity vs slower consolidation and to a persistent 'context' memory.
- Add a long-horizon auxiliary objective at the slow level (predict ~8 words ahead, per Caucheteux 2023) instead of relying solely on next-byte loss.
- Treat segmentation as action (Friston 'Active listening'): score candidate boundary placements by expected free energy and pick the segmentation that minimises it; at decode time, EFE over the slow level chooses the next chunk before the fast level spells it out (Block-Transformer-style global-to-local decoding).
- Byteify a pretrained backbone (Bolmo recipe: freeze global model, train local byte encoder/decoder + boundary predictor on ~10B tokens, then end-to-end on ~40B) so connectome-prior stages can be tested cheaply against a fixed strong core.
- Evaluate with tokenizer-agnostic bits-per-byte plus CUTE/EXECUTE character tasks and noised benchmarks where byte hierarchies provably win (+20 pts CUTE), and report math/arithmetic separately where they lose.
- Pre-register hierarchy ablations that separate 'slow/fast hierarchy' from 'number of inference iterations' (HRM vs TRM lesson): compare same-parameter flat model with more refinement steps.
- Small-scale calibration: expect the byte-level hierarchy to trail a subword baseline early in training (1.41 vs 1.37 BPB at 10 min) and reach parity only later (1.2070 vs 1.2074 at 4 h) — budget runs accordingly.
- Use the nested-POMDP examples (Namjoshi 2026 Ch.10 §10.4; Van de Maele 2024 CSCG+PyMDP) as unit tests for the discrete slow/fast dynamics before scaling to text.

**Dead ends**

- Hard-wired clockwork/dilated update schedules (CW-RNN 2014, Dilated RNN 2017) are absent from competitive 2024-2026 LMs; learned per-channel decay (SSMs) and learned chunking replaced them — do not bake fixed periods into the core.
- HM-RNN-style hard boundary learning with only a straight-through estimator is unstable and collapses to trivial segmentations; it needed H-Net's ratio loss + EMA smoothing to work.
- Assuming a fast/slow recurrent hierarchy produces reasoning gains: HRM's H/L modules contributed only a few points; TRM (7M, no hierarchy) beat HRM (27M) on ARC-AGI-1 (45% vs ~40%/32% verified) via iterative refinement alone.
- Sentence-embedding-only autoregression (Large Concept Models) loses fine-grained fidelity and did not clearly beat token LLMs at equal scale.
- Byte-level hierarchies trade a measurable loss on math (Bolmo 48.9 vs OLMo 3 55.3) for character robustness; a hierarchy does not automatically preserve numeric competence.
- Backprop-free predictive-coding training has not produced a competitive LM: µPC scales depth (128 layers) only on MNIST/CIFAR; PC still trails BP in benchmark suites.
- Deep temporal AIF / renormalising generative models (RGM, AXIOM) have only been demonstrated on MNIST, short videos and toy games; no natural-language corpus result exists to build on.
- Fixed large patches (MegaByte-style patch 8/16) were outperformed by boundary-aware (SpaceByte) and learned (H-Net) segmentation at matched compute.

**Open gaps**

- No active-inference language model with expected-free-energy decoding has a reported bits-per-byte/perplexity at any scale; no comparison of EFE-based decoding vs sampling/beam on standard LM metrics.
- H-Net has not been shown beyond 2 stages or beyond ~GPT-3-XL compute; whether a 3+ level hierarchy (bytes->words->sentences) keeps improving is untested.
- No work links a connectome's intrinsic timescale hierarchy (neuron time constants, region-level recurrence such as MB vs CX) to the chunk/timescale structure of a language model.
- Precision (attention) and chunk boundaries are treated separately in all hierarchical LMs; no model lets estimated precision set the temporal resolution of a level.
- Dynamic-shape hierarchical models are FLOP-matched but rarely wall-clock-matched; the true training-cost premium of learned chunking vs BPE is not well quantified.
- Multi-frequency memory updates (HOPE CMS) and dynamic chunking have not been combined; no study of which update frequency should attach to which chunk level.
- The ARC Prize style ablation (hierarchy vs iterations vs augmentation) has not been run for byte-level hierarchical LMs; unclear how much of H-Net's gain is 'hierarchy' vs 'more layers at the byte level'.
- No results yet on whether surprise/entropy-triggered boundaries (BLT) and similarity-triggered boundaries (H-Net) produce different segmentations or generalisation.

**Key numbers**

- H-Net 2-stage XL: d_model [1024,1536,2048], layers m4 / T1m4 / T27 / m4T1 / m4, windows [1023,1023,full], vocab 256, Mamba2 d_state 128; 1-stage XL: m4 / T24 / m4, d [1024,2048]; trained on 100B-token FineWeb-Edu, compute-matched to GPT-3 Large/XL
- H-Net abstract: 1-stage byte H-Net > compute/data-matched BPE Transformer; 2-stage matches token Transformer of ~2x size; ~4x data efficiency on DNA
- BLT: scaling study to 8B params / 8T bytes (README; 4T in arXiv v1); Llama 3 tokens ~4.4 bytes, BLT patches ~6-8 bytes, patch 8 -> up to ~50% fewer inference FLOPs (recollection)
- Bolmo: byteification <1% of pretraining cost; Stage 1 9.8B tokens (~43B bytes), Stage 2 39.3B tokens (~173B bytes); Bolmo-7B 7.6B params; CUTE 78.6 vs OLMo 3 56.9 vs BLT 7B 52.3; EXECUTE 71.6 vs 55.1 vs 46.3; math 48.9 vs 55.3; code 41.0 vs 40.1
- EvaByte: 6.5B params, 1.5T bytes, 5x less data than tokenizer LMs, up to 2x faster decoding
- SpaceByte-793M+184M: 28 global layers d=1536, 26 local layers d=768, byte context 8192, global context 1344 (~6.1 bytes/global slot), window 768
- bGPT: 110M params, 12 patch layers + 3 byte layers, patch 16 bytes x 512, CPU emulation 99.99%, ABC->MIDI 0.0011 bpb
- MambaByte: 353M and 972M on PG19 (11.68B training bytes)
- MBLM: trains on 5M+ byte contexts; example stages seq [1024, 8], layers [5,5]
- Block Transformer: 10-20x decode throughput at equal perplexity, block length 4, 1.2B checkpoint
- ByteChunk (EMNLP 2026): zero-shot POS avg 36.9 -> 47.3 (+10.4) with H-Net router on frozen backbone; 97.7% of Bhojpuri words fragmented by subword tokenizer
- Parameter Golf 2026 (16 MB): byte260 H-Net 1.2070 BPB vs sp1024 H-Net 1.2107 vs baseline 1.2074 at 4 h; 1.4116 vs 1.3734 at 10 min
- HRM: 27M params, ~1000 training examples, claimed ARC-AGI-1 40.3% / ARC-AGI-2 5.0% (ARC Prize verified ~32% / ~2%, recollection); TRM: 7M params, ARC-AGI-1 45%, ARC-AGI-2 8%, Sudoku-Extreme ~87% (MLP) / ~75% (attention)
- HOPE reproduction: 1.721 bits/byte at 30.6M byte-level params; ppl ~81 at 161.5M BPE; no degradation at 4x training length; CMS levels update every 1 / 2 / 4 chunks
- LCM: 1.6B params on ~1.3T tokens (7B in paper); SONAR covers 200 text / 57 speech languages
- GeneZip: 70M-636M params, 12.8K and 128K contexts, eQTL fine-tuning 50.4x faster than JanusDNA (50 vs 2520 min)
- Brain multiscale prediction (Caucheteux 2023): optimal forecast horizon ~8 words
- µPC: predictive-coding nets trained to 128 layers, but only MNIST/Fashion-MNIST/CIFAR-10

## lit:efe-decoding

29 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Uncertainty of Thoughts (UoT, NeurIPS 2024): information-gain-driven question planning — the LLM simulates possible answers in a tree, scores candidate questions by an uncertainty/entropy-based reward with accumulated expected reward, and prunes nodes. Reported: +57.8% average improvement in task-completion success rate over direct prompting across multiple LLMs (GPT-3.5, GPT-4, Llama-2-70B, Mistral-Large, Gemini-1.0-pro) on 20 Questions, Medical Diagnosis and Troubleshooting; in open-set settings +54.9% (GPT-3.5) and +21.1% (GPT-4) over the DPOS baseline; possibility sets capped at 10,10,10,5,5,5 across rounds; also reduces number of questions needed. | [zhiyuanhubj/UoT README (NeurIPS 2024)](https://github.com/zhiyuanhubj/UoT) | 2024 | strong |
| CA-BED (Conversation-Aware Bayesian Experimental Design, ICLR 2026 workshop): inference-time dialog planning that combines BED with LLM-estimated likelihoods, propagating EIG through a simulated conversation tree while modelling ambiguous/partial answers. Reported +21.8% average success-rate gain over direct prompting on Detective Cases (AR-Bench) and Twenty Questions (UoT benchmark) at a cost of only +1.8 turns on average; performance comparable to UoT. | [DanielArnould/ca-bed README](https://github.com/DanielArnould/ca-bed) | 2026 | moderate |
| STaR-GATE: iteratively fine-tunes a Questioner on questions that raise the probability of high-quality (oracle) responses, using 25,500 synthetic persona-task prompts; after two self-improvement iterations its responses were preferred in 72% of tasks vs the initial model (GPT-4 judged). | [scandukuri/assistant-gate README (STaR-GATE, arXiv 2403.19154)](https://github.com/scandukuri/assistant-gate) | 2024 | strong |
| GATE (Generative Active Task Elicitation): LMs generate open-ended/yes-no questions and edge cases to elicit user preferences in email validation, content recommendation and moral reasoning; in preregistered experiments LM-driven elicitation was often more informative than user-written prompts/labels and rated as lower effort, but not uniformly better across domains. | [alextamkin/generative-elicitation README](https://github.com/alextamkin/generative-elicitation) | 2023 | moderate |
| ClarifyGPT: detects ambiguous code requirements via a code-consistency check (sampling multiple solutions and testing agreement), asks targeted clarifying questions only when inconsistent, then regenerates; GPT-4 Pass@1 on MBPP-sanitized rose from 70.96% to 80.80%. | [ClarifyGPT/ClarifyGPT README](https://github.com/ClarifyGPT/ClarifyGPT) | 2023 | strong |
| Active-VLM / FOVEA (2026): frames active visual reasoning as sequential Bayesian optimal experimental design, choosing crops that maximize a tractable EIG surrogate (coverage–resolution objective); training-free with Qwen3-VL-30B it reaches 77.7% aggregate over four high-resolution benchmarks vs Gemini 2.5 Flash 78.0%, and in remote-sensing counting narrows the gap to the human oracle from ~30 to ~13 points (54.7% vs 68%). | [iamlilAJ/active-vlm README](https://github.com/iamlilAJ/active-vlm) | 2026 | moderate |
| BoxingGym (2025): benchmark for LLM agents doing experimental design and model discovery across 10+ environments, scoring designs by EIG = E_y[KL(p(θ/y,x)//p(θ))] via nested Monte Carlo and an 'EIG regret' vs optimal designs. README gives no numbers; the paper (from memory) found current LLMs (incl. GPT-4o) select designs with substantial EIG regret and that adding an explicit statistical-model agent ('Box's Apprentice') did not reliably help. | [kanishkg/boxing-gym README](https://github.com/kanishkg/boxing-gym) | 2025 | moderate |
| The Entropy Mechanism of RL for reasoning LMs (2025): policy entropy collapses during RLVR, with performance fitting R = −a·exp(H) + b (entropy exhaustion bottleneck); collapse is driven by positive covariance between action log-prob and advantage. Clip-Cov / KL-Cov, which restrict updates on high-covariance tokens, give +2.0% (7B) and +6.4% (32B) average over GRPO, with +15.0/+14.6 on AIME24/AIME25 for the 32B model, while sustaining entropy >10x higher at convergence. | [PRIME-RL/Entropy-Mechanism-of-RL README](https://github.com/PRIME-RL/Entropy-Mechanism-of-RL) | 2025 | strong |
| Beyond the 80/20 Rule (NeurIPS 2025): only a minority (~20%) of high-entropy 'forking' tokens steer reasoning; restricting policy-gradient updates to the top-20% entropy tokens improves RLVR (from memory: Qwen3-32B +11.04 AIME'25 and +7.71 AIME'24 over full-token GRPO; gains also on 14B, near parity on 8B), whereas training on the 80% low-entropy tokens degrades performance. The README itself does not list the numbers. | [Shenzhi-Wang/Beyond-the-80-20-Rule-RLVR README (arXiv 2506.01939)](https://github.com/Shenzhi-Wang/Beyond-the-80-20-Rule-RLVR) | 2025 | moderate |
| EMPO (NeurIPS 2025 spotlight): fully unsupervised RL that minimizes semantic entropy (entropy over meaning clusters of sampled answers) on unlabeled queries; matches supervised GSPO: Qwen-7B average 51.6 vs 51.7 (MATH500 81.4 vs 82.4, AIME24 23.3 vs 23.3, AMC23 65.0 vs 60.0), Qwen-1.5B 42.6 vs 42.8. | [QingyangZhang/EMPO README](https://github.com/QingyangZhang/EMPO) | 2025 | strong |
| Entropy-minimization family (RENT, 'Unreasonable Effectiveness of Entropy Minimization' EM-FT/EM-RL/EM-INF, One-shot EM, INTUITOR self-certainty, RLSC): unsupervised objectives that reward low token/semantic entropy or self-confidence improve math reasoning on Qwen2.5 models; EM-INF does inference-time logit optimization with a threshold, KL weight and adaptive temperature. Repos give no numbers in READMEs. | [satrams/rent-rl; shivamag125/EM_PT; falonss703/Awesome-Uncertainty-based-Reinforcement-Lea](https://github.com/shivamag125/EM_PT) | 2025 | moderate |
| Spurious Rewards (2025): RLVR with random, incorrect or format-only rewards yields large MATH-500 gains on Qwen2.5-Math (from memory: random reward ~+21 points vs ~+29 for ground-truth on the 7B model) but little or none on Llama-3 / OLMo; attributed to eliciting pre-existing code-reasoning behaviour plus GRPO clipping bias. README lists reward types (math, box_only_format, contain_python_wo_backticks, random0.5) but not numbers. | [ruixin31/Spurious_Rewards README (arXiv 2506.10947)](https://github.com/ruixin31/Spurious_Rewards) | 2025 | moderate |
| DEER (Dynamic Early Exit in Reasoning, 2025): at reasoning transition points the model emits a trial answer; if its confidence exceeds a threshold (0.95 in examples; geometric mean for over-confident Qwen3) generation stops. Across 11 reasoning models: CoT length reduced 19.1%–80.1% with accuracy improved 0.3%–5.0% at 16k budgets. | [iie-ycx/DEER README](https://github.com/iie-ycx/DEER) | 2025 | strong |
| DeepConf (Meta, 2025): uses token-level confidence (window-averaged negative entropy / low-probability signals) to early-stop weak traces online (warm-up traces set thresholds) and to weight/filter votes offline; from memory reaches 99.9% on AIME 2025 with gpt-oss-120b and reduces generated tokens by up to 84.7%. README confirms mechanism but not numbers. | [facebookresearch/deepconf README (arXiv 2508.15260)](https://github.com/facebookresearch/deepconf) | 2025 | moderate |
| DoLa (ICLR 2024): contrasting final-layer vs earlier-layer logits improves factuality without retrieval/fine-tuning; LLaMA-family TruthfulQA (MC) improves by 12–17 absolute points. SLED (NeurIPS 2024) extends this with 'self logits evolution' at negligible overhead across 2B–70B models (LLaMA-2/3, Gemma, MoE), reporting gains over greedy and DoLa on TruthfulQA, FACTOR, GSM8K, StrategyQA (numbers not in README). | [voidism/DoLa README; jayzhang42/SLED README](https://github.com/voidism/DoLa) | 2024 | strong |
| Semantic entropy (Farquhar et al., Nature 2024): entropy over meaning-equivalence clusters of sampled answers detects confabulations across LLaMA-2 (7B–70B), Falcon, Mistral on TriviaQA, SQuAD, BioASQ, NQ, SVAMP; from memory AUROC ≈0.79 vs ≈0.70 for naive entropy/p(True) baselines; README confirms setup but not numbers. | [jlko/semantic_uncertainty README](https://github.com/jlko/semantic_uncertainty) | 2024 | moderate |
| FLARE (Active Retrieval Augmented Generation, 2023): forecasts the next sentence, and if any token's probability falls below a threshold, retrieves with the forecast as query before regenerating; the README confirms the mechanism; the paper reports gains over single-time and multi-time retrieval on four long-form tasks (numbers not in README). | [jzbjyb/FLARE README](https://github.com/jzbjyb/FLARE) | 2023 | moderate |
| Locally typical sampling (Meister et al., TACL 2023) selects tokens whose information content is close to the conditional entropy (τ mass); Mirostat (ICLR 2021) feedback-controls sampling so the running surprise tracks a target τ, avoiding the repetition ('boredom') and incoherence ('confusion') traps. Both are information-theoretic decoders reported (from memory) to match or beat nucleus sampling in human evaluations and reduce repetition; READMEs give no numbers. | [cimeister/typical-sampling; basusourya/mirostat](https://github.com/basusourya/mirostat) | 2021-2023 | moderate |
| Entropix (2024): community sampler that switches sampling regimes by logit entropy and varentropy (low/low → greedy; high entropy → careful; high varentropy → branch). Two years on, the README still reports no rigorous benchmarks ('initial eval results look incredible', 'NOT A FINISHED PRODUCT'). | [xjdr-alt/entropix README](https://github.com/xjdr-alt/entropix) | 2024 | weak |
| pymdp / deep active inference lineage: EFE = risk + ambiguity (or −pragmatic −epistemic) with policy π = σ(−γG); canonical implementations are discrete-state and enumerate policies. Scaling work (Ueltzhöffer 2018; Tschantz et al. 2020 'Scaling active inference'; Millidge 2020 'Deep active inference as variational policy gradients' which amortizes the EFE value function) demonstrated the epistemic term's exploratory value but only on small control tasks with explicit transition models. The BerenMillidge FEP paper list contains no LLM/language entries. | [infer-actively/pymdp; BerenMillidge/FEP_Active_Inference_Papers](https://github.com/BerenMillidge/FEP_Active_Inference_Papers) | 2018-2020 | strong |
| GitHub-wide code search (Sept 2026) finds zero repositories combining 'expected_free_energy' with transformers / vLLM / AutoModelForCausalLM; all EFE code hits are discrete pymdp-style agents, hobby cognitive architectures, or LLM-orchestration wrappers that compute EFE over a handful of LLM-proposed 'policies' (e.g. NeuralBlitz/lrs-agents, naderloocodelab/micro-actinf, ISE-Lab-AI4LIFE/AgentTroop, 0xLian117/RLproj). | [GitHub code search: "expected_free_energy" transformers OR vllm; "expected free energy" "l](https://github.com/search?q=%22expected_free_energy%22+transformers&type=code) | 2026 | strong |
| LLM-orchestration EFE wrappers: lrs-agents scores 3–7 LLM-proposed tool policies by G = epistemic − precision-weighted pragmatic with a Beta-updated precision γ (start 0.5, η_gain 0.1, η_loss 0.2, re-explore when γ<0.4) and claims '94% task completion' and '98% optimal policy, 127x faster than exhaustive search' with no public benchmark; micro-actinf is a 20.75 KB C EFE filter over six engineering regimes (1.677 µs/decision) with no task evaluation; AgentTroop uses EFE (KL between beliefs before/after a probe) to pick red-team interventions on RMCBench (473 prompts) but reports no ASR numbers in its README. | [NeuralBlitz/lrs-agents; naderloocodelab/micro-actinf; ISE-Lab-AI4LIFE/AgentTroop](https://github.com/NeuralBlitz/lrs-agents) | 2025-2026 | speculative |
| ActiveCircuitDiscovery (2025): a pymdp POMDP agent uses EFE scoring to choose which transcoder features to intervene on in Gemma-2-2B / Llama-3.2-1B attribution graphs; vs random selection: +16.3% (IOI, 5 prompts, 58.3% oracle efficiency) and +30.4% (multi-step, 3 prompts, 73.3% oracle efficiency); 17/100 interventions significant (p<1e-15). | [SharathSPhD/ActiveCIrcuitDiscovery README](https://github.com/SharathSPhD/ActiveCIrcuitDiscovery) | 2025 | weak |
