No language component; README/gameworld repo contain no comparative numbers. | [VersesTech/axiom README (arXiv 2505.24784); VersesTech/gameworld](https://github.com/VersesTech/axiom) | 2025 | moderate |
| Position literature: Pezzulo, Parr, Cisek, Clark & Friston (TiCS 2024, 'Generating meaning: active inference and the scope and limits of passive AI') argue LLMs are passive predictors lacking the action–perception loop that makes epistemic value meaningful; Duraisamy (2025, 'Active Inference AI Systems for Scientific Discovery') and Prakki (arXiv 2412.10425, 'Active Inference for Self-Organizing Multi-LLM Systems') propose EFE-organized LLM systems with, at most, small illustrative demos. None report benchmarked gains. | [Trends in Cognitive Sciences 2024; arXiv 2506.21329; arXiv 2412.10425 (not fetchable in th](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10425) | 2024-2025 | weak |
| Survey of agent exploration (2026) classifies LLM-agent exploration into uncertainty-, competence- and reachability-driven methods and lists uncertainty-driven works (TreeRL from uncertain intermediate states, Beyond-80/20 forking tokens, KnowSelf uncertainty routing, PSRL-style textual posterior sampling, Search-o1 uncertainty-triggered retrieval); no active-inference/EFE LLM method is listed, indicating none has reached the mainstream literature. | [banyikun/agent_exploration README (survey)](https://github.com/banyikun/agent_exploration) | 2026 | moderate |
| RLproj (2026 course project) replaces RLVE's heuristic difficulty schedule with an EFE controller: G(d) = λ_pref·(−log U_K(p̂)) − λ_info·I(s;o/d) + λ_cost·C(d), sampling difficulty via π(d) ∝ exp(−G(d)/T) from ProRL-1.5B-v2 on one GPU; no results reported. autoresearcher2 uses 2–3 step EFE (risk + ambiguity) policy scoring for an ML-research agent and beat random search on synthetic environments (6.5 vs 17.5 regret, 20 seeds; 95–100% factor-ranking accuracy) with real-hardware comparisons still 'in progress'. | [0xLian117/RLproj; ErikDeBruijn/autoresearcher2 READMEs](https://github.com/ErikDeBruijn/autoresearcher2) | 2026 | weak |

**Usable ideas**

- Sparse EFE decoding: compute the pragmatic+epistemic score only at high-entropy 'forking' tokens (~20% of positions per Beyond-80/20; entropy/varentropy gates as in Entropix/DeepConf) and decode greedily elsewhere; this bounds the cost of any lookahead/rollout-based epistemic term.
- Turn-level epistemic value via simulated futures (UoT/CA-BED recipe): maintain an explicit, capped possibility set (<=10 hypotheses) over user intent/latent state, generate N candidate continuations, simulate J steps of answers, score by expected entropy reduction; use it for 'ask vs answer' and 'retrieve vs continue' decisions rather than per-token.
- Use semantic (meaning-cluster) entropy, not token entropy, as the ambiguity/epistemic quantity (Farquhar 2024; EMPO); sample-consistency checks (ClarifyGPT) are a cheap proxy that already yields ~10-point task gains when used to trigger clarification.
- Treat precision (gamma) as an online-controlled variable: Mirostat-style feedback to hold running surprise at a target tau, or Beta-updated confidence with asymmetric gain/loss rates and a re-explore threshold (lrs-agents sketch); pre-register gamma schedules rather than hand-tune.
- If the EFE policy is trained by RL, add an entropy floor / covariance clipping (Clip-Cov, KL-Cov) — otherwise the pragmatic term collapses entropy (R = −a·exp(H)+b) and the epistemic term is learned away.
- Confidence-gated compute as the pragmatic proxy for 'stop': DEER-style early exit (threshold ~0.95 on a trial answer) and DeepConf-style confidence-weighted voting are proven, cheap, and map onto an EFE 'terminate' action.
- Uncertainty-triggered epistemic actions (FLARE, Search-o1, KnowSelf): retrieval, questioning or memory lookup fire only when local surprise exceeds a threshold; treat these as actions with EIG-minus-cost scoring.
- Layer-contrast logit corrections (DoLa/SLED) as a near-free factual pragmatic prior to add to the token distribution before EFE scoring.
- Amortize EFE: train a small head to predict the epistemic term (Millidge 2020 style) from hidden state instead of rolling out; validate against rollout-based EIG on a held-out set.
- Pure entropy-minimization (EM-INF logit refinement, EMPO/RENT training) and typical/Mirostat sampling must be included as baselines; an EFE decoder must beat 'exploit-only' and 'surprise-regulated' decoders, not just nucleus sampling.
- Control for prior-specific gains (Spurious Rewards lesson): test any epistemic/entropy signal on at least two model families or connectome-prior vs shuffled-prior variants; random/format rewards are the null comparator.
- EFE for experiment/curriculum scheduling around the model (RLproj, autoresearcher2): select which hypotheses/difficulties to run next by risk + ambiguity + cost; cheap to add to the existing harness.

**Dead ends**

- Exact token-level EFE with a full latent posterior and enumerated policies (pymdp style): no public implementation for transformer LMs exists (GitHub code search returns none), and deep active inference scaled only to small control tasks with explicit transition models (Tschantz 2020, Millidge 2020) — policy enumeration is exponential and needs amortization.
- Entropix-style entropy/varentropy regime switching: two years without benchmarks; heuristic thresholds, no demonstrated gain.
- Bio-inspired mechanism stacking without ablations (bio_inspired_nanochat 2026): fatigue, fast/slow weights, neuromodulators, uncertainty-gated decoding etc. produced null/negative pilots at toy scale with 4–18x overhead.
- Coupling an EFE-driven latent world model to a frozen strong LLM via token biases (pratyabhijna H5b): no lift over the bare LLM on text (effect size −0.47).
- Relying on the LLM itself to estimate EIG / optimal designs (BoxingGym): LLM agents show high EIG regret and adding an explicit statistician agent did not reliably help; compute EIG outside the LM.
- Pure entropy-minimization / self-certainty rewards as evidence of 'epistemic' benefit: gains overlap with those from random or incorrect rewards on Qwen2.5-Math and largely vanish on Llama/OLMo (Spurious Rewards); they also drive entropy collapse (Entropy Mechanism).
- LM-driven elicitation without an explicit information objective (GATE): not uniformly better than user-written prompts across domains.
- Rollout-based EIG at every token (UoT/CA-BED cost model N candidates x J depth x |possibility set| LLM calls per decision): unaffordable per token; must be sparse or amortized.
- Unbenchmarked EFE wrappers around LLM tool agents (lrs-agents '94% completion', micro-actinf) — claims without public baselines; do not cite as evidence.
- AXIOM/Verses-style explicit Bayesian EFE planning: validated only on pixel games (Gameworld 10k), not language.

**Open gaps**

- No published token-level EFE (pragmatic + epistemic) decoder for language models benchmarked against nucleus, typical, Mirostat, EM-INF and DoLa on standard LM/dialogue benchmarks.
- No evidence on open-domain dialogue quality: epistemic-term gains are only shown on constrained information-seeking tasks (20Q, diagnosis, troubleshooting, detective cases) and code clarification.
- No amortized EFE head for LMs (predicting expected information gain from hidden state) validated against rollout EIG.
- Semantic entropy vs token entropy vs varentropy as the decode-time epistemic quantity has not been compared head-to-head.
- Online precision (gamma) learning for LMs is unstudied beyond Mirostat's surprise control; no principled link between precision and prediction-error statistics in transformers.
- Interaction between an explicit epistemic bonus and RL entropy collapse (does EFE training resist the exp(H) bottleneck?) is uncharacterized.
- No work uses biologically derived priors (e.g. connectome-constrained recurrent cores) inside an EFE decoder; whether structured priors change exploration/epistemic behaviour is untested.
- Cost-aware 'ask vs act' objectives (EIG per token/turn cost) are only partly addressed (CA-BED reports +1.8 turns); no standard metric.
- Coverage caveat for this sweep: the WebSearch budget was exhausted and arXiv/HF/OpenReview/ACL hosts were blocked, so verification relied on GitHub READMEs; numbers marked 'moderate' or 'weak' are from memory of the papers and should be re-checked before citing.

**Key numbers**

- UoT: +57.8% average success-rate improvement over direct prompting; open-set +54.9% (GPT-3.5), +21.1% (GPT-4) vs DPOS; possibility set capped 10/10/10/5/5/5
- CA-BED: +21.8% success over direct prompting for +1.8 turns (Detective Cases, 20Q)
- STaR-GATE: 72% preference after 2 iterations; 25,500 persona-task prompts
- ClarifyGPT: GPT-4 MBPP-sanitized pass@1 70.96% -> 80.80%
- Active-VLM S-BOED: 77.7% (Qwen3-VL-30B, training-free) vs Gemini 2.5 Flash 78.0%; remote-sensing gap to oracle 30 -> 13 points (54.7% vs 68%)
- Entropy Mechanism: R = -a*exp(H)+b; Clip-Cov/KL-Cov +2.0% (7B), +6.4% (32B); AIME24/25 +15.0/+14.6 (32B); entropy >10x higher
- Beyond 80/20: ~20% high-entropy tokens; top-20% training ~ +11.04 AIME'25 / +7.71 AIME'24 on Qwen3-32B (from memory)
- EMPO (semantic-entropy minimization, unsupervised): Qwen-7B avg 51.6 vs supervised GSPO 51.7; 1.5B 42.6 vs 42.8
- DEER: CoT length -19.1% to -80.1%, accuracy +0.3 to +5.0 across 11 models; confidence threshold 0.95
- DeepConf (memory): 99.9% AIME 2025 with gpt-oss-120b, up to 84.7% fewer tokens
- DoLa: +12-17 absolute on TruthfulQA MC (LLaMA family)
- Semantic entropy (memory): AUROC ~0.79 vs ~0.70 baselines
- ActiveCircuitDiscovery: EFE agent +16.3% (IOI) and +30.4% (multi-step) over random feature selection, N=5 and 3 prompts
- pratyabhijna: EFE actor 29.72x reward vs REINFORCE; LLM bridge effect size -0.47 (negative)
- bio_inspired_nanochat: max 2 layers x 64 dims; fast-weights dECE ~ +0.03; NAS regressed loss 8/8 seeds; 4-18x slower
- autoresearcher2 (synthetic): regret 6.5 vs 17.5 random, 20 seeds
- lrs-agents precision rule: gamma0=0.5, eta_gain=0.1, eta_loss=0.2, re-explore when gamma<0.4 (unvalidated)
- micro-actinf: 20.75 KB, 1.677 us/decision, 596,422 decisions/s (no task eval)
- AXIOM (memory): beats DreamerV3/BBF within 10k steps with ~0.3-1.6M params; planning_horizon 16, rollouts 16 in reduced config
- GitHub code search: 0 repos with expected_free_energy + transformers/vLLM

Adding recordings from a subset of neurons collapses the degeneracy; the number needed scales with the dimensionality D of the dynamics, not N (e.g., ~7 recorded neurons suffice for 2-D dynamics in a 1000-neuron network; grows ~N for high-dimensional chaos). **Usable ideas**

- Decide the mask question by parameterisation, not ideology: use flyvis's recipe — W = count x sign x alpha[type_pre, type_post] with per-type time constants/biases — as the connectome INITIALISER/TEMPLATE (734 params for 64 types; ~10^4-10^5 for MaleCNS's 11,710 types), then add a learnable low-rank deviation (BrainTrace: W_conn + A B^T, rank ~20) instead of a per-edge unstructured mask. This keeps the count/sign information Shiu 2024 shows is predictive (84-91%) while avoiding the 46x unstructured-sparse training penalty seen in fly-as-a-lm.
- If you do keep a hard MaleCNS mask, schedule it: start dense (or block-dense by cell type) and anneal toward the connectome mask between 25% and 75% of the compute budget (Journey Matters), because loss tracks the average parameter count over training; enforcing the mask from step 0 minimises that average.
- Budget the sparse penalty explicitly: learned sparsity at 75% only equals a dense model with ~2-2.2x its non-zero params (Frantar), dynamic sparse training needs ~5x the steps to reach dense accuracy (RigL), and fixed masks lag learned masks by ~5-7 pp (RigL static) or 2-3x perplexity (MaskLLM vs SparseGPT). Plan the hard-mask arm with >=2-5x tokens relative to the dense arm, or expect it to lose at matched compute.
- Enforce Dale signs whenever the core is sparse: NCAP found sparse+unconstrained signs failed to learn while sparse+E/I learned and dense learned; use a DANN-style (subtractive inhibition) parameterisation to avoid the performance loss of naive column sign constraints. fly-as-a-lm learned signs unconstrained — one plausible reason its mask gave nothing.
- Build the control ladder used by cqw-acq/connectome-null-models and the nematode project: (1) degree-preserving directed edge swaps that ALSO preserve the contact-count magnitude paired to each stub (or re-randomise magnitudes in both arms), (2) Erdos-Renyi, (3) weight-shuffle on real topology, (4) cell-type-only / SBM null (Fly.exe found type identity carried AUROC 0.797 of 0.808), (5) no-recurrence floor; pin spectral radius (0.95) and match firing/activation rates (Fly.exe's 9 Hz vs 56 Hz confound) and input->readout reachability; pre-register n>=8 paired seeds (single-seed reversals documented twice).
- Choose sub-circuits by eigencircuit analysis (Pospisil): the connectome's dominant linear modes are tiny local circuits (lobula plate opponent motion; ellipsoid-body winner-take-all). Extract MB and CX motifs as modules with Dale signs, rather than masking the whole 166.7k-node recurrence — this is the NCAP/NCP/FlyHash abstraction level where priors have actually paid off.
- Give anatomical interfaces baseline-matched capacity: routing readout through the 97 MBONs cost +0.6 nat/token vs random ports; if the MB module writes to an EFE/decoding head, widen the readout and treat MBON count as a design hint, not a constraint.
- Cheap replication that would settle your H5-type question: rerun fly-as-a-lm's central-brain N5,600 comparison (real 88.45 vs shuffled 124.89 ppl; 6/6 seeds) with a magnitude-preserving, self-loop-free, spectral-radius-matched shuffle and a same-size GRU/Transformer; if the 46.9% gap survives, that is the first robust wiring effect in an LM setting; if not, it was an initialisation artefact.
- Use the connectome where it is strongest as a prior: initial-state geometry (untrained CC networks differ from random, r=-0.03 vs 0.84) and count-derived magnitudes; treat it as a KL/L2 regulariser toward the count-initialised weights rather than a mask, and measure regulariser strength as a hyperparameter (0 = template only, inf = hard mask).
- Report solution degeneracy (Beiran & Litwin-Kumar): with no activity data, any trained connectome-constrained LM is one of many; never present the trained weights as 'the fly's'; if any biological claim is made, add a small recorded-activity constraint (D-dimensional dynamics need ~D+ recorded neurons).

**Dead ends**

- Frozen whole-connectome reservoir as a language model: MaleCNS 211k-neuron echo-state LM reached 3.644 bpc vs 2.266 for a 6M-param transformer, tied with shuffled (3.629) and random-graph (3.546) reservoirs; usable memory ~1 token (decay 0.3^lag). Reason: leak/gain-limited memory, not wiring.
- Frozen connectome as side-channel to a frozen LLM (FLM adapter, 278k params over 166.7k nodes): parameter-matched direct-input adapter did slightly better. Reason: the adapter, not the graph, carries the information.
- MaleCNS as hard mask with trained per-edge weights and free signs (16,384-neuron optic-lobe-biased subset): identical to degree-preserving rewiring (45.21% vs 45.21%), ~3.3x worse perplexity than a parameter-matched GRU (187 vs 56.5) and ~46x slower per run. Reason: unstructured sparsity is neither a good inductive bias nor hardware-efficient at this scale.
- Static random masks fixed at initialisation: ~5-7 pp below RigL at equal FLOPs on ImageNet; lottery-ticket masks found at init fail at ImageNet scale; one-shot heuristic 2:4 masks are 2-3x worse perplexity than learned masks on LLaMA. Reason: which connections are kept must co-adapt with the weights.
- Whole-connectome-as-topology RL controllers (FlyGM, FLYNN) and larval-connectome 'Biological Processing Units' (98% vs 97% MNIST): reported gains disappear under from-scratch initialisation and degree-preserving nulls (Dhiman 2026; cqw-acq: +0.09 pp, CI [-0.20,+0.38], n=15). Reason: prior comparisons used pretrained checkpoints or lacked rewired controls.
- Real vs degree-matched wiring in trained flyvis and trained C. elegans policies: statistically indistinguishable on task and on biological-fidelity metrics (connectome-fidelity p=0.41; nematode delta -3.28, CI spans 0, n=8). Reason: connectome under-constrains dynamics once weights are re-optimised (Beiran & Litwin-Kumar).
- Naive column-wise Dale constraints and sparse-but-sign-free architectures (Cornford 2021; NCAP ablation): both learn poorly; only the combination sparse+signed (NCAP) or DANN-parameterised signs recovers performance.
- Routing LM I/O through published anatomical ports (313 ALPN in, 97 MBON out): worse than random ports in 6/6 seeds (median -0.63 nat/token). Reason: readout capacity bottleneck.
- Anatomy-only dynamics as ground truth: Shiu's LIF fails on peptidergic (Usnea) and disinhibition cases; Randi 2023 shows extrasynaptic signalling makes anatomy mispredict function; gap junctions and neuromodulation are absent from every connectome-scale model as of Sept 2026.
- Single-seed or n=5 topology comparisons: nematode smoke (57.2 vs 46.5) reversed at n=8; cqw-acq CIFAR +0.35 pp (p=0.078, d=1.28) fell to +0.09 pp (p=0.51) at pre-committed n=15 — winner's curse.
- Verification limits of this sweep: WebSearch budget was exhausted before the task and Nature/arXiv/OpenReview/HF hosts are blocked, so Frantar's fitted coefficients, the exact in-paper controls of Lappalainen 2024, NCAP parameter counts and RigL's static-mask row were taken from third-party transcriptions or memory (flagged 'moderate').

**Open gaps**

- No published or repo study fits the sparse scaling law with an EXOGENOUS (biological or random) mask: the (1-S)^{b_S} penalty for a MaleCNS mask vs magnitude-learned sparsity at matched N, D and compute is unmeasured, so the 'compute penalty to budget' has no direct estimate beyond RigL-static (vision) and one 46x wall-clock anecdote.
- No matched-compute comparison of the three regimes for one connectome — hard mask vs count-initialised regulariser vs type-level topology template (SBM/block) — on any language task; every LM-side result to date is either frozen-reservoir or hard-mask-with-free-signs.
- It is unverified whether Lappalainen 2024's own trained DMN beats an equally trained degree-preserving-rewired DMN on the paper's tuning metrics; the two 2026 re-analyses are short-horizon (5-10 steps) or unpublished, and none pins spectral radius or activity level.
- No standard null preserves contact-count magnitudes, spectral radius, activity rate and input->output reachability simultaneously; the only positive LM wiring effect (central-brain N5,600, 46.9% ppl reduction) used a shuffle that breaks magnitude pairing and allows self-loops, and has no same-scale transformer baseline or fresh-corpus test.
- Whether the cell-type block structure alone (11,710 MaleCNS types; an SBM null) reproduces any advantage attributed to exact wiring has not been tested — the decisive experiment for a 'topology template' design (Fly.exe's cell-type-only AUROC 0.797 vs 0.808 is the only hint).
- No MaleCNS-based (as opposed to FlyWire) LIF or rate model has a validation comparable to Shiu's 91%/164 predictions; MaleCNS transmitter-sign and count priors are unvalidated for dynamics.
- Whether count-derived magnitude priors help when signs are learned vs fixed by transmitter is untested (fly-as-a-lm learned signs; Shiu fixed them; NCAP says signs matter).
- Hardware-efficient realisations of a connectome prior (cell-type block-sparse or Kronecker/Monarch-style templates, 2:4 within blocks) have not been built; all MaleCNS LM attempts used unstructured CSR/dense recurrence.
- MB-motif utility beyond embeddings/hashing (FlyHash 2017, fly word embeddings 2021) is unmeasured for autoregressive LMs at scale, including the value of APL-style k-WTA sparsity and compartmentalised three-factor writes for associative recall (your H1/MQAR line).
- Active-inference-specific gap: no work couples a connectome-constrained recurrent core to precision-weighted prediction errors or expected-free-energy decoding; the degeneracy result implies such a core needs activity- or behaviour-level constraints to be identifiable.

**Key numbers**

- flyvis: 64 cell types, 45,669 neurons, 1,513,231 synapses, 721 columns, 734 free params, dt 20 ms, 50-model ensemble
- MaleCNS v1.0: 166,700 neurons, 11,710 types, 25,582,938 directed pairs, 124,177,617 contacts; edge density ~0.09%; released 2026-06-08, Cell paper 2026-09-03
- Shiu 2024 LIF: 127,400 neurons, 52.8M synapses, w_syn 0.275 mV, v_th -45 mV vs rest -52 mV (~25 coincident inputs), 91% of 164 predictions (84% excl. screen), 4.4 s CPU per simulated second; Loihi-2: 12-54 ms per simulated second
- fly-connectome-lm: transformer 2.266 bpc vs real connectome 3.644 vs shuffled 3.629 vs random graph 3.546 vs bigram 3.572; memory probe 27.3% (prior 14.6%) at lag 0, prior by lag 2
- fly-as-a-lm BPE WikiText: anatomical ppl 187.04 vs rewired 196.88 vs frozen 384.02 vs GRU 56.53 vs Transformer 65.32 (~2.25M params each); 25.85 min vs 0.56 min
- fly-as-a-lm central brain N5,600 TinyStories: real 88.45 vs shuffled 124.89 ppl; 6/6 seeds, ratio 0.5314 (46.9% reduction), p=0.03125; optic-lobe subset 57.10 vs 57.64 (no gap)
- Anatomy ports: ALPN->MBON 6.1925 nat vs random ports 5.5975 (median -0.6255 nat/token, 6/6 seeds)
- FLM: 278,528-param adapter; direct-input control 'slightly better'
- cqw-acq nulls: MNIST 97.30 vs DP 97.28 vs ER 97.53; CIFAR-10 43.04 vs 42.95 (+0.09 pp, CI [-0.20,+0.38], n=15); 99.9% edges moved; spectral radius pinned 0.95
- connectome-fidelity: untrained CC-vs-DP RDM r=-0.032 (within-CC 0.838); trained CC vs DP p=0.41, vs ER p=0.61
- Nematode: wild-type 52.78% vs rewired 56.06% (n=8, CI [-8.56,+1.61]); single seed 57.2 vs 46.5 reversed; ranking MLP 89.0 > CfC 75.8 ~ Transformer 74.0 > LSTM 60.1 > connectome 52.2
- Fly.exe: AUROC exact 0.8077 vs shuffled 0.500 vs cell-type-only 0.7972; activity confound 9 Hz vs 56 Hz
- Frantar 2024: 75% sparsity ~ dense with 2-2.2x non-zero params; optimal sparsity rises with D; pruning pretrained > sparse-from-scratch
- Journey Matters 2025: prune from 25% to 75% of compute is near-optimal among 80 schedules; loss follows average parameter count
- RigL ResNet-50: dense 76.8; 1x ERK 75.12 (80%) / 73.07 (90%); 5x ERK 77.17 (80%), 75.9 (90%), 74.36 (95%); static masks ~70.6/65.8 (memory)
- MaskLLM 2:4: LLaMA-2 7B 6.78 vs SparseGPT 10.42 vs dense 5.12; LLaMA-3 8B 8.49 vs 17.64 vs 5.75; masks learned on 2B tokens
- Pospisil effectome: top-10 eigenvectors ~50 neurons (<0.05%), 100th-1000th ~1,500; 1000th eigenvalue ~1/10 of largest; eigvec 1: 75% synapses in lobula plate; eigvec 45: 99% in ellipsoid body
- Beiran & Litwin-Kumar: recordings needed scale with dynamics dimension D not N (~7 neurons for 2-D dynamics in N=1000)
- Randi 2023: 23,433 stimulated pairs; anatomy-based predictions differ from measured propagation
- BrainTrace: LoRA rank 20 over FlyWire scaffold, synaptic scale 0.000825 mV, eligibility decay 0.99
- MB: ~50 PN types -> ~2,000 KCs (40x expansion, ~6 inputs each), ~5% active under APL, 15 DAN compartments; FlyHash ~20x cheaper projection; BioVSS >50x speedup at ~99% recall
- MaleCNS reservoir tagging: F1 0.2753 (real) vs 0.2782 (shuffled) vs 0.2727 (byte-only); tic-tac-toe 0.854 vs rewired 0.850

**Usable ideas**

- Represent every circular/periodic latent as a single-harmonic sinusoid over 8–16 columns (a 2-vector): phase = value, amplitude = precision (von Mises kappa). Posterior fusion of any number of cues is then plain vector addition of natural parameters (Kutschireiter 2023; Sun 2020; Mitchell 2023), and no separate 'precision head' is needed.
- Use an amplitude-free (plane-like) attractor instead of a fixed-amplitude ring: cosine recurrent kernel with gain w = alpha_tilde + 1/(kappa_phi+kappa_y), threshold-linear units, Delta7-like global inhibition, and a slow amplitude leak dkappa ≈ -(kappa^2-kappa)/(kappa_phi+kappa_v) dt as the 'forgetting/diffusion' prior. This makes the precision variable an emergent state, readable as |Σ r_i e^{i phi_i}|.
- Build the vector ALU from the FB motifs: (a) PFN-like offset copies of a latent at +/-45 deg whose amplitude is gated by a scalar drive (velocity, wind, evidence) — a 2-basis encoder; (b) hDelta-like 180-deg projections for subtraction/negation (with a nonlinearity, cf. Ishida 2025); (c) FC-like goal registers updated as leaky weighted means (2026 olfactory paper); (d) PFL3-like comparators with +/-67.5 deg offsets whose left-minus-right sum ≈ A_goal·A_state·sin(goal-state) gives a precision-weighted error-to-action signal; (e) PFL2-like 180-deg unit for unsigned error magnitude to set action gain/speed (policy precision).
- Instantiate the EFE/action-selection output as two antagonistic scalar channels (DNa02-style see-saw, R^2=0.51 linear map to turning) rather than a high-dimensional policy head; Franconville 2018: recurrent hub, low-dimensional output.
- Learn likelihood mappings (features -> ring phase) with the Basnak 2024 anti-Hebbian inhibitory rule dW = -1·r⊗g + 1·r⊗1 - 17·W·(r⊗1), eta=5e-5, gated by |velocity| (i.e., learn only during state change); this replaces backprop for the sensory-to-latent map and gives plasticity a built-in precision gate (dopamine during turns, Fisher 2022).
- Calibrate the transition/shift operator with Vafidis' local delta rule ΔW = eta·[f(V_teacher) - f(pV_prediction)]·P_pre — a predictive-coding rule using the allothetic channel as teacher; expect D≈25 deg^2/s-class drift and use an Oja/homeostatic rule (Aceituno 2024) to keep the cosine kernel within the ~2% tolerance.
- Let context/policy selection be gating of vector registers, not a separate controller: FB tangential-like units (state, reward, novelty) multiplicatively scale specific PFN/hDelta pathways (Hulse 2021); MB novelty (MBON) sets the weight of the memory-based cue and flips a mutually exclusive SN1/SN2 switch between strategies (Sun 2020) — a discrete policy switch with a continuous precision knob.
- Goal/prior learning as a reward-weighted vector mean over heading columns (Dan 2024, alphaW=0.001 vs alphaH=0.05): the goal bump amplitude then automatically encodes goal confidence, which multiplies the steering drive (Mussells Pires 2024).
- Exploit inhibition-profile knobs for update dynamics: global (fly-type) inhibition gives fast, abrupt bump jumps to new evidence (good for discrete token-level state changes), local Gaussian inhibition gives slower, noise-tolerant drift (Pisokas 2020, FWHM 88 vs 68 deg).
- Exploration without an explicit epistemic term: couple the steering primitive to an oscillator and let forward-speed/gain modulate exploration vs exploitation (Freas & Wystrach 2026); or use an attractor-based planner over future slots (Jensen 2026) that needs no plasticity at plan time.
- Use N=8 columns per latent (Noorman 2024; Aceituno 2024) — the CX's 8-fold symmetry is sufficient for accurate continuous integration, so the ring latent can be tiny; spend parameters on the number of registers, not columns per register.

**Dead ends**

- Classical fixed-amplitude/fixed-width ring attractors (Amari/Zhang) cannot represent certainty: bump width is set by the recurrent kernel and independent of input strength; certainty must live in amplitude, which requires an amplitude-free attractor (Kutschireiter 2023) — do not bolt a scalar 'precision' onto a normalised ring.
- Continuous attractors without a learning/homeostatic rule drift: ~2% weight mismatch across ~8 neurons destroys accuracy; Vafidis' network needed ~20 simulated hours to converge and then had to be degraded with noise (sigma~1.5 → D from 24.5 to 82 deg^2/s) to match flies — over-precise integrators are a modelling artefact.
- Discrete small rings without proper kernel become discrete attractors: 60-unit Vafidis network shows bump hopping to adjacent attractors; fly-style global inhibition settles with 30-40 deg dispersion after cue removal (Pisokas) — not a free lunch.
- Positing ring attractors in the FB (Sun 2020) was rejected by anatomists ('no known ring attractors in the FB'); EB->FB direct links and the POTu compass route lack support; the model also needed condition-specific TUN parameters (zero- vs full-vector) and had an ANN route-follower with no neural substrate.
- Naive embodied E-PG/P-EN loops driven by gait-locked periodic velocity input produce phase slips (−80.8 deg body vs −56.8 deg ring) and three ad hoc fixes failed (arterialist log) — shift operators must be gated/integrated, not driven raw.
- Ring-attractor action selection in deep RL (arXiv 2410.03119): gains not isolated by ablation, 297% overhead for CTRNN, hurts non-spatial action spaces, rejected at ICLR 2025 — mapping a non-circular action/vocabulary space onto a ring is unjustified.
- Running the raw connectome as LIF neurons in a body (Fly.exe, FruitFly-repo, 2026) yields no CX-level function: photoreceptor edges zeroed, hand-engineered mappings, self-declared unvalidated — structure alone does not produce vector computation; phase offsets and amplitude channels must be built in explicitly.
- Treating locust-style local inhibition as universally 'better' is wrong: it trades speed for stability (Pisokas); likewise fly Delta7 modulates only ~10% with heading, so a strongly heading-tuned inhibitory population is not the fly's solution.
- Hulse 2021 / Nature 2022-2024 full texts (nature.com, elifesciences.org, biorxiv, PNAS, PLoS, Frontiers, Royal Society, Janelia, FlyWire codex, Wikipedia) are all blocked in this environment and the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted; only GitHub mirrors/READMEs were reachable — some numbers above are from memory and should be re-verified against the papers.

**Open gaps**

- No published work applies FB-style sinusoid vector arithmetic with amplitude-as-precision to sequence/language modelling; every CX model targets heading/2-D navigation with 1–3 registers.
- How downstream circuits READ EPG bump amplitude as certainty is unknown; only goal-bump (FC2) amplitude has a demonstrated readout via PFL3 (Mussells Pires 2024). Whether steering scales multiplicatively with both goal and state amplitude has not been quantified.
- Multi-goal arbitration: how several FB registers (FC2, hDelta types, 9 layers) compete or sum, and how tangential neurons gate them quantitatively, is unmeasured; models assume a single static goal (Freas & Wystrach 2026 explicitly).
- The 9-column PB / 8-tile EB mismatch and 9 FB columns: no functional account of the extra column; models use 8 or 16 uniformly.
- Vector inversion needs a Ca2+-spike nonlinearity (Ishida 2025) — the algebra of which CX operations are linear (sums) vs nonlinear (negation, thresholded comparison) is not catalogued; a full 'instruction set' of the FB is missing.
- No demonstration that the Basnak/Kim inhibitory plasticity rule scales to high-dimensional or non-circular feature spaces (all tests use 1-2 cues on one ring).
- Kutschireiter's amplitude-free network was validated on synthetic trajectories (10k runs); no closed-loop behavioural test that flies' cue weights match the network's kappa dynamics under time-varying reliability.
- Whether the Delta7 kernel and PFN/PFL offsets are identical in the MaleCNS v1.0 (male, whole CNS) vs hemibrain/FlyWire (female) has not been reported; sexual dimorphism analyses (Berg 2025) did not single out the CX.
- EFE-style epistemic value has no identified CX correlate; exploration models rely on oscillators/speed gains, and Dan 2024's goal learning is purely exploitative reinforcement.
- Hierarchical stacking of ring latents (rings over rings, or context-gated ring copies as in Low 2021 / Jensen 2026) has not been connected to the CX anatomy (e.g., FB layers as hierarchy).

**Key numbers**

- Circular Kalman filter: dkappa_pred = -1/2·1/(kappa_phi+kappa_v)·kappa·f(kappa) dt; quadratic approx dkappa = -(kappa^2-kappa)/(kappa_phi+kappa_v) dt; dmu = kappa_v/(kappa_phi+kappa_v)·dy; kappa_0=10 (Kutschireiter 2023 code)
- Bayesian ring network: N_HD=100, N_Delta7=100, N_AV+/-=50, tau_AV=10 ms, tau_Delta7=1 ms, W_HD_HD=2/N·(-0.2+max(0,2cos Δphi)), AV weights ∝ max(0, ±sin(Δphi ± pi/4)), dt=1 ms, 10,000 trajectories/config, kappa_z ∈ [0.01,100]
- Basnak 2024 plasticity: gamma_Hebb=1, gamma_postboost=1, gamma_decay=17, eta=5e-5, dt=0.0025 s, update scaled by |angular velocity|; sims 150–240 s; 15 flies
- Vafidis 2022: 60 HD + 60 HR units; convergence ~20 h (~4000 cycles); D=24.5 deg^2/s (82.3±15.7 with weight noise sigma~1.5); gain-1 for |v|<500 deg/s; impaired for |v|<30 deg/s; regain new gain (0.25–2.0) in ~3 h
- Pisokas 2020: PB 18 glomeruli (fly)/16 (locust); EB 8 tiles; FWHM E-PG 88.3±0.3 deg (fly) vs 68.3±0.1 (locust), P-EN 80.4 vs 63.1, P-EG 71.0 vs 63.1; post-cue dispersion ~30–40 deg (fly) vs ~10–20 deg (locust); locust inhibition sigma=0.8; LIF R_m=10 MΩ, C_m=2 nF, V_rest=-52 mV, thr=-45 mV, I_PSC=5 nA, 5 ms
- Turner-Evans 2017: P-EN modulation 5.6±3.7 Hz, bandwidth 145±82 deg/s; P-EN lags E-PG ~45 deg in PB, leads 20.7±11.7 deg in EB
- Franconville 2018: ~70 pairs tested, 43/59 possible connections; Delta7 only strong PB columnar link
- Hulse 2021 (memory): EB 8 tiles/16 wedges; PB 18 glomeruli; FB ~9 columns × 9 layers; PFN offsets ±45 deg; hDelta 180 deg; PFL3 ±67.5 deg; PFL2 180 deg; ~250 CX types; ~400 PFN (Sayre 2021 comparison), bumblebee 854+ PFN, 188 hDelta, 69 EPG/PEG, ~40 PEN
- Sun 2020: CPU4 accumulation r=0.0025, loss k=0.1; k_TUN=0.1 (0.025–0.0125); SN2 threshold 2.0–3.0; MB 81 vPN → 4000 KC → 1 MBON, eta=0.1; motor k=0.125–0.375; Wystrach 2015 capture distances 0.1/1/3/7 m; PI 270 deg vs VH 135 deg; reviewer: ant speed 1 cm/s vs real ~50 cm/s
- Sun 2021: 16 CPU1 units; 8-unit rings; 4.5 deg shift resolution
- Aceituno 2024: N=8 optimal; single harmonic f=1; Oja rule self-organises cosine weights
- Dan 2024 code: 32 heading units, 16 basis functions, alphaW=0.001, alphaH=0.05, 9 trials × 240 s, saccade lognormal mean 3.89 / spread 0.54, 320 ms, fixation thr 0.79
- Rayshubskiy 2025: R^2=0.51 DNa02 vs rotational velocity; DNa02 leads turns ~150 ms; 87±10 ATP ejections/fly; bump returned in 40% of trials
- Currier 2020: ventral PFN tuned 45 deg ipsilateral (orthogonal basis pair)
- Freas & Wystrach 2026: 8 sectors × 45 deg; PFL shift ±45 deg; peak correction at 135 deg; g_ang=0.03; k=0.5/1.5; fixations 50–150 ms
- Olfactory vector navigation 2026: inside-bout 4.9 s (n=755), margin 10.4 mm, re-crossing <4%, plume 50×1000 mm, n=40 flies
- MaleCNS v1.0: 165,122 traced neurons; 25,563,197 edges; 166,700 bodies; release 8 Jun 2026; Cell paper 3 Sep 2026; Berg 2025 bioRxiv 10.1101/2025.10.09.680999; 81 neuropils validated; 311 communities
- Ring-attractor RL (arXiv 2410.03119): +53% Atari-100k, Asterix +110%, Boxing +105%, 297% overhead; ICLR scores 5/6/6/5 reject
- Embodied compass failure log: body −80.80 deg vs ring −56.80 deg (+24.00 deg) over 400 ticks; 44-tick gait period
- Continuous-attractor fine tuning: ~2% weight matching across ~8 neurons; D ∝ sigma^2/N
- Low 2021 RNN remapping: 248 units; 8.13±0.51 deg error after 300 steps; 988 fixed points, ~60% on rings
- Jensen 2026 spacetime attractor: 800-unit RNN, ~90% planning success, adjacency correlation r=0.91±0.07

As tabulated by Bricken et al. 2023 and reused by the Elephant-NN paper on class-incremental Split MNIST: FlyModel 0.77 with 1K KCs and 0.91 with 10K KCs, the best entries in the table, but it 'requires task boundary information' (single data pass). | [Shen, Dasgupta, Navlakha 2021/2023 (primary text not reachable); numbers via Bricken et al](https://github.com/weathon/split_review/blob/main/datasets/deepreview_13k_test_refetch/papers/kxe0hQ5mxp.txt) | 2023 | moderate |
| Bricken, Davies, Singh, Krotov, Kreiman (ICLR 2023, 'Sparse Distributed Memory is a Continual Learner'): a one-hidden-layer MLP made SDM-like via top-k activation, a 'GABA switch' (annealed k to avoid dead neurons), non-negative weights, L2-normalized inputs/weights and SGD without momentum. Split MNIST (task-free, single pass) with 1K neurons: SDMLP 0.69 vs MLP 0.665 vs MLP+streaming EWC 0.708; with 10K neurons the third-party table lists SDMLP 0.53 (vs MLP 0.621, EMLP 0.802, FlyModel 0.91). Reviewers (scores 5/8/6/8): strong mainly on CIFAR-10 with pretrained embeddings, heavy hyperparameter tuning, 'too many modifications'. | [Bricken et al. ICLR 2023 (OpenReview JknGeelZJpHP reviews mirrored on GitHub; numbers via ](https://github.com/songxxzp/openreviewer/blob/main/data/iclr2023/reviews/JknGeelZJpHP.json) | 2023 | moderate |
| Elephant Neural Networks (Lan & Mahmood; ICLR 2024 submission kxe0hQ5mxp): sparse activation functions producing sparse activations AND sparse gradients reach 0.723 (1K) / 0.802 (10K) on task-free single-pass Split MNIST without task boundaries or replay, i.e. below FlyModel (0.77/0.91) but above SDMLP and EWC baselines. | [Elephant Neural Networks paper text (OpenReview kxe0hQ5mxp)](https://github.com/weathon/split_review/blob/main/datasets/deepreview_13k_test_refetch/papers/kxe0hQ5mxp.txt) | 2023 | moderate |
| Fly-CL (Xu, Lukasiewicz, Zhang; ICLR 2026, arXiv 2510.16877): frozen pretrained features -> fixed sparse random projection to m>>d (each row has p nonzeros ~N(0,1)) -> top-k -> streaming ridge classifier with lambda chosen by GCV (the 'KC->MBON' stage). 10-task CIFAR-100: 76.5 vs RanPAC 76.4 vs SimpleCIL 70.8; ImageNet-R: 78.8 vs 78.6 vs 71.4; training time after feature extraction reduced 38-93%; accuracy gains 1.17-2.38% over baselines. Theory: sparse random matrices keep full column rank w.p. 1-o(1); top-k error decays polynomially when k = Omega(m^alpha). Limitations: top-k needs tuning; projection is fixed; images only. | [Fly-CL ICLR 2026 (paper notes and research digest on GitHub; code gfyddha/Fly-CL)](https://github.com/zhaoyang97/Paper-Notes/blob/main/docs/ICLR2026/self_supervised/fly-cl_a_fly-inspired_framework_for_enhancing_efficient_decorrelation_and_reduce.md) | 2026 | moderate |
| FlyPrompt (ICLR 2026, arXiv 2602.01976): a 'Random Expanded Analytic Router' mimicking the mushroom body routes inputs to experts with a fixed random projection d=512 -> M=1024 (only 2x expansion), ReLU giving ~30% active units (not 5%), and a closed-form ridge solution (lambda=0.01) with no task labels; plus temporal-ensemble EMA expert heads (alpha 0.9/0.99). Si-Blurry general continual learning: CIFAR-100 A_auc 83.24 (+11.23 vs MISA), ImageNet-R 56.58 (+12.43), CUB-200 70.64 (+7.62); router accuracy 82.7% on CIFAR-100. Image-only. | [FlyPrompt paper (sparsis review notes on GitHub)](https://github.com/lewisbakkero/sparsis/blob/main/2026-05-24/flyprompt-brain-inspired-random-expanded-routing-with-temporal-ensemble-experts.md) | 2026 | moderate |
| BioHash (Ryali, Hopfield, Grinberg, Krotov, ICML 2020): learn the PN->KC projection with the Krotov-Hopfield Hebbian/anti-Hebbian rule (p=2, eps0=0.02, 100 epochs, batch 100, activity k/m 0.5-5%, m typically 10-50x d) and kWTA to +-1 codes. MNIST mAP@all: k=32 55.5% vs ITQ 43.6 vs LSH 26.2; k=16 54.9 vs 41.2 vs 20.3; k=2/4/8: 44.4/49.3/53.4. CIFAR-10 mAP@1000: k=32 24.0 vs ITQ 20.6 vs LSH 18.1. Costs: codes need k*log2(m) bits (not k bits), argmax ranking is sequential, activity level is a sensitive hyperparameter. | [Ryali et al. ICML 2020 (summarized in IBM primeqa/docuverse BioHash scripts)](https://github.com/primeqa/docuverse/blob/main/scripts/biohash/README_BIOHASH.md) | 2020 | moderate |
| SoftHash (rejected ICLR submission cNwugejbW6, scores 6/5/6/5): replacing BioHash's hard WTA with a softmax (soft WTA) during learning improved image retrieval (approx. k=128 mAP@1000: Fashion-MNIST ~78 vs BioHash ~70 vs FlyHash ~55 vs LSH ~45; CIFAR-10 ~68/60/38/25) and on word similarity (WS353, SimLex999, MEN) approached float GloVe at k=128-512, whereas BioHash 'no longer improves when hash lengths reach certain values'. Reviewers flagged small test sets, missing baselines and unclear protocol. | [SoftHash paper text and human reviews (OpenReview cNwugejbW6 mirrored on GitHub)](https://github.com/weathon/split_review_2/blob/main/datasets/deepreview_13k_train/papers/cNwugejbW6.txt) | 2023 | weak |
| Fly novelty detector / Bloom filter (Dasgupta, Sheehan, Stevens, Navlakha, PNAS 2018): after the KC hash, a single MBON-like readout stores a weight per KC that is depressed when the KC fires (delta) and passively recovers (tick); novelty = mean weight of active KCs before update. A 2025-26 reimplementation (ffbf) uses m = 30x expected items, k = 5% of m, projection sparsity 0.12, delta 0.5, tick 0.01; 4 us per novelty query and 7.7 us per add at 384-d, m=1200; enabling forgetting slowed drift-induced degradation 36x. | [Dasgupta et al. PNAS 2018 as implemented in clembarr/ffbf-novelty-detector](https://github.com/clembarr/ffbf-novelty-detector) | 2018 | moderate |
| Attention approximates Sparse Distributed Memory (Bricken & Pehlevan, NeurIPS 2021): softmax attention with L2-normalized keys/queries (LayerNorm) approximates SDM's Hamming-ball circle-intersection weighting; the fitted softmax beta corresponds to the SDM read radius and different GPT-2 heads learn different effective betas; SDM maps onto cerebellum/Kenyon-cell-like expansion circuits. | [Bricken & Pehlevan 2021 (GitHub code/README and CS25 lecture notes)](https://github.com/TrentBrick/attention-approximates-sdm) | 2021 | moderate |
| Connectome-derived RNN study (pathintegrationBPU, GitHub 2025-26, unreviewed): hemibrain mushroom-body graph (14,025 neurons, 574,660 edges) as a trainable recurrent substrate on MQAR (vocab 32, 8 KV pairs): 0.925 +- 0.003 (5 seeds) vs weight-shuffled same topology 0.914 vs random sparse 0.836 +- 0.008 vs degree-preserving random 0.768 +- 0.033; reaches 0.995 with 1000 epochs; ~2x faster grokking than random. But the optic lobe graph off-diagonal gives +8.5% vs MB's +10.6%, and subsampling OL to 14,025 neurons drops it to 0.83 ~ MB's 0.80, so the advantage is 'generic structured-recurrence sample-efficiency (~1.8x), region-agnostic'. Truncating MB to 2000 neurons caps accuracy at ~0.53. Domain-incremental CIFAR-10 (5 binary tasks): MB connectome 0.623 final / forgetting 0.235 vs random sparse 0.595 / 0.245 -> 'no connectome-specific advantage in continual learning'. | [eonsystemspbc/pathintegrationBPU results docs (mqar_associative_recall, region_task_matrix](https://github.com/eonsystemspbc/pathintegrationBPU/blob/main/docs/results/mqar_associative_recall/README.md) | 2026 | weak |
| PGCN (FlyWire FAFB v783-based, GitHub, unreviewed): PNs (~58 glomeruli) -> 5,374 KCs with 4-6% activity (kWTA) and ~5% PN->KC density -> 44 MBONs, 231 DANs; KC->MBON three-factor rule dW ~ KC x MBON x DA at lr 0.001. Reports <5% forgetting with an Or7a-inspired 'veto' vs ~40% catastrophic forgetting without, and a blocking index +0.99/-0.98. Caveat noted by authors: copying hemibrain KC->MBON weights directly produced 1000x larger naive MBON responses for one glomerulus, requiring random re-initialization at 1e-4 under anatomical masks; small circuits show low forgetting under all conditions. | [colehanan1/Plasticity-Guided-Connectome-Network-PGCN README](https://github.com/colehanan1/Plasticity-Guided-Connectome-Network-PGCN) | 2025 | weak |
| Fly-LSH expansion on LM embeddings (hd-instrument, GitHub 2026, unreviewed prereg'd experiments): Pythia-2.8B hidden states projected to 768-d, sparse fan-in K=5, kWTA 2%, fly top-k 0.5%, expansion 8x-4096x, evaluated on adversarially similar keys (adjacent windows sharing 15/16 tokens). Reported: fly-LSH top-1 retrieval 0.997 at M=10k keys vs raw cosine 0.018 (55x lift), but at M=100k adversarial keys 'whitening / random hash / fly-LSH all HARD_FAIL'. Same project's design note argues biological expansion circuits converge on f = 0.05-0.10 active and that their f=0.001 VQ was 50-100x too sparse. | [mcox79/hd-instrument preregs and notes (2026-06)](https://github.com/mcox79/hd-instrument/blob/main/experiments/exp_substrate_anisotropy_fly_lsh_expansion_sweep_v2_cpu_path.py) | 2026 | weak |
| Johnson et al. (JHU APL, 2023 preprint) report a mushroom-body-inspired memory-formation circuit for continual learning giving 'over 20% accuracy improvement' in an incremental CIFAR-100 setting, citing Shen/Dasgupta/Navlakha, alongside a heading-direction circuit for sensor fusion and cortex-constrained transformers with >10x fewer attention parameters. | [Johnson et al. 2023, 'Exploiting large neuroimaging datasets to create connectome-constrai](https://github.com/clankwright/science/blob/main/bpu/raw/johnson_connectome_nas_2023.md) | 2023 | moderate |
