**Usable ideas**

- Two-stage KC front end (Muscinelli 2023; FlyVec; Fly-CL): a learned low-dimensional 'PN' compression of the residual stream (linear or Krotov-Hopfield competitive layer with 1/p frequency weighting) followed by a FIXED sparse random expansion with in-degree K and kWTA. Do not random-project raw token one-hots (RandExp lost to learned weights at every hash length in FlyVec).
- Set KC in-degree from the sqrt rule, not a constant 7: K_opt ~ 6-7 for 50 inputs scales roughly ~sqrt(N_in) (Litwin-Kumar 2017), so for a 512-2048-d PN stage budget K ~ 15-30 and tune by maximizing the participation ratio (tr C)^2/tr C^2 of KC activity on real token data. Keep the connectome's PN->KC bias (non-uniform glomerulus sampling, up to 15x) as an optional prior arm.
- Treat coding level as a swept hyperparameter tied to task type (Xie 2023): 5% (fly APL) for categorical/episodic keys and novelty; 10-30% for smooth next-token prediction readouts (FlyPrompt used ~30% active with only 2x expansion for pretrained features). Pre-register both arms.
- Use a closed-form MBON readout before committing to Titans-style gradient memory: streaming ridge regression over KC codes with GCV-selected lambda (Fly-CL) or an analytic router (FlyPrompt) matched RanPAC-level continual learners with 38-93% less training time. Compare it head-to-head with (a) a three-factor Hebbian KC->MBON rule and (b) a gradient memory on the same KC codes.
- Hebbian KC->MBON with partial freezing (FlyModel): update only synapses from currently active KCs; forgetting fell as KC width rose (Split MNIST 0.77 at 1K -> 0.91 at 10K KCs). Test the same width scaling on a text continual-learning stream (e.g., domain-incremental language modeling), noting FlyModel needed task boundaries.
- Borrow SDMLP's stability kit for any gradient-trained KC layer (Bricken 2023): top-k activation, annealed k ('GABA switch') to avoid dead KCs, non-negative weights, L2-normalized inputs/weights, SGD without momentum.
- Add a fly Bloom-filter novelty MBON (Dasgupta 2018; ffbf): one weight per KC, depressed by delta~0.5 when the KC fires, recovering with tick~0.01; novelty = mean weight of the active KCs. Use it as the surprise/precision signal in the active-inference loop; cost is microseconds per query.
- Use KC hashes as SDM addresses for the memory module and calibrate the kWTA radius against the attention softmax beta (Bricken & Pehlevan 2021); FlyHash's advantage is at short codes (k=4-32) and requires multi-probe/multi-table (DenseFly) to hold recall at scale; add whitening/decorrelation before hashing anisotropic LM states (hd-instrument failure at 100k near-duplicate keys).
- Soft WTA during Hebbian learning, hard WTA at inference (SoftHash claim) to avoid the plateau of hard-WTA codes on word-similarity quality as hash length grows.
- Controls to pre-register for any 'connectome prior helps' claim, learned from pathintegrationBPU: weight-shuffled same topology, degree-preserving random, ER random sparse, AND a matched-size other-region graph; their MB advantage on MQAR (0.925 vs 0.836 random) survived weight shuffling (0.914) but not the matched-size optic-lobe control.
- Do not initialize KC->MBON weights from raw connectome synapse counts (PGCN saw 1000x valence bias); use connectome masks with learned or small random magnitudes.

**Dead ends**

- Random-projection expansion for word embeddings: FlyVec abandoned the biological random 40x expansion for a learned 40,000->400 contraction; random expansion baselines (RandExp) were worse at every hash length (e.g., MEN 27.5 vs 34.0 at k=4).
- Expecting KC sparse-binary codes to match dense embeddings on fine-grained semantics: FlyVec trails word2vec by 13-22 Spearman points on MEN/SimLex/RW and BERT by 3.5 (WiC) to 7.7 (SCWS) points; gains exist only versus other binarized codes and at very short hash lengths.
- Hard-WTA Hebbian hashing plateaus with hash length on word-similarity tasks (BioHash, per the SoftHash submission); longer KC codes do not keep improving semantic quality.
- FlyHash as a drop-in ANN index: proof of concept without multi-probe/multi-table; reimplementations show 1.3-3x mAP gains that depend on the ground-truth metric, and a 2026 attempt on 100k adversarially similar LM keys hard-failed despite a 55x lift at 10k keys.
- SDMLP scaling: third-party tabulation shows Split MNIST 0.69 at 1K but 0.53 at 10K neurons; reviewers flagged extensive hyperparameter tuning and reliance on pretrained embeddings; width scaling is not automatically beneficial for gradient-trained KC-like layers.
- FlyModel-style associative continual learning requires task-boundary information in its evaluated form; task-free single-pass settings drop to Elephant/SDMLP-level numbers (0.72-0.80).
- Using the MB connectome graph as a generic recurrent backbone for continual learning: no advantage over random sparse graphs (0.623 vs 0.595 final accuracy, forgetting 0.235 vs 0.245) in the unreviewed pathintegrationBPU replication; and its MQAR advantage is region-agnostic and disappears when the truncated graph is 2000 neurons (accuracy ~0.53).
- Copying literal KC->MBON synapse counts as initial weights produced a 1000x MBON response bias (PGCN) and had to be replaced by masked random initialization.
- 'Expansion before sparsifying' for smooth continuous prediction under local learning rules hurt consolidation in the TSL project (NRMSE 1.048 vs 0.778), consistent with Xie 2023: 5% coding is wrong for smooth targets.
- Extremely sparse codes (f~0.001, one-hot VQ) are 50-100x sparser than the biological optimum (f~0.05-0.10) and hit an entropy floor for token prediction (hd-instrument analysis).
- Fly-inspired continual learners published in 2026 (Fly-CL, FlyPrompt) are image-only on frozen ViT features; their numbers do not transfer as evidence for language streams.

**Open gaps**

- No published result trains a Hebbian or three-factor KC->MBON readout to predict next tokens over a 20k+ vocabulary; every text result (FlyVec, SoftHash, BioHash) evaluates similarity or uses a gradient-trained TextCNN/ridge readout on top of KC codes.
- No scaling law for KC width vs corpus/vocabulary: FlyVec only tested K=200-600 KCs, 20k vocab, ~6B tokens; forgetting-vs-width is known only on Split MNIST (1K vs 10K KCs).
- Optimal in-degree K for high-dimensional token/PN inputs is untested; the ~sqrt(N_in) extrapolation from Litwin-Kumar 2017 (K~7 at N=50) has not been validated on language activations, nor has the participation-ratio objective been compared with perplexity.
- Task-dependent coding level (Xie 2023) has not been measured for language: the right kWTA fraction for next-token prediction vs episodic key storage vs novelty detection is unknown.
- Non-random PN->KC wiring from the connectome (glomerulus-type bias up to 15x; Zheng 2022 co-occurrence structure; MaleCNS-specific in-degree distribution) has never been used as an ML prior; all fly-inspired ML uses uniform random sparse projections.
- The attention-approximates-SDM correspondence has not been turned into a KC-hash SDM replacing or gating attention in a trained LM with perplexity numbers.
- No text continual-learning benchmark with MB-style partial freezing exists; forgetting numbers for KC->MBON readouts are all on image class-incremental splits and mostly require task boundaries.
- APL-like normalization (Pearson-correlation-preserving divisive feedback) has not been combined with LayerNorm'd transformer residual streams, and its interaction with anisotropy (which broke fly-LSH at 100k keys) is unstudied.
- Soft-WTA-during-learning claims (SoftHash) were never peer-reviewed; the effect of soft vs hard competition on KC code semantics remains unverified.
- Primary PDFs of Litwin-Kumar 2017 and Shen et al. 2021/2023 could not be reached in this environment (WebSearch quota exhausted; academic hosts blocked); their exact tables (e.g., FlyModel vs EWC/GEM on CIFAR-100, dependence of K_opt on N and M) should be re-checked before being written into the spec.

**Key numbers**

- Fly MB circuit: ~50 PN glomeruli -> ~2000 KCs (40x expansion); ~6-7 PN inputs per KC; APL WTA keeps top ~5%; cerebellum granule cells ~4 mossy-fiber inputs (Litwin-Kumar 2017 predicts K~7 fly / K~4 cerebellum; optimum grows ~sqrt(N_in)).
- FlyHash MNIST k=4: mAP 16.0% (dense LSH) vs 44.8% (fly); sparse binary projection ~20x cheaper; reimplementations 1.3-3x mAP at short codes.
- FlyVec: K=400 KCs, Nvoc=20,000, w=11, OWT ~6B tokens, 2-3 epochs to plateau, 8-24 min/epoch on 3xV100, 122-150 MB GPU RAM; ~3% of float-embedding memory.
- FlyVec Spearman (x100) at k~50: MEN 56.6 / WS353 63.7 / SimLex 21.0 / RW 39.4 / RG 69.0 / MTurk 56.1 vs GloVe 69.5/64.0/31.5/46.8/74.2/57.5 vs word2vec 75.5/66.5/41.7/61.3/75.4/69.8; at k=4: MEN 34.0, WS353 43.2, SimLex 13.4 (vs LSH-binarized GloVe 16.9/8.2/6.8).
- FlyVec WiC 57.7 vs BERT 61.2 (GloVe 54.9, w2v 50.0); SCWS 49.1 vs BERT 56.8; TextCNN on k=30 hashes: 20NG 78.2 (BERT 78.6), SST-2 77.1 (90.8), WOS 83.8 (86.7), TREC-6 90.4 (94.0).
- BioHash MNIST mAP@all: k=32 55.5% vs ITQ 43.6 vs LSH 26.2; k=16 54.9/41.2/20.3; CIFAR-10 mAP@1000 k=32 24.0 vs 20.6 vs 18.1; typical activity 0.5-5%, m = 10-50x d; code cost k*log2(m) bits.
- Krotov-Hopfield rule defaults: p=2, k=2, delta=0.4, eps0=0.02, 200 epochs (MNIST); FlyVec: delta=0, lr 2e-4, 15 epochs, batch 10k, 400 hidden.
- Split MNIST class-incremental (single pass): FlyModel 0.77 (1K KCs) / 0.91 (10K KCs, needs task boundaries); SDMLP 0.69 / 0.53; MLP 0.665 / 0.621; MLP+streaming EWC 0.708 / 0.609; Elephant MLP 0.723 / 0.802 (task-free).
- Fly-CL (ICLR 2026): CIFAR-100 10-task 76.5 vs RanPAC 76.4 vs SimpleCIL 70.8; ImageNet-R 78.8 vs 78.6 vs 71.4; training time -38% to -93%; accuracy +1.17 to +2.38%.
- FlyPrompt (ICLR 2026): random expansion 512 -> 1024 (2x), ~30% active, ridge lambda=0.01; CIFAR-100 A_auc 83.24 (+11.23), ImageNet-R 56.58 (+12.43), CUB-200 70.64 (+7.62); router accuracy 82.7%.
- Xie 2023: optimal coding level < 0.1 for random categorization; substantially higher for smooth targets; holds under an online climbing-fiber rule.
- Fly Bloom filter (ffbf): m = 30x items, k = 5% of m, projection sparsity 0.12, delta 0.5, tick 0.01; 4 us/query, 7.7 us/add at 384-d, m=1200; forgetting slows drift degradation 36x.
- pathintegrationBPU MQAR (vocab 32, 8 KV pairs): MB hemibrain graph (14,025 neurons, 574,660 edges) 0.925 +- 0.003; weight-shuffle 0.914; random sparse 0.836 +- 0.008; degree-preserving random 0.768 +- 0.033; attention 1.000; MB truncated to 2000 neurons ~0.53; matched-size optic lobe 0.83 vs MB 0.80; ~1.8x sample efficiency over random; continual CIFAR-10 pairs: MB 0.623/forgetting 0.235 vs random sparse 0.595/0.245.
- PGCN (FlyWire): 5,374 KCs, 4-6% active, PN->KC density ~5%, 44 MBONs, 231 DANs; reported <5% forgetting with veto vs ~40% without; raw connectome KC->MBON weights gave 1000x MBON bias.
- hd-instrument fly-LSH on Pythia-2.8B states: fan-in K=5, kWTA 2%, fly top-k 0.5%, expansion 8x-4096x; top-1 0.997 at 10k keys vs raw 0.018 (55x); HARD_FAIL at 100k adversarial keys; biological coding-level target f = 0.05-0.10 vs their 0.001.
- Johnson et al. 2023 (JHU APL): MB-inspired continual learning >20% accuracy gain on incremental CIFAR-100.

## gap:Interneuron microcircuits that

22 findings

| claim | source | year | strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wilmes, Petrovici, Sachidhanandam & Senn (eLife RP95127 v3, Jan 2025) give a concrete E/I microcircuit for precision: SST+ units learn the stimulus MEAN (subtractive inhibition on the pyramidal dendrite), PV units learn the VARIANCE (divisive inhibition near the soma), and two rectified pyramidal populations compute UPE+ = (1/sigma^2)*floor(s-mu)^+ and UPE- = (1/sigma^2)*floor(mu-s)^+; a representation unit R integrates UPE+ minus UPE-. Error unit: tau_E dr = -r + phi( I_dend / (I_0 + w_UPE,PV * r_PV) ), I_dend = floor(w*s - w_SST*r_SST)^k with k=2 (dendritic supralinearity, 'NMDA'); PV activation phi_PV(x)=x^2 (clipped) so r_PV ~ sigma^2; SST delta rule dw = eta (r_SST - phi(w r_a)) r_a with nudging beta=0.1; PV delta rule dw = eta (r_PV - phi_PV(w r_a)) r_a with correction w_s = sqrt((2-beta)/beta) so E[(w r_a)^2]=sigma^2. In the released code (k47h4/UPE, Circuit.py) division is explicit arithmetic 1/(PV_weighting + wEP*rP) with PV_weighting=1, exponent=2.0, eta_P=0.001, eta_PS=1e-4, tau=1. | [Wilmes et al., 'Uncertainty-modulated prediction errors in cortical microcircuits', eLife ](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.95127.3) | 2025 | strong |
| Wilmes et al. results are single-representative-unit rate simulations only (no spiking, no population heterogeneity): stimuli s~N(mu,sigma) with mu in {1,3,5}, sigma in {0.4,0.8}, N=1000-20000 samples, D=10-100 steps per stimulus, dt=0.1, eta_SST=0.1, eta_PV=0.001, eta_R=0.1. Demonstrated: w_SST ∝ mu; w_PV ∝ sigma and r_PV ∝ sigma^2 (also monotone with a power-3 transfer); UPE response falls with sigma for fixed /s-mu/; k=2.5 amplifies large mismatches; with UPEs the mean is learned faster at low sigma and R's std grows only sublinearly with input std (contraction bias) at high sigma; a 2-level hierarchical version implements r = (s/sigma_s^2 + p/sigma_p^2)/(1/sigma_s^2 + 1/sigma_p^2). Reviewers (eLife assessment 'important'/'convincing' but) called 'stimulus uncertainty encoded in PV firing rate' rather speculative and noted many assumptions; authors concede any subtractive/divisive interneuron pair would do and that only expected (not volatility) uncertainty is covered; VIP for unexpected uncertainty is left as speculation. | [Wilmes et al. eLife RP95127 manuscript, reviews and author response (OpenEvalProject/evals](https://github.com/OpenEvalProject/evals/blob/main/manuscripts/elife-95127/v1/manuscript.md) | 2025 | moderate |
| Hertäg & Sprekeler (eLife 2020, 9:e57541) show that negative (nPE) and positive (pPE) prediction-error neurons EMERGE from homeostatic inhibitory plasticity in a canonical L2/3 circuit: 70 two-compartment PCs, 10 PV, 10 SOM, 10 VIP; PC soma gets sensory input + PV inhibition, apical dendrite gets prediction + SOM inhibition (rectified, calcium-spike threshold 28 s^-1, amplitude 7 s^-1); tau_E=60 ms, tau_I=2 ms; leak factors lambda_D=0.27, lambda_E=0.31; connection probabilities ~0.1-0.6. nPE requires two E/I balance equalities (their Eqs 8-9): somatic sensory excitation cancelled by PV, dendritic prediction cancelled by SOM, with VIP disinhibition balancing the pathway. Plastic synapses: PV->PC soma and SOM->PC dendrite (rate-homeostatic Hebbian, target rho_0 and epsilon=0.1 s^-1), SOM/VIP->PV (backprop-like or a local approximation requiring pre/post overlap). After quasi-natural training all 70 PCs become nPE (>=20% response in mismatch, <±10% elsewhere); random-gain (uncoupled) training roughly halves nPE count and shrinks mismatch responses (matches Attinger 2017); the fraction f of SOM receiving sensory vs prediction input sets the nPE/pPE mix (f≈1 → ~100% nPE, f≈0.5 → ~50%); PV activity is a diagnostic of whether PCs receive somatic sensory input. | [Hertäg & Sprekeler, 'Learning prediction error neurons in a canonical interneuron circuit'](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.57541) | 2020 | strong |
| Hertäg & Sprekeler 2020 explicitly could NOT co-develop nPE and pPE in the same network ('constraints appear contradictory'; would need gating/neuromodulation); their most accurate SOM/VIP->PV plasticity rule is non-local (backprop-like); the model has no feature selectivity, one apical compartment, only three interneuron classes, and non-plastic excitatory pathways. | [Hertäg & Sprekeler eLife 2020, Limitations section](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.57541) | 2020 | strong |
| Hertäg & Clopath (PNAS 2022, 'Prediction-error neurons in circuits with multiple neuron types: formation, refinement, and functional implications'; code lhertaeg/SourceCode_Hertaeg2021 with Run_FullNet/Run_MFN/Run_PPE) extend the 2020 model so that both nPE and pPE populations form in one network with PV/SOM/VIP via the same homeostatic inhibitory plasticity when input configurations are heterogeneous, with excitatory plasticity refining them; reported functional benefits are robust error coding under noise/heterogeneity. Results are noted to vary slightly with random seeds. | [Hertäg & Clopath, PNAS 2022; GitHub SourceCode_Hertaeg2021 README](https://github.com/lhertaeg/SourceCode_Hertaeg2021) | 2022 | moderate |
| Hertäg, Wilmes & Clopath ('Uncertainty estimation with prediction-error circuits', bioRxiv 2023.12.13.571410, earlier title 'Knowing what you don't know...'; Nat Commun 16:3036, 2025) offer the ALTERNATIVE precision mechanism: uncertainty is read out from the prediction-error neurons themselves. In the released code (lhertaeg/weighted_sensory_prediction) a memory/prediction neuron integrates (pPE - nPE) via dr_mem = (U·rates)/tau_E, a variance neuron integrates the squared PE mass dr_var = (-var + (Q·rates)^2)/tc_var with tau_var = 2000 (tc_var_per_stim = tc_var_pred = 5000), and sensory vs prediction are weighted by alpha = (1/v_lower)/((1/v_lower)+(1/v_higher)) across a two-level (stimulus/prediction) hierarchy; population sizes 140 E, 20 PV, 20 SOM, 20 VIP, tau_E=60, tau_I=2, baseline rates [0,0,0,0,4,4,4,4]; PE->prediction weights ±0.003. | [Hertäg, Wilmes, Clopath; code lhertaeg/weighted_sensory_prediction (functions_networks.py,](https://github.com/lhertaeg/weighted_sensory_prediction) | 2025 | moderate |
| Sacramento, Costa, Bengio & Senn (NeurIPS 2018, arXiv 1810.11393) dendritic microcircuit: pyramidal soma u_P with basal (feedforward, g_B=1.0) and apical (g_A≈0.28-0.8) compartments, leak g_l=0.1; apical potential v_A = W_pi phi(u_I) + W_down phi(u_{k+1}) so lateral SST-like interneurons (driven by same-layer pyramidals, nudged by the layer above via g_som) learn to cancel top-down input; the 'self-predicting state' W_pi = -W_down makes apical potential zero at baseline so any apical deviation IS the backpropagated error; plasticity tau_w dW_up = (phi(u_P) - phi(v_hat_B)) r^T (low-pass filtered), similar rules for P->I and I->P. Independent reimplementation (phll/Neuro-Backprop) reached 96.7±0.8% (SPS init) / 97.2±1.1% (lateral init) on YinYang with a 4-120-3 net, 45 epochs, 6000 samples; the original paper reports ~98% MNIST test accuracy for a 784-500-500-10 net (from memory, not re-verified). | [Sacramento et al. NeurIPS 2018; reimplementation report phll/Neuro-Backprop (neuro-backpro](https://github.com/phll/Neuro-Backprop) | 2018 | moderate |
| Reimplementation of the Sacramento microcircuit documents practical failure modes: the settling phase 'contributes significantly to the overall prediction error during one pattern' so a ~20 ms learning lag is needed; the network 'can become unstable, especially with unbounded activation functions'; the steady-state approximation's weight error grows with training samples; best results required lateral I->P weights to remain plastic; 'introducing different learning phases may be unavoidable, at the cost of bio-plausibility'. | [phll/Neuro-Backprop report (Sacramento & Senn model)](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/phll/Neuro-Backprop/master/report/neuro-backprop.tex) | 2020 | moderate |
| Bern successors of the dendritic microcircuit: Latent Equilibrium (Haider et al., NeurIPS 2021; unibe-cns/le_NeurIPS_code) removes the settling problem ('arbitrarily fast computation with arbitrarily slow neurons'), tested on Fourier synthesis, MNIST (10 seeds), HIGGS, CIFAR10 and dendritic-microcircuit 'bars'/'mimic' tasks; PAL (Max et al., arXiv 2212.10249, Nat Mach Intell 2024; unibe-cns/pal_microcircuits_genn) learns the feedback weights inside the microcircuit (YinYang, MNIST vs feedback alignment); GLE (Ellenberger et al., arXiv 2403.16933; unibe-cns/gle-code) extends to time and beats truncated BPTT (windows 4/2/1) on MNIST-1D and Google Speech Commands but needs ~240 GPU-hours per GSC run; SAL (Gierlich et al. 2025, arXiv 2503.02642; unibe-cns/sal-code) solves weight transport via spike timing on CIFAR-10/SVHN/MNIST/FMNIST and cortical microcircuits (~4 h per microcircuit simulation); NLA (Senn et al., eLife 2024; unibe-cns/nla-code) derives real-time error backprop from a least-action principle (MNIST, iEEG 40-100-56 completion). LE and GLE code carry patent-protection notices. | [unibe-cns GitHub organisation READMEs](https://github.com/unibe-cns) | 2021-2025 | moderate |
| Mikulasch, Rudelt & Priesemann (PNAS 2021, 118:e2021925118; Priesemann-Group/dendritic_balance) show that local dendritic E/I balance itself acts as the prediction-error signal enabling efficient-coding learning in spiking networks (MNIST, natural scenes, speech); their 2023 TINS perspective 'Where is the error? Hierarchical predictive coding through dendritic error computation' argues errors are computed in the dendrites of representation neurons rather than in separate error-neuron populations, and their models of visuomotor mismatch (Neural Computation 2023, 'explaining away in causal inference') and 'prediction mismatch responses arise as corrections of a predictive spiking code' reproduce mismatch neurons without dedicated PE cells. | [Priesemann-Group repos dendritic_balance, mismatch_responses, prediction_mismatch; TINS 20](https://github.com/Priesemann-Group/dendritic_balance) | 2021-2023 | moderate |
| Experimental caution on 'PV = divisive, SOM = subtractive': Phillips & Hasenstaub (eLife 2016) found the arithmetic depends on manipulation direction — inactivating SST produced larger multiplicative/gain changes (slope difference p=0.01) and increased information per trial (p=0.022), inactivating PV produced larger additive/subtractive changes (intercept p=1.5e-4) and decreased information (p=1.3e-4), whereas ACTIVATING either class gave indistinguishable mixtures (slope p=0.68, intercept p=0.77, transformation-type proportions p=0.51). Anderson et al. (eLife 2017) found zinc chelation makes PV responses divisive (slope>1, no intercept change) and SOM responses subtractive (intercept shift). Widmer, O'Toole & Keller (eLife 2022) report that CaMKII inhibition in PV cells produced a 'uniform increase in all response types' (global gain) while SST cells carry the visually driven inhibition needed for mismatch responses. | [Phillips & Hasenstaub eLife 2016 (e18383); Anderson et al. eLife (e29893); Widmer et al. e](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.18383) | 2016-2022 | strong |
| Bos, Miehl, Oswald & Doiron (eLife 2024/25, e99808) analyse E-PV-SOM circuits with supralinear transfer f(q)=α q^β (α=1/4, β=2) and show that recurrent connections onto SOM let network gain and stability increase together (example Δg=0.35, Δλ=+0.04) whereas feedforward SOM or PV modulation trades gain against stability (Δg=0.12, Δλ=-0.03); network gain is largest for high E and low PV rates. | [Bos et al., 'Untangling stability and gain modulation in cortical circuits with multiple i](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.99808) | 2025 | moderate |
| The 2026 AI-assisted Allen 'VIP Cortical Interneurons: A Critical Reassessment of the Disinhibition Framework' (v2, ~59k words, 391 cited works, 74.7% of 3,816 inline citations verified) concludes the exclusive VIP->SST->PC disinhibition motif 'is no longer supported': VIP inhibits PV (IPSC τ≈6 ms) and SST (τ≈18 ms) and directly contacts pyramidal somata (calretinin+ bipolar VIP); VIP->SST amplitudes differ ~10x across studies; SST are not uniformly suppressed by locomotion (Pakan 2016, Dipoppa 2018). For models it stresses that 'gain-knob' and 'credit-signal' readings of VIP produce identical within-trial physiology and differ only across trials, and that almost no cited experiment separates the two; it classifies Hertäg 2020/2021, Wilmes 2019 and Hertäg 2023 as credit/uncertainty-signal models whose specific parameterisations remain untested. | [AllenNeuralDynamics/ComputationalReviewVIP (sections 07, 12)](https://github.com/AllenNeuralDynamics/ComputationalReviewVIP) | 2026 | moderate |
| Fly APL is not one global normaliser but many local ones: Amin et al. (eLife 2020) measured an APL space constant of ≈50 µm (activity evoked at the vertical-lobe tip is undetectable by the lobe branch point; activating α'β' KCs activates only the α' lobe of APL, neurites 2-3 µm away silent); hemibrain counts give 52.6±13.4 KC->APL and 49.6±17.9 APL->KC synapses per KC (96,867 KC->APL and 15,656 APL->KC sites on APL's skeleton); distance-weighted connectivity predicts each KC inhibits itself ≈40% more than it inhibits another KC; APL was modelled as four independent regions (vertical tip, stalk, γ1-3, horizontal lobe) matching DAN/MBON compartments; APL receives DAN input differentially across regions. | [Amin, Apostolopoulou, Suárez-Grimalt, Vrontou, Lin, 'Localized inhibition in the Drosophil](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.56954) | 2020 | strong |
| Prisco et al. (eLife 2024, e74172) show APL normalises odour-evoked activity in the calyx: APL synapses with 126/136 PNs (92.6%) and 1,871/1,919 KCs (97.5%), 84±2% of APL->PN synapses sit on PN boutons, PN->APL and APL->PN synapse numbers correlate (r²=0.63); APL responses differ by odour (octanol 45±27% stronger than methylcyclohexanol, 76±30% stronger than δ-decalactone) yet KC claw responses stay homogeneous while PN bouton responses vary; silencing APL removes this normalisation and KC responses then mirror PN input strength; inhibition is locally graded around active PN terminals and differs across γ, α/β, α'/β' KCs. | [Prisco, Deimel, Yeliseyeva, Fiala, Tavosanis, 'The anterior paired lateral neuron normaliz](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.74172) | 2024 | strong |
| Ray, Aldworth & Stopfer (eLife 2020, e53281; ModelDB 262670) built a full-scale locust model: 50,000 KCs, 830 PNs, GGN with 5,283 compartments (~2 mm path length); GGN receives excitation from all KCs and inhibits all KCs 400-500 µm away in the calyx; 10 mV depolarisations at the α lobe attenuate to 5-9 mV in the calyx; feedback raises the KC threshold and confines spiking to brief windows so ~10% of KCs respond per odour with 0-2 spikes across intensities; a second neuron IG inhibits GGN, is inhibited by GGN, and receives odour-driven excitation from an unknown pathway (an inhibitor-of-the-normaliser). | [Ray, Aldworth, Stopfer, 'Feedback inhibition and its control in an insect olfactory circui](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.53281) | 2020 | strong |
| Antennal-lobe local neurons in the hemibrain (Schlegel et al., eLife 2021, e66018): 196 ALLNs in the right hemisphere, 5 lineages, 4 morphological classes (broad >30 glomeruli, patchy, regional, sparse <10 glomeruli), 25 anatomical groups, 74 cell types; ~40% (78) bilateral; about half feed back onto ORN axons (presynaptic inhibition), with lineages v2/v sending only 1.2-5.2% of output to ORNs versus >16% for others; broad lateral-lineage LNs dominate LN-LN connectivity; a centrifugal neuron AL-MBDL1 integrates MB and LH input and targets LN subsets. In one glomerulus (VA1v; Horne et al. eLife 2018, e37550) 56 LNs of six types innervate it (of >150 in the AL), with reciprocal LN pairs averaging 6.7±4.8 synapses and 85% of reciprocal pairs involving an LN. | [Schlegel et al. eLife 2021; Horne et al. eLife 2018](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.66018) | 2021 | strong |
| Olsen, Bhandawat & Wilson (Neuron 2010) established divisive normalisation in the fly AL: PN output follows a normalisation-model form (ORN drive^n divided by a term that grows with total activity across glomeruli), implemented by GABAergic LNs acting largely through GABA_B presynaptic inhibition of ORN terminals, so each glomerulus' gain is scaled by summed AL input (from memory; not re-verified this session). | [Olsen, Bhandawat, Wilson, 'Divisive normalization in olfactory population codes', Neuron 6](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2010.04.009) | 2010 | moderate |
| MB connectome (Li et al., eLife 2020, e62576): ~2,000 KCs per hemisphere, 22 typical + 14 atypical MBON types, ~158 DANs (6 PPL1 + 14-15 PAM types); extensive direct MBON->DAN feedback (median 31 input neurons per DAN at a 5-synapse threshold); APL is GABAergic, innervates the entire MB and is gap-junction coupled to DPM. Fly prediction-error models exist at circuit level: Springer & Nawrot 2021 (iScience; MBON->DAN feedback implements reward prediction and extinction), Jürgensen et al. 2024 (iScience 10.1016/j.isci.2023.108640; PE-driven learning in a spiking larval MB), Bennett/Philippides/Nowotny 2021 (Nat Commun), Zhao et al. 2021 (Sci Rep, Bern 'predictive olfactory learning'). | [Li et al. eLife 2020; nawrotlab repos ExtinctionLearningModelDrosophila and PEcodingDosoph](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.62576) | 2020-2024 | moderate |
| Jordan et al. (arXiv 2104.13238, 2021; unibe-cns/learning-bayes-optimal-dendritic-opinion-pooling) show that conductance-based dendrites implement Bayes-optimal cue combination without explicit division: the somatic potential settles at a conductance-weighted consensus of dendritic 'opinions', so reliability (precision) is encoded as total synaptic conductance and can be learned. | [Jordan, Sacramento, Wybo, Petrovici, Senn, 'Learning Bayes-optimal dendritic opinion pooli](https://github.com/unibe-cns/learning-bayes-optimal-dendritic-opinion-pooling) | 2021 | moderate |
| Wilmes & Clopath 2019 ('Inhibitory microcircuits for top-down plasticity of sensory representations', spiking Brian2 model, ModelDB 259546) and 'Dendrites help mitigate the plasticity-stability dilemma' (Wilmes & Clopath 2023; k47h4/Dendrites) use PV/SST/VIP disinhibitory motifs to gate when top-down errors reach dendrites and drive plasticity; Wilmes' 2025 PV_opto repo models 'Activation of V1 PV interneurons enhances visual discrimination dependent on activation strength, timing and task difficulty' (PV as adjustable gain). | [ModelDBRepository/259546; k47h4/Dendrites; k47h4/PV_opto](https://github.com/ModelDBRepository/259546) | 2019-2025 | moderate |
| Recent bioRxiv items relevant but unverified: Westerberg et al. 2024 (bioRxiv 2024.10.02.616378) argue 'stimulus history, not expectation, drives sensory prediction errors' in cortex; Lee 2024 (bioRxiv 10.1101/2024.10.27.620494) reports that cortical networks with multiple interneuron types generate oscillations during predictive coding; Tsai/Senn et al. 2024 (bioRxiv 2024.09.30.615819; unibe-cns/TopDownOFC) model a hierarchy of prediction errors incl. context-PE with dendritic computation and top-down OFC modulation in a go/no-go reversal task. | [Lcollection/Arxiv_Daily bioRxiv digests; unibe-cns/TopDownOFC](https://github.com/unibe-cns/TopDownOFC) | 2024 | weak |

**Usable ideas**

- Instantiate the precision unit as the Wilmes UPE motif: per error channel, dendrite = rectify(w_s*input - w_SST*r_SST)^k (k=2), soma output = phi(dendrite / (I0 + w*r_PV)); PV unit has quadratic transfer and a local delta rule on the mean-subtracted input so r_PV -> sigma^2; SST unit learns the mean by a delta rule with nudging beta=0.1 (correction factor sqrt((2-beta)/beta)); keep separate rectified + and - channels and let the representation integrate their difference. All rules are local (pre x (post - dendritic prediction)).
- Alternative (Hertäg-Wilmes-Clopath 2025): a slow 'variance neuron' per level integrates (nPE + pPE)^2 with a long time constant (their tau_var ≈ 2000 steps vs tau_E=60) and levels are combined with alpha = precision_lower/(precision_lower + precision_higher). This needs no assumption about which interneuron encodes variance and stacks naturally over a hierarchy; a good default for the transformer-scale version.
- Use Hertäg & Sprekeler's E/I balance equalities as explicit constraints when carving error units out of the Dale RNN: sensory excitation at the 'soma' must be cancelled by a PV-like unit driven by sensory input, prediction excitation at the 'dendrite' cancelled by a SOM-like unit driven by prediction; enforce with rate-homeostatic inhibitory plasticity (target rate rho_0, dendritic target epsilon≈0.1) and classify error units with their thresholds (>=20% in mismatch, <±10% otherwise).
- Make the fly mapping compartment-local, not global: APL (lambda≈50 µm; ~50 KC->APL and ~50 APL->KC synapses per KC; ~40% self-inhibition bias) should be modelled as N independent divisive feedback units aligned with DAN/MBON compartments; AL broad presynaptic-inhibition LNs (~half of 196 ALLNs feed back onto ORN axons) map to input-layer divisive normalisation; sparse/regional LNs to local gain; MB->AL centrifugal neuron (AL-MBDL1) to top-down precision control.
- Add a 'precision-of-precision' controller after GGN/IG: a unit that inhibits the normaliser and is itself inhibited by it plus driven by input; this is how the locust keeps ~10% KC sparseness with 0-2 spikes across intensities and gives a place for volatility (unexpected uncertainty) that the cortical UPE model lacks.
- Implement division biophysically as conductance normalisation (dendritic opinion pooling): output = sum_i g_i E_i / (g_leak + sum_i g_i) with precision = conductance; this is numerically stable (no explicit 1/x), is learnable, and matches shunting inhibition better than an arithmetic denominator.
- Carry the error in an apical compartment (Sacramento): lateral SST-like interneurons learn to predict top-down input so apical potential is zero at baseline (self-predicting state, W_pi = -W_down); then precision gating = divisive PV-like input on the apical->soma coupling. Learn feedback weights with PAL/SAL rather than assuming symmetry; use latent-equilibrium (prospective) dynamics to avoid settling-phase corruption.
- For the Lyapunov-controlled balanced core, route precision increases through a SOM-like recurrent branch (Bos et al. 2025: gain and stability can rise together, Δg=0.35, Δλ=+0.04) rather than through PV alone (gain-stability trade-off).
- Pre-register cell-type-agnostic predictions: variance-unit activity ∝ sigma^2 (and silent when no predictive input exists), error-unit response ∝ |s-mu|^k/sigma^2, contraction bias at high sigma, faster mean learning at low sigma; and measure ACROSS-trial (learning) effects of perturbing the precision unit, since within-trial gain effects cannot distinguish a gain knob from a credit signal.
- Use the nonlinearity exponent k as a tunable OOD detector: k=2-2.5 amplifies large mismatches while retaining uncertainty scaling and compensates for imperfect variance estimates.

**Dead ends**

- Hard-coding 'PV = divisive precision, SOM = subtractive prediction' as a molecular fact: activation experiments give indistinguishable mixed arithmetic for PV and SST (Phillips & Hasenstaub 2016), PV manipulations act as a uniform gain across all response types in V1 (Widmer 2022), and the authors of the UPE model concede 'any subtractive or divisive inhibition could implement' the computation. Treat class identity as a hypothesis to test, not a design axiom.
- Modelling VIP as a pure VIP->SST disinhibitory gate for prediction errors: VIP also inhibits PV (τ≈6 ms) and pyramidal cells directly; SST are not uniformly suppressed by state; models with only the disinhibitory edge 'generally underperform' those with direct edges (Allen 2026 review).
- Single global APL/precision scalar for the mushroom body: contradicted by the ≈50 µm APL space constant, compartment-specific inhibition and locally graded calyx normalisation (Amin 2020; Prisco 2024).
- Co-developing nPE and pPE in one homogeneous Hertäg-Sprekeler network: the balance constraints conflict; needs heterogeneous interneuron inputs (Hertäg & Clopath 2022) or separate rectified channels (Wilmes 2025).
- Exact (non-local) SOM/VIP->PV plasticity: the accurate rule needs the postsynaptic PC population error; the local surrogate only works with sufficient pre/post PC overlap.
- Naive Sacramento-style dendritic microcircuits at scale: settling-phase plasticity corrupts learning (needs ~20 ms learning lag or phases), instability with unbounded activations, weight-transport assumption, plastic lateral weights required; demonstrated only on YinYang/MNIST/CIFAR-10-class tasks; GLE needs ~240 GPU-hours per Speech Commands run; LE/GLE code is patent-encumbered.
- Wilmes UPE model as-is for populations: only single representative rate units, Gaussian scalar stimuli (mu in {1,3,5}, sigma in {0.4,0.8}), requires a 'direct variable code' with matched excitatory/inhibitory weights, covers only expected uncertainty (no volatility), quadratic PV transfer speculative.
- Coverage caveat: WebSearch was unavailable (session budget exhausted) and academic hosts were blocked, so evidence came from GitHub mirrors of eLife manuscripts, code repositories and READMEs; Hertäg & Clopath 2022, Olsen 2010, Mikulasch 2023 and the Sacramento MNIST number are from memory and should be re-verified before citation.

**Open gaps**

- No published model maps variance/precision estimation onto identified fly inhibitory types (APL, MB-C1, AL LN classes, CX Δ7); no experiment tests whether APL or any LN activity scales with stimulus variance rather than with summed input.
- No test of UPE/variance-neuron circuits beyond scalar Gaussian streams: nothing at token-sequence or language-model scale, no high-dimensional 'direct variable code' weight matching, no learned feedback weights across >2 levels.
- Volatility (unexpected uncertainty, learning-rate increase) is unmodelled in these microcircuits (VIP only speculated); the GGN/IG 'inhibitor of the normaliser' motif has no cortical or fly-MB counterpart model.
- Which fly signal plays the pyramidal PE role is unresolved: DAN dopamine (MBON-fed) versus MBON mismatch versus KC dendritic imbalance; DAN->APL and MBON->APL routes as precision-on-learning are unmodelled.
- No experiment (fly or mouse) separates within-trial gain effects from across-trial learning effects of perturbing the putative precision unit — the readout needed to validate precision-as-credit-signal.
- No comparison of the two precision wirings (PV-variance vs PE-derived variance neuron vs conductance opinion pooling) on the same task with stability (Lyapunov) metrics.
- MaleCNS v1.0-specific numbers for APL/KC synapse distributions, LN type counts and MB compartment boundaries were not obtainable here; the numbers above are hemibrain/FAFB-based.
- Confound: stimulus-history adaptation can masquerade as prediction error (Westerberg 2024); no microcircuit model above controls for it.

**Key numbers**

- Wilmes 2025 UPE: k=2 dendritic exponent (2.5 tested), PV transfer x^2, beta=0.1, w_s=sqrt((2-beta)/beta), eta_SST=0.1, eta_PV=0.001, eta_R=0.1, dt=0.1, 1,000-20,000 samples, D=10-100 steps, mu∈{1,3,5}, sigma∈{0.4,0.8}; code: division 1/(1 + wEP*rPV), eta_P=0.001, eta_PS=1e-4
- Hertäg & Sprekeler 2020: 70 PC / 10 PV / 10 SOM / 10 VIP; tau_E=60 ms, tau_I=2 ms; lambda_D=0.27, lambda_E=0.31; Ca-spike threshold 28 s^-1, amplitude 7 s^-1; nPE criterion >=20% mismatch and <±10% elsewhere; 100% nPE after quasi-natural training, ~50% after random-gain; connection probs 0.10-0.60; training stimuli 0-7 s^-1
- Hertäg-Wilmes-Clopath 2025 code: 140 E / 20 PV / 20 SOM / 20 VIP; tau_var=2000, tc_var=5000; PE->prediction weights ±0.003; alpha = (1/v_low)/((1/v_low)+(1/v_high))
- Sacramento microcircuit reimplementation: g_l=0.1, g_B=1.0, g_A=0.28-0.8, tau_w=30, pattern 100 ms, learning lag ~20 ms; YinYang 96.7±0.8% (4-120-3, 45 epochs, 6,000 samples), 97.2±1.1% lateral init; original MNIST ≈98% (memory)
- GLE: ~240 GPU-hours per Google Speech Commands run; SAL microcircuit sim ~4 h; Bos 2025: Δg=0.35 with Δλ=+0.04 (recurrent SOM) vs Δg=0.12 with Δλ=-0.03; transfer f=q^2/4
- Phillips & Hasenstaub 2016: SST-inact gain effect p=0.01, PV-inact offset p=1.5e-4; activation slope p=0.68, intercept p=0.77; info/trial SST-inact +(p=0.022), PV-inact -(p=1.3e-4)
- VIP IPSC decay τ≈6 ms onto PV vs ≈18 ms onto SST; VIP->SST amplitude estimates differ ~10x across studies
- APL: space constant ≈50 µm; 52.6±13.4 KC->APL and 49.6±17.9 APL->KC synapses per KC; 96,867 KC->APL and 15,656 APL->KC sites; self-inhibition ≈40% stronger; contacts 126/136 PNs (92.6%) and 1,871/1,919 KCs (97.5%); 84±2% of APL->PN synapses on boutons; PN->APL vs APL->PN r²=0.63; odour-specific APL response differences 45±27% and 76±30%
- GGN/locust model: 50,000 KCs, 830 PNs, 5,283 GGN compartments, ~2 mm path, 10 mV → 5-9 mV attenuation, ~10% KCs respond with 0-2 spikes, feedback range 400-500 µm
- AL LNs: 196 ALLNs (right hemisphere), 5 lineages, 4 morphological classes, 25 groups, 74 types, ~40% bilateral, ~50% feed back onto ORN axons (v2/v lineages 1.2-5.2% vs >16%); VA1v glomerulus: 56 LNs, 6 types, reciprocal 6.7±4.8 synapses, 85% of reciprocal pairs involve an LN; >11,140 presynaptic sites
- MB: ~2,000 KCs/hemisphere, 22 typical + 14 atypical MBON types, ~158 DANs, median 31 inputs per DAN at 5-synapse threshold
